# StudioAI — operating instructions for Claude Code

**StudioAI is ONE studio for ANY show (Julian's stipulation, 2026-09-01: "this has to be a software for any
project… we create projects, assets within the project, and episodes").** The engine (`engine/`), the
studio UI (`cb-studio/`), the chairs (`studio/chairs/`) and the tools contain no show. Every show is a
PROJECT — `projects/<id>/` — that owns its show bible, canon, laws, chairs' taste, creative memory,
assets, scripts, episodes and every emitted package and take. `projects/crystal-bears/` is the first
project; `projects/the-box-monsters/` the second. The active project is chosen by `STUDIO_PROJECT`,
then the studio's last switch (`cb-studio/data/active-project.json`), then the one profile marked
`"default": true`. Read STUDIO_BIBLE.md (how the studio works) and RESTRUCTURE_SPEC_PROJECTS.md (why it
is shaped this way) before touching structure.

Source of truth order, for a project: `projects/<id>/canon/LOCKED_CANON.md` (what the show is) >
`projects/<id>/SHOW_BIBLE.md` (how that show is made) > STUDIO_BIBLE.md (how the studio works) > this
file. Where they touch, canon wins. For the first project the root `CRYSTAL_BEARS_LOCKED_CANON.md` and
`CRYSTAL_BEARS_STUDIO_BIBLE.md` are compatibility links to those files, until T61.

Every session: read this file, the current ticket pack (TICKET_PACK_002.md) and the current phase of
WORLD_CLASS_ROADMAP.md before writing any code. Work tickets in the pack's order of play, one commit per
ticket, the commit message naming the ticket. A ticket is done only when its Definition of Done is true
AND the Bible agrees — never merely when the code compiles.

The rules below were written while the studio was built around its first show; where one names Crystal
Bears, a bee, a bear or a Crystal Bears file, read it as "the active project" and its own canon/laws —
the dated history is kept as written, per this file's own practice for superseded entries (rule 87).

Hard rules (never work around these):
1. Gates are hard locks. Never advance past an unsigned gate; never sign past an open BLOCK.
2. Taste lives in prose, law lives in code, canon lives in data — and each in ITS OWN HOME (T40–T60, 2026-09-01): a chair's CRAFT (role, responsibility, workflow, influence, the runtime contract) is the studio's, at `studio/chairs/<role>/SKILL.md`, and names no show; a show's TASTE for that chair is the project's, at `projects/<id>/chairs/<role>.md` (only its `RUNTIME_TASTE` block reaches a worker call); law lives in the engine; canon lives in the project's `canon/` (facts) and `laws/` (the show's own rules the engine enforces — cast vocabulary, forbidden elements, continuity rules, emission checks — read only through `engine/project_laws.py`). A cast name, a species, a gag word or a show's file path found in engine/studio/tools code is in the wrong layer and moves the day it is found (`test_t44_profile_authority.py`, `test_t46_cast_vocabulary.py`, `test_second_project_isolation.py` fail on it).
3. No output is ever hand patched. A fault routes through a retake with a layer diagnosis (keyframe / brief / reference / take).
4. Law 5: the voice lives in the render. One combined @Audio1, supplied by the Voice Director and lip-synced by Seedance; no native-voice fallback (a beat with dialogue whose V3 track fails REFUSES to render); no post voice swap. cb_post has no swap function by design — do not add one.
5. Never describe a character's appearance in prompt text. Identity comes only from the reference images. Binding a name to its reference slot in the prompt text ("Fuzzby is the bee from @图2") is allowed and is now the standard (Julian, 2026-07-05, the blessed v4 template — reversing this rule's earlier "names live only in the audio" wording); what stays absolutely forbidden is DESCRIBING the character — no physical/appearance text anywhere, ever. The distinction: a name is a label, not a description.
6. Canon is edited ONLY at the project's own `projects/<id>/canon/LOCKED_CANON.md`, then `python3 tools/sync_canon.py --project <id>` (T54) regenerates the compatibility copies the project's `canon/lock_policy.json` declares. A copy is generated; editing one is drift. `--check` must pass before any sign off. The chairs no longer carry canon copies (T52); a chair reads the active project's canon from the project. Editing a lock-hashed source (the locked canon, characters.json, the style/wing laws, a chair file the lock policy names) marks the project's canon DRIFTED until it is re-locked — the showrunner's explicit action in the studio; a new show LAW goes in `laws/*.json`, which the lock does not hash.
7. When code becomes sharper than the documents, the documents update in the same commit.
8. Restructure work follows RESTRUCTURE_SPEC_T30.md: one phase per commit, baseline byte-identical prompts proven after each phase.
9. A temporary state (e.g. a pollen moustache) resolves WITHIN the take it started in — it never carries across a take boundary (T2 ruling, 2026-07-02, Julian). There is no continuity-tail/previous-clip-tail chaining mechanism; do not re-add one without a fresh ruling.
    RECONCILED 2026-07-08 (contradiction sweep — found reading as flatly contradicted, with no cross-reference either way): the relay chain / carryMarks mechanism (rules 15/21/26/27/31/33/36, Julian's own dated rulings, 2026-07-02 through 2026-07-05) IS the "fresh ruling" this rule itself requires before such a mechanism may exist — it was simply never named as such here. carryMarks explicitly and deliberately carries a declared temporary state (a moustache, leg pollen) across the beat boundary into the next beat's own @图1 reference and is the join-check's own STATE hard-gate (rule 36) — this is the previous-clip-tail chaining this rule's original text says does not exist. The two are reconciled, not in conflict: this rule's ORIGINAL scope — no mechanism carries a temporary state forward by accident or by default — still holds; carryMarks is the named, deliberate, director-authored exception rule 9 itself said would require a fresh ruling, not a silent violation of it.
10. cb_seedance.py's older validation machinery (the 15 minds, physical-action archetypes, authoring/compact validators) is a KEPT, intentional second validation layer underneath the Director's chair (T3 ruling, 2026-07-02, Julian) — not leftover code. It can legitimately refuse a render; that is by design.
11. When a bug is found, the fix is not done until the same pattern has been hunted across every module — not just the one file where it was noticed. State the sweep's result in the commit message: what was checked, what else was found, and what was fixed vs deliberately left for a later ticket (T33 ruling, 2026-07-02, Julian, from the startState/shotSize field-leak find).
12. A chained keyframe's environment comes SOLELY from its chain-reference image — never a competing text re-description of the scene's location alongside it. That competition is what let a chained beat (Ep1 1.B2-1.B4) drift to a visibly different world than the approved plate; `build_keyframe_prompt` fixed and locked 2026-07-02 (Julian, after Scene-1 Gate 2b review — "we need you to make sure that... is now locked in and baked into the software... a standard across the board"). Applies unconditionally, every scene, every episode — do not reintroduce a scene-level text description into a chained beat's prompt without a fresh ruling.
13. SCENE1_DIRECTORS_CUT.md is the studio's taste for comedy staging — its staging laws are baked into cb_director.py's system prompt (`_mind()`, above the Pixar-masters DNA paragraph) and are HARD RULES for every comedy beat: camera-as-character, full-throttle-from-frame-one, one-gag-arc-per-clip (8-10s), simultaneous contrast (never alternating coverage), short cutaway punctuation only, never-acknowledge-failure, escalation ladder, holds-only-on-buttons (max 1, under 1.5s), one earned full stop per scene, sound-as-comic-percussion, a beat never ends on a leaf hit (the flower is always the finisher). Ep1 Scene 1 is 5 beats (1.B1-1.B5), not 4 — the old combined tumble+escalation+thunder beat was split because it violated law 3. Dialogue stays Gate-0-locked regardless of this document's own wording; reconcile and flag diffs, never silently choose (2026-07-02, Julian).
    CORRECTED 2026-07-08 (contradiction sweep): this rule used to say "its eleven staging laws" and treated SCENE1_DIRECTORS_CUT.md as a live repo-root document — both stale. The 2026-07-05 total-reset commit (755d282) moved it to `engine/media/_archive_ep1_full_reset_20260705/director_cut/SCENE1_DIRECTORS_CUT.md` (archived, not deleted; never updated back into CLAUDE.md). The count has grown twice since — law 12 (rule 23, 2026-07-04), law 13 (rule 33, 2026-07-05) — and `cb_director.py`'s own live system prompt now states "THE FOURTEEN STAGING LAWS" (law 14, the Character Vocabulary Law, rule 45, 2026-07-06) as the current, authoritative list; the archived source file itself was never updated past law 13. `cb_director.py` never reads this file from disk — the citation is a hardcoded provenance string in the system prompt, so this staleness is purely descriptive, not a render-time risk. Treat `cb_director.py`'s own "FOURTEEN STAGING LAWS" header as the current count; the archived .md is historical record only.
14. A Gate-1 deliverable change auto-relocks every downstream sign-off (cb_pipeline._relock_if_stale, mirrored in serve.py's locked_state()) — a scene's approved beats and its Gate 2/3/4/5 sign-offs are compared by content fingerprint on every gate-status read, never trusted stale. Verified by engine/test_gate_cascade.py (scratch files only); run it after touching either copy of this logic.
15. FRAME CHAIN doctrine (2026-07-02, Julian): a continuation beat's @图1 chains off the PREVIOUS beat's ENDING FRAME (cb_scene.build_ending_frame — the literal last frame of its rendered clip, composed as a first-class deliverable, media/<ep>_<code>_<slug>_end.png), not its opening frame — the true state after the full take beats a separately re-imagined approximation. Falls back to the opening frame when no ending frame exists yet (an unrendered beat never blocks the chain). Turnarounds (@图2/@图3+) stay in every prompt regardless — the identity anchor against chain drift. A retake upstream (a new ending frame) auto-dirties every downstream beat's keyframe+clip lock (cb_pipeline._relock_chain_if_dirty, same lazy fingerprint pattern as rule 14) — verified by the same engine/test_gate_cascade.py.
    ⚠ PENDING CORRECTION — RESOLVED (was: queued, not yet applied — 2026-07-03, Julian, change freeze holds; resolved 2026-07-03 by rule 21, found still reading as open in the 2026-07-08 contradiction sweep): vendor guidance confirmed an ending frame is a render-time FIRST-FRAME CARRY (something Seedance itself consumes to seed a render's continuity), never a generation ANCHOR for a separate keyframe-composition step. Rule 21 (THE RELAY CHAIN, same day) applied this correction in full — not by reverting Gate 2b's use of the ending frame above, but by removing the anchor step entirely for relay beats (a beat now opens off the previous beat's harvested settle frame directly, no separate keyframe-image-generation step at all). `cb_scene.py`'s `harvest_settle_frame`/`relay_source_for` are the live implementation. The freeze this note describes no longer holds; this paragraph is kept as the dated record of the correction, not a description of a still-open item.
16. ACTION-STATE FIDELITY (2026-07-03, Julian — "if the first scene is them flying through the meadow, then they need to be flying through the meadow"): whenever a beat's own action text names an active locomotion verb, the KEYFRAME must show that state concretely, not just gesture at it — checked by cb_qa's new ACTION_STATE_MISMATCH (KEYFRAME_DONE.md) and generated by cb_prompts.build_keyframe_prompt's WINGS/FLIGHT-ENERGY law (asymmetric mid-downstroke wings, forward-leaning body, legs tucked — never both wings spread symmetrically with the body hanging vertical like a puppet at rest, which reads as floating in place and forces Seedance to invent a standstill-to-motion transition instead of continuing existing motion). Generation and QA ask for/verify the SAME concrete criteria, not a vague "does this look dynamic" taste call — a generic ask is what let the first real keyframes pass QA while still reading static. Deliberately excludes "hover" — the default bee state, not inherently wrong.
17. VISION-QA CHECKS MUST STATE CONCRETE VISUAL CRITERIA, NEVER SUBJECTIVE QUESTIONS (Julian's Ruling, 2026-07-03). Worked example: ACTION_STATE_MISMATCH's first draft asked the vision model a vague question — "does this pose read static, posed, standing still, or passively floating?" — and the model PASSED both of the known-bad 1.B1/1.B2 keyframes clean; it is a subjective taste call a generous grader waves through. Rewritten to ask two concrete, checkable things instead — are the wings SYMMETRICAL (fail) or ASYMMETRIC/mid-downstroke (pass)? Is the body VERTICAL with legs dangling (fail) or LEANING FORWARD into the direction of travel (pass)? — and it correctly failed both. Every vision-QA check added from here must name the specific, literally-checkable visual feature(s) that separate a pass from a fail (an angle, a count, a symmetry, a position) — never phrase the ask as an open judgment call ("does this look right/dynamic/off").
18. A NEW OR TIGHTENED QA CHECK APPLIES FORWARD ONLY, NEVER RETROACTIVELY, UNLESS JULIAN RULES OTHERWISE (his Ruling, 2026-07-03, re: ACTION_STATE_MISMATCH vs the already-signed 1.B1/1.B2). Work he has already signed off stands; a check catching something that sign-off missed is logged as calibration data (engine/QA_CALIBRATION_LOG.md) for tuning the check, never used to trigger an unrequested regeneration of signed work.
19. THE JOIN CONTRACT (JOIN_CONTRACT.md, Julian, 2026-07-03 — "the last frame needs to be the start of the next frame... we need to get it to flow across"): a handoff/edit-layer doctrine, deliberately separate from prompt-building. Every beat's final 0.5-1s is a SETTLE (character at rest in the pose that IS the next beat's startState; camera arrived at its stated end state; screen positions held at their frame-left/frame-right home); every beat's shot 1 opens FROM that settle. `cb_qa.check_join` compares beat N's composed ending frame (rule 15) to beat N+1's actual opening frame for POSITION, STATE and LIGHT — concrete criteria per rule 17, report-only. `cb_post.assemble_conformed` is Gate 4's rough-cut assembler: trims a fixed settle allowance off each clip's tail/head before hard-cutting (still no in-scene cross-dissolves) so a cut lands mid-motion instead of hold-into-hold; `assemble_picture` (the raw butt-join) stays, unchanged, as the deliberate comparison baseline. Diagnosing a join is not license to touch prompt code or re-render — a join found broken only names what's wrong; the fix (if any) is a separate, later ruling.
    CORRECTED 2026-07-08 (contradiction sweep): this line's present-tense claim that assemble_conformed "is Gate 4's rough-cut assembler" is stale — rule 46 (2026-07-07) found and recorded that cb_pipeline.gate4()/gate5() never call it; cb_post.run() calls assemble_picture (the raw butt-join) exclusively, and assemble_conformed has zero live callers anywhere in the codebase as of this writing. Whether to actually wire assemble_conformed into gate5()/cb_post.run() is a separate, undecided functional change — not made here — see rule 46's own explicit deferral. The identical stale claim also appears in PRODUCTION_DOCTRINE.md's own stage map; same correction applies there.
20. THE HANDLE DOCTRINE (Julian, 2026-07-03 — "every shot shoots long so the cutter has meat to trim into"): every beat now renders at 15s (`cb_segprompt.HANDLE_TOTAL`), superseding the old per-beat 8-15s `durationSec` range. 13s (`HANDLE_ACTION`) is the story-action budget, split across the beat's own shots by weight exactly as before; the final 2s (`HANDLE_SETTLE`) are appended to the closing shot as a DIRECTED LIVING SETTLE — never dead air, per the dead-time-causes-hallucinations rule — built from the beat's own `endState` field (`cb_segprompt._v3_settle`; a universal idle-life guarantee is always appended even before every beat has an authored `endState`). The settle window serves two jobs at once: the relay's harvest window (rule 21 — the sharpest frame ANYWHERE in those 2s, not a prayer that the literal last frame is clean) and Gate 4's trim handle (rule 19 — the editor cuts into the settle per join). Both emitters carry it; goldens re-blessed same day.
21. THE RELAY CHAIN (Julian, 2026-07-03): a beat's OWN Gate-2b keyframe is generated only when it has no signed predecessor to open from. A continuation beat instead opens directly off the previous beat's signed clip's HARVESTED SETTLE FRAME (`cb_scene.harvest_settle_frame` — the sharpest frame anywhere in its 2s settle window, scored by Laplacian variance, never just the literal last frame), RE-MINTED per rule 22 before it anchors anything, fed into Seedance's ref2vid as `@图1` — no separate keyframe-image-generation step for that beat at all. This finally applies the FRAME CHAIN doctrine's pending correction (rule 15's note, LAB_BACKLOG.md's "Chain doctrine note"): an ending frame is a render-time first-frame carry, never a generation anchor — resolved by removing the anchor step entirely for relay beats rather than reverting to opening-frame chaining. A 4th reference (the scene's plate) anchors the world's canonical look/palette without forcing the frame, alongside `@图1` and the character turnarounds. Applies to a beat only once its predecessor has an actual SIGNED clip — an unsigned/undecided predecessor blocks the relay at that beat, it does not fall back to guessing.
22. THE RE-MINT STEP (Julian's ruling, 2026-07-03 — "the relay gains the RE-MINT step as standard... re-mint is now every link, not QA triggered"): supersedes the earlier same-day lab rejection of a "NB2 chain refresh" (LAB_BACKLOG.md) — the director overrides the lab, explicitly and on the record. Every harvested settle frame is re-minted through NB2 with a LOCKED, deliberately minimal prompt (`cb_prompts.build_remint_prompt`) before it may anchor the next beat: turnarounds attached solely to hold identity, artifacts and motion blur removed, nothing else changes — a restoration pass, never a re-generation. `cb_qa.check_remint` is a hard BLOCK comparing the re-mint against the harvest (position/state) and the turnarounds (identity). Julian approves the cleaned anchor before it launches anything: `cb_beats.fire_next_beat` prepares (designate → harvest → re-mint → drift-check) and STOPS, returning only once called again with `approved=True` — no auto-advance past an unapproved anchor, ever. `cb_scene.relay_source_for` prefers an already-produced re-mint over the raw harvest, falling back to the raw harvest only if a relay beat is fired outside the `fire_next_beat` ceremony (graceful degradation, not a silent skip of the step when it's actually available).
23. FUZZBY'S MOTION DOCTRINE, EXTENDED (Julian's ruling, 2026-07-04 — "when he's moving, he's never moving slowly... always too fast, always too bumpy... always too high, always too low... never does anything slowly, never hovers"): the existing always-on "Fuzzby is always fast, manic, hyper, clumsy in flight" rule (`skills/crystal-bears-director/SKILL.md` §0.1, `characters.json`'s Fuzzby.bible.motionRule/dos/donts) gains an explicit ALTITUDE clause — he is exactly as erratic vertically as he already was horizontally, constantly overshooting too high and undershooting too low, porpoising, never holding a level or controlled line. Separately, a new OPTIONAL technique (Julian — "sometimes, when he has the big crashes, it would be good to put some slow motion in there... then speed up again"): on Fuzzby's single BIGGEST hit in a beat (the BUMBLE/BONK of his three-beat comedy engine) the moment MAY stretch into exaggerated cartoon slow motion before snapping back to full speed — reserved for the biggest hit only, never every bump. This is a TEMPO STRETCH, not a hold — it does not compete with staging law 8 (holds only on buttons, max one, under 1.5s) because motion never stops, only stretches; wings keep beating fast per the WING LAW throughout. Filed as new staging law 12 in `SCENE1_DIRECTORS_CUT.md` and mirrored in `cb_director.py`'s system prompt (now "THE TWELVE STAGING LAWS").
24. THE FOUR-ANCHOR GROUNDING SET (Julian, 2026-07-04 — "point anchor, duration anchor, identity anchor, performance track. That's the full grounding set — nothing left for the model to invent."): every relay beat's reference stack names FOUR anchors, each doing exactly one job, stated as such in the prompt text itself so the split is never left implicit. POINT anchor (@图1, the harvested/re-minted settle frame): this instant only — "this image IS the first frame... nothing is composed fresh" — now also carries a CONTENT description mechanically derived from the previous beat's own authored `endState` field (`cb_segprompt._v3_prev_frame_content`), so the model is told what @图1 shows, not just its job. DURATION anchor (the scene plate, always the last @图N): the environmental constant for the WHOLE clip — lighting direction, palette, textures — "the world must not drift from this plate," explicitly never a framing/pose reference. IDENTITY anchor (the character turnarounds, @图2/@图3...): proportions, colours, features, held every beat. PERFORMANCE track (@Audio1): mouth, timing AND full physical performance, camera locked whenever any character speaks. All four are mechanical — derived from existing beat/scene data, nothing invented — and generalize forward automatically: once a beat is relayed, its own `endState` becomes the NEXT beat's point-anchor content, with no beat-specific code (verified live for 1.B3 ahead of its own re-fire). Swept the same day: `cb_seedance.get_seedance_prompt` was found missing `relay=` entirely, meaning the studio's own preview/export path showed a DIFFERENT, weaker prompt than what `cb_beats.run` actually fired for any relay beat — fixed to match.
25. THE LOCKED RELAY-BEAT TEMPLATE (Julian, 2026-07-04 — "This is the locked relay-beat template — canonical for every beat after a scene's opening anchor, all scenes, from now."): the JSON emitter's (`emit_json_v3`) shape is now the SCHEMA every relay beat's shipped prompt follows, every scene, every episode. Fields and their jobs:
    - `references`: the four-anchor grounding set (rule 24) — one entry per @图N, size-only identity handles (below).
    - `voices`: the PERFORMANCE track law — @Audio1 only, full physical performance, camera locked while any character speaks (rule 24).
    - `world`: the actual environment/atmosphere prose, each element stated EXACTLY ONCE — never repeating what `references` already declared for @图1/the plate (the duplication-with-a-broken-join bug found and fixed 2026-07-04: `_v3_environment`'s `include_refs=False` mode for this field).
    - `tone`: the beat's emotional register, mechanically mapped from `comedyMode` (`_v3_tone`).
    - `ambience`: THE LOCKED AMBIENT BED — word-for-word identical every clip in the scene, read straight from the scene's own `ambientBed` field (`_v3_ambience`), never varying beat to beat; each beat's own `soundIntent` supplies foreground SFX layered ON TOP, per shot — the bed is the constant, the foreground is what changes.
    - `rule`: the written invariant for the whole beat (wing law, camera-lock-during-dialogue, hold/timing-lock) — never per-shot, always whole-beat.
    - `shots`: the per-shot camera/action/dialogue breakdown. Action text keeps its own established "full descriptive label on first mention, short label after" cadence (`_delabel`) — that stays exactly as is; it is PERFORMANCE DIRECTION, a different job from the binding handle below, and Julian confirmed it explicitly: "leave the action-prose flavour language as-is."
    - `constraints`: one positive-framed identity-consistency line.
    - `negatives`: SIX standing items, always exactly six. FOUR are universal, every scene, every cast: no morphing/redesign/rescale of the characters; no extra characters or props; no on-screen text/subtitles/logos/watermarks; no foreign-language speech. The remaining TWO are species-appropriate and swap by cast (`_v3_negatives`'s `any_bee` branch) — for a bee scene: no crystals on/attached to the bees, no still/frozen wings while airborne; for a non-bee cast: no flicker/compression artifacts, no continuity break in lighting/palette. A future scene's cast decides which pair applies — never invent a seventh.
    HISTORICAL NOTE (added 2026-07-08, contradiction sweep): this describes `emit_json_v3`'s negatives shape as it stood the day this rule was written — accurate then, not now. `_v3_negatives` (the species-conditional six-item function this paragraph names) was deleted outright in THE DEFINITIVE BUILD purge (rule 40); the live mechanism (`cb_segprompt._standing_negatives()`) has been an unconditional ELEVEN-item list, no species branch, since rules 43/44 (2026-07-06). Left as dated historical record rather than rewritten, matching this file's own established practice (rule 52) for entries describing a past, since-superseded state — see rule 28 Layer 1 Law 10 for the current count.
    Binding handles (the SUBJECTS line and each character's `references` entry) are SIZE-ONLY ("the larger bee") — personality adjectives ("eager, manic") were trimmed out of the identity-binding declaration specifically (Julian: "trim the personality adjectives from the binding handles — size handles only"), since that label's only job is binding an @图N slot to a character, not directing performance.
    PLATE DECISION (Scene 1, 2026-07-04): Scene 1's plate (`Ep1_S1_plate.png`) was found to visually show faceted, cut-gemstone-shaped crystals, violating the Crystal World Rule's "natural, organic, never cut or arranged" standard — but it stays AS IS; continuity with 1.B1's already-approved clip outweighs it. Going forward, every future scene's plate is checked for this at the earliest possible point: `cb_qa.check_plate` (fires automatically right after `cb_scene.build_plate`, both on first build and on any retake/regen — confirmed the only two plate-creation call sites) now asks a concrete, rule-17-compliant question about crystal SHAPE (faceted/geometric/symmetrical = FAIL; rough/irregular/geode-like = PASS) — never crystal PRESENCE, which stays required brand identity on every plate. Report-only, matching the existing PASS/FLAG pattern every other plate/keyframe QA check in this pipeline already uses — Julian's own Scene-1 call is proof a FLAG doesn't have to hard-block, it's a signal for his judgment.
    Also fixed the same day, found while syncing this template's crystal-canon wording at the source: `config/locations.json` (the SSOT-cache mirror of each scene's descriptive fields) was carrying the same stale "cut crystals" text as the beat package — re-synced, including its `_sourceHash`, so `scene_cache_stale`'s freshness check doesn't itself block on the fix.
26. THE ANTI-HOLD FIX + THE FIFTH ANCHOR (Julian, 2026-07-04, watching 1.B2's actual rendered seeds — "it's looping the first frame as the end frame... it finishes back to the start"): confirmed by extracting and inspecting both seeds' last frames directly — Fuzzby was still in his opening superhero pose (arm raised, one leg lifted) the whole 15s, moustache pasted on top, Zenny barely moved. Root cause: rule 24's own @图1 language ("this image IS the first frame... nothing is composed fresh") was so emphatic the model read it as a pose to MAINTAIN, not a one-frame launching point — an over-correction from the earlier opening-frame fix. Fixed in two places (the @图1 reference declaration and the per-shot OPENING FRAME LOCK text): added an explicit counter-instruction immediately alongside each — "moves DECISIVELY AWAY from this pose for the rest of the shot... never a pose to hold, repeat or return to — by the shot's end the characters are in a completely different position and pose." Also rewrote 1.B2's own authored cut 1 (`cuts[0].action`/`framing`) for the erratic-altitude/chaotic doctrine (rule 23) and the staging Julian described — Fuzzby flies a chaotic loop first, Zenny established as already at her own flower (not appearing suddenly), then he swings back for the dive — through the beat's own data, never a one-off prompt edit.
    THE FIFTH ANCHOR — @Video1 (Julian, 2026-07-04, after installing and mining the `seedance-20` community skill package at `~/.claude/skills/seedance-20/`): fal's own field guidance (`api-status.md`) states the preferred way to continue a clip on this surface is "reference-to-video with the previous clip as a **video** reference — keeps motion and audio context; chaining image-to-video from the previous clip's last frame is **the fallback**." We were doing the fallback. Now ADDITIVE to the still-frame re-mint anchor (never a replacement — Julian: "we still need to do the re-mint, but put the video URL in there as well"): `cb_gen.generate_video_seedance_ref` gains a `video_urls` parameter; `cb_beats.run` (and `fire_next_beat`'s dry-run preview) uploads the previous beat's own signed clip alongside the usual 4 images whenever relay=True — guaranteed to exist by that point, since `relay_source_for`'s "relay" status is itself gated on a successful harvest from that same clip. Declared explicitly in the prompt as `@Video1`, alongside @图1, in both emitters: real motion/audio context, reference only, never to be re-rendered or repeated. The mining pass that surfaced this (FLF2V support, `continuation-handoff.md`, `continuity-qc.md`, `retake-protocol.md`, `multishot-grammar.md` compared against this doctrine) is filed in the session record, not reproduced here — the two adopted findings so far are this one and the still-open `extension_depth`/scheduled-re-anchor ceiling (Scene 1 is already 5 beats deep with no such ceiling, unresolved).
27. THE TEMPORAL-CONTRADICTION BUG — `endStateStill`, a field separate from `endState` (Julian, 2026-07-04, diagnosing the very next fire under rule 26's fix, which STILL failed the join check the same way): three errors in one field, all traced to `_v3_prev_frame_content` injecting the previous beat's `endState` VERBATIM as @图1's content description. `endState` is DIRECTING PROSE for that beat's own SETTLE block — full of temporal verbs and imperatives ("straightens into... and holds it", "End on his pose... both at rest, camera locked, as the meadow's hum resumes"). Pasted into @图1's description for the NEXT beat, Seedance has no way to know those verbs describe yesterday: "End on his pose, both at rest" reads as THIS clip's own ending instruction, directly contradicting rule 26's own anti-hold clause ("by the shot's end the characters are in a completely different position") — two opposing ending instructions in one prompt, and the render satisfied the wrong one, holding the anchor pose for the whole clip exactly as before. It also near-duplicated shot 4's own SETTLE wording, and restated the `ambience` field a second time, wrongly bound to the opening reference.
    THE FIX: a new field, `endStateStill`, authored PARALLEL to `endState` (same beats, same authoring moment — see rule 55's original endState backfill for the precedent) — a STATIC PICTURE DESCRIPTION of the same moment, every temporal verb, imperative, camera instruction and ambience restatement already stripped at authoring time, describing only subjects, poses, positions, expressions and setting, the way a photograph would. `_v3_prev_frame_content` now reads ONLY `endStateStill`, never `endState` — and returns empty (no content clause at all) when a predecessor has no `endStateStill` yet, rather than falling back to the raw `endState`, since that fallback IS the bug. Not implemented as an automatic per-render transform of `endState` (rejected: Julian's own worked example rewrites verb phrases into adjectival ones and adds static detail — "holds it one blink too long" -> "frozen mid-hover", "her rapid wings" -> "wings a rapid blur" — genuine rewriting a regex strip could not reproduce reliably or safely for future beats' differently-phrased prose). Populated for 1.B1-1.B4 (1.B5 has no `endState` yet either). Confirmed via the golden set: zero diffs, since this only touches the relay=True content-description clause, still the harness's one known untested path (`LAB_BACKLOG.md`).
28. THE PRODUCTION DOCTRINE — THE WHOLE MACHINE, ONE PAGE (Julian, 2026-07-04 — "once all twelve are code-enforced, every prompt for the next ten scenes is built by the same hands, and the only thing you ever author again is Layer 2, which is the job you actually wanted: directing"; CONSOLIDATED 2026-07-05, lock-in night, absorbing rules 29-36 into a single standalone, current statement). Rules 15/19-27 are the case-by-case history of how the original constitution was arrived at; rules 29-36 are the case-by-case history of the junction-type pivot and everything it triggered the same night. This rule absorbs all of it — the numbered rules stay in place as the dated record of WHY each change happened; this rule is WHAT IS TRUE NOW, the one to check against going forward. The same doctrine also lives at repo root as `PRODUCTION_DOCTRINE.md` (the one document a new session reads first) and in `REPLICATOR.md`'s own gate map — all three describe the same machine; if they ever disagree, fix the disagreement the day it's found (rule 7).
    CORRECTED 2026-07-08 (contradiction sweep): "all three" is stale — `REPLICATOR.md` was retired into `PRODUCTION_DOCTRINE.md` and deleted (`git rm`) by rule 40, the night after this rule was written; `PRODUCTION_DOCTRINE.md` is now the sole stage-map document. The two sibling `REPLICATOR.md` mentions later in this same rule (the PRODUCTION LINE passage and the Scene Bubble Law citation) are dated history from before that retirement and are left as the accurate-when-written record, per this file's own established practice, rather than rewritten.

    THE FIDELITY LAW (Julian's ruling, 2026-07-06, CLEAN RESTART ON v5 — written in as the governing principle above every other law this rule states, not an addition alongside them): nothing is invented that canon already defines. Everything on screen is pulled from the bible — in the bible's own words — or it does not render.
    - **Character contract**: every character's acting DNA is extracted VERBATIM from their bible entry — movement, rhythm, comedy register, contrast pairings — locked in the character data, quoted into every prompt they appear in. No paraphrase, no softening, no session ever rewrites it. The bible is the only editor. Mechanically: `cb_segprompt._v5_acting_dna_source` quotes `actingNote` (Fuzzby/Zenny — pure movement/comedy, appearance-free by construction) or falls back to `bible.mannerisms` (the 9 bears, who have no actingNote) — the fallback is a FLAGGED gap, not a silent acceptance: several bears' mannerisms text mixes in appearance/wardrobe detail that would violate rule 5 if quoted verbatim into a shipped prompt, and only Julian's own edit of the bible entry can close that gap, never a Claude Code edit at prompt-build time. `bible.dos[0]`/`bible.donts[0]` (the Always/Never lists) feed the beat's own staging clause and negative line, verbatim, index-0 deterministic — never curated for "relevance," since choosing which existing sentence is most relevant is itself a form of authoring.
    - **Scene contract**: every scene's identity — location, light, atmosphere, register — is pulled from the script and scene canon, locked as the bubble's three constants (rule 35). A scene renders as itself.
    - **Style contract**: the look-and-feel is the locked style law plus the plate, verbatim, every prompt, every beat, every scene, forever (`cb_segprompt._v5_style`, unconditional).
    - **Enforcement**: every one of these lives as a locked data field with its bible source cited (`cb_segprompt._v5_acting_dna_source` returns the field it drew from for exactly this reason). `cb_preflight.check_characters_technical` blocks any character appearing in a beat whose store has neither `actingNote` nor `bible.mannerisms`, and neither `bible.dos` nor `bible.donts`, populated — a prompt whose character content can't trace to a locked field never compiles. Drift becomes a build error, not a mood.
    - **THE ACTINGDNA RETRACTION** (same ruling, same day): an earlier v5 draft added a NEW `characters.json` field, `actingDNA`, hand-authored per character (partly from Julian's own capsule phrases, partly synthesized from bible prose). Julian's correction was explicit and immediate — "STOP any actingDNA authoring or extraction — the content already exists" — because that field was authored prose, not a verbatim quotation, and so violated this very law the moment it was written. RETRACTED the same day: all 11 entries removed from `characters.json`, restoring the file to its pre-session state; `cb_preflight`'s per-character check now requires `actingNote`/`mannerisms` + `dos`/`donts` instead. `actingRule` (a pre-existing, similarly-condensed one-line field, rule 33) is likewise retired from every check and from the emitter — v5 reads neither `actingRule` nor `actingDNA` anywhere; the character's own real store is the only source.
    - **THE WORD-BUDGET CONSEQUENCE, FLAGGED**: pulling `actingNote`/`mannerisms` verbatim plus `dos[0]`/`donts[0]` per cast member is real, richer, more-cited content than the retracted `actingDNA` field — and it costs more words. On Scene 1 (Fuzzby + Zenny, both speaking in most beats), this pushed 4 of the 5 beats over the 400-word hard cap (402-427 words); only 1.B1 (the scene's shortest cast footprint) stays clean at 394. This is not a bug to quietly work around — it is the real, honest cost of two full characters' verbatim-cited acting register appearing in every independently-generated beat of a two-character scene, and it is reported to Julian exactly as measured, not trimmed to fit.

    THE HIERARCHY: Episode → Scene → Beat.

    THE SCENE BUBBLE (rule 35): a scene's identity is its THREE LOCKED CONSTANTS — the scene plate, the ambient bed, the style law — held VERBATIM across every beat inside it. A scene boundary is a FULL, DELIBERATE RESET: new plate, new ambient bed, a fresh canon-generated anchor keyframe for the scene's own opening beat, relay depth back to zero, no visual continuity owed across the boundary. Every law below applies identically inside every bubble.

    THE BEAT: one gag arc, 15 seconds hard-locked — 13s action + 2s settle (the Handle Doctrine, rule 20). Never two arcs in one take.

    LAYER 1 — THE INVARIANT SKELETON (machine-filled, identical logic every prompt; a law here can be forgotten only if it's convention rather than code — see the audit this rule commissioned, PROMPT_LAWS_AUDIT.md):
    1. One beat, one prompt, one gag arc. 15s = 13s action + 2s settle. Never two arcs in one take.
    2. Opener vs relay vs junction type: a scene's first beat opens from its generated canon anchor keyframe. Every beat after declares its OWN junction type (a beat-level field, `junctionType`, rule 31) — `intentional_next_shot` (THE DEFAULT: beats join by CUT, not freeze — a new gag arc, a fresh camera setup, energy already in frame) or `seamless_continuation` (ONLY where the director's own cut explicitly continues one shot across the boundary) — and opens from the previous beat's harvested settle frame either way. Omission is NEVER read as seamless — that stays the rarer, explicitly-declared case.
    3. The reference stack, every relay prompt, each entry one declared job: @图1 — for a `seamless_continuation` beat, the locked opening frame (point anchor); for an `intentional_next_shot` beat (the default), a STATE REFERENCE only — identity, declared carry marks, lighting and world-position carry forward exactly; camera, pose, energy and motion do not (rules 31/33/34). @Video1 — motion energy and action continuity ONLY; never camera framing, shot size or composition, which comes from THIS beat's own direction (the corrected line, FIXED 2026-07-05 lock-in night — the earlier wording, "previous visual motion continuity and energy," still left framing implicitly copyable). CORRECTED 2026-07-08 (contradiction sweep): @Video1 is described here as a live standing anchor — stale since rule 51 (2026-07-07), THE FIFTH ANCHOR RETIRED, removed it entirely from the shipped prompt (`cb_segprompt._v5_references` emits no @Video1 line, opener or relay); the reference stack is now @图1, character turnarounds, the scene plate, and @Audio1 only. Character turnarounds — identity anchor, one per character, held every beat. Scene plate — duration anchor, the environmental constant for the whole clip (lighting, palette, texture), never a framing or pose reference. @Audio1 — the sole vocal source, covering lines, hums and sing-song rhythm alike; performance anchor: timing, mouth rhythm and full physical performance, camera locked whenever any character speaks.
    4. The @图1 description is a photograph for a `seamless_continuation` beat (content, not choreography — no temporal verbs, no imperatives, no "end on") or the fixed state-carry template for an `intentional_next_shot` beat (Law 3) — never a mix of the two, and never the predecessor's full endState sentence stuffed in wholesale (rule 33's rewrite of `_v4_state_carry`). CORRECTED 2026-07-08 (contradiction sweep): `_v4_state_carry` was deleted in the v4→v5 purge (rule 42); the live mechanism building @图1's description is `cb_segprompt._v5_references` (rules 51/52's anti-hold-safe relay wording). This line just never caught up when v5 shipped.
    5. Anti-hold — `seamless_continuation` ONLY: frame one IS @图1 exactly, then motion moves decisively away, immediately; never held, repeated or returned to (rule 26). An `intentional_next_shot` beat (the default, rule 31) never opens on @图1's frame at all — it opens on its own fresh camera setup, carrying only @图1's declared state forward; the model is never asked to hold or repeat a pose that isn't this beat's own.
    6. Audio law: all vocal sound — lines, hums, exclamations, sing-song — is the V3 performance fired in as @Audio1. Seedance generates no voice-like sound of its own. The prompt never contains the spoken words — dialogue binds speaker, timing and expression only, by name and ordinal.
    7. Ambience is scene-property: one locked ambient-bed line, word-for-word identical across every prompt in the scene (the Scene Bubble Law). Per-beat SFX layers on top; ambience never appears inside any other field.
    8. Camera law: locked during any SPOKEN line (rule 38, 2026-07-06 — a continuous hum or sing-song is motion-exempt); motion only in non-speaking action; species-scaled (mechanical, from the cast — "bee-scale") and naming the SPECIFIC eyeline/action characters when the beat has declared an `opensOn` pair (rule 33), falling back to generic phrasing otherwise.
    9. The living settle: every clip ends on ITS OWN completed beat, in character — read from the beat's own authored `endState` VERBATIM (rule 33; never the old generic idle-life placeholder once `endState` is authored), so a manic character stays visibly manic at rest and a deadpan character gets its own full pause. NEVER a restatement of the previous beat's pose — the settle is this beat's OWN distinct moment (advisory-checked by `check_settle_distinctiveness`); that settle is the next beat's harvest.
    10. The standing negatives, every prompt, in full — Crystal World Rule and Wing Law always — plus the beat's own `stagingProhibited` items additive on top (rule 30), never fewer than the standing count. CORRECTED 2026-07-08 (contradiction sweep): this used to say "six," describing v3/v4-era `_v3_negatives` (species-conditional, deleted in THE DEFINITIVE BUILD purge, rule 40) — the live mechanism (`cb_segprompt._standing_negatives()`) has been an unconditional ELEVEN-item list since rules 43/44 (2026-07-06); this line just never caught up, the same staleness already found and fixed in MANIFEST.md the same day.
    11. Character law: identity by reference only, name-welded directly to its @图N slot ("Fuzzby is the bee from @图2," rule 5/30) — a name is a label, never a description; no physical/appearance text anywhere, ever.
    12. Style law and world text: verbatim from the show profile and scene data, never retyped, never paraphrased.

    LAYER 2 — THE CREATIVE SLOTS (director-authored; the ONLY things that change beat to beat):
    1. The beat's own junction type (Law 2) — defaults to cut; declare `seamless_continuation` only when the shot genuinely doesn't travel.
    2. Action prose: the beat's own gag, ERRATIC IN CHARACTER, PRECISE IN CHOREOGRAPHY (rule 33) — every manic action a SPECIFIC NAMED GAG with cause and consequence (rockets, brakes too late, loops once, stops); adjective-chaos ("wildly," "crazily," a generic frenzy word with no physical beat behind it) is BANNED as unreadable — the model executes actions and butchers adjectives.
    3. `opensOn: {who, action}` — WHO the camera opens on and their immediate mid-motion state; the Coverage Law's reaction-bridge mechanism, rendered as a fixed "OPEN ON {NAME} mid-motion: {action}." sentence — a foil character's look or turn (Zenny's head-turn) is the house bridge whenever the cut travels.
    4. `carryMarks` — a SHORT phrase (never a full sentence) naming what specifically, visibly persists (a moustache, leg pollen) — the ONLY thing the join-check's STATE tier hard-gates on (rule 36), and what the NEXT beat's own @图1 clause quotes.
    5. `endState` — directing prose for THIS beat's own ending, written as its own distinct moment in character, never a restatement of the predecessor's pose. `endStateStill` — the same moment as a static photograph, hand-authored in parallel (rule 27), never auto-derived from `endState`.
    6. `actingRule` (per character, a new one-line `characters.json` bible field) + `actingContrast` (per beat, which characters play off each other and how) — feed the `acting_rules` field. CORRECTED 2026-07-08 (contradiction sweep): this item is stale, contradicted by THE ACTINGDNA RETRACTION earlier in this same rule (actingRule/acting_rules retired from every check and the emitter, 2026-07-06) — no `acting_rules` field exists anywhere in the shipped v5 prompt. `actingContrast` alone survives as a real, live per-beat field (schema + emitter + cb_preflight, rules 47/53); per-character acting register now comes solely from `actingNote`/`bible.mannerisms` (`cb_segprompt._v5_acting_dna_source`), never `actingRule`. Left as dated historical record of Layer 2's original (v4-era) shape, matching this file's own established practice for superseded entries.
    7. Dialogue bindings (who speaks, expression direction), tone, per-beat foreground SFX, `stagingProhibited` (beat-specific failure modes, merged with the standing negatives — eleven as of rules 43/44, see Law 10's 2026-07-08 correction above).

    THE COVERAGE LAW (rule 34): a scene is ONE CONTINUOUS SPATIAL BUBBLE. Every beat opens on a NEW ANGLE WITHIN the same space, close to where the previous beat ended, motivated by eyeline or motion (Layer 2.3's `opensOn`) — never a relocation, never a fresh establishing wide mid-scene (the wide belongs to the scene's opening beat only). The join-check's STATE tier gains COVERAGE as a fourth, always-on criterion alongside STATE/LIGHT/GEOGRAPHY: does the opening read as a continuation within the same space, or a re-establish? Distinct from GEOGRAPHY (which only asks "is this the same world at all") — a technically-same-location shot can still fail COVERAGE by reading as a re-establish.

    STATE CONTINUITY, CARRYMARKS-SCOPED (rule 36): the join-check hard-gates STATE only on the beat's own declared `carryMarks` — present in both frames or absent in both, never present in one and gone in the other. Any OTHER visible prop or incidental set dressing (a bee casually holding pollen, say) is explicitly excluded from the hard gate and surfaces only as an advisory `flags` entry — logged for the record, never blocking.

    NO RE-MINT ON A CUT (rule 32): an `intentional_next_shot` next beat (the default) opens off the RAW harvested settle frame directly — no NB2 cleanup pass, no drift check, `cb_qa.check_remint` not in the path. Re-mint (and its drift check) is retained only for a `seamless_continuation` next beat, whose @图1 really is used as literal first-frame pixels — the cleanup pass earns its keep there. State continuity for a cut moves entirely to the post-fire join-check instead of a pre-fire drift check — verification happens on the actual rendered result, not a synthetic proxy for it.

    THE V4 TEMPLATE (rule 30 — `cb_segprompt.emit_v4`/`for_beat_v4`, the definitive builder for every beat, v3/v2/v1 all retired to loud-fallback-only): the six-line reference contract (Law 3 above, with the corrected @Video1 line); one continuous `prompt` field carrying the opener, character bindings, an OPEN-ON sentence (Layer 2.3, intentional-cut beats only) then a labelled timing clock whose closing segment is always "settle in character:" (Law 9); `acting_rules` its own top-level field (Layer 2.6); `continuity` naming the beat's own carry marks plus the show's warm light; `prohibited` merging the beat's own staging list with the six standing negatives, always at least six. Dialogue words never appear in the prompt (Law 6); V3 fires in as @Audio1 and drives generation, never stitched on after (rule 29 — no post voice swap, even in a future two-step fallback).
    HISTORICAL NOTE (added 2026-07-08, contradiction sweep): this describes `emit_v4`/`for_beat_v4` as they stood 2026-07-05 — accurate then, not now. Both were deleted outright in rule 42's v4→v5 purge; the current definitive builder for every beat is `cb_segprompt.emit_v5`/`for_beat_v5` (see rule 42, and rules 51-54 for the reference-stack, tech-line and shot-timing revisions made to it since). Left as dated historical record rather than rewritten, matching this file's own established practice (rule 52) for entries describing a past, since-superseded state.

    THE GATES — MACHINE VS SHOWRUNNER: the machine checks what it CAN check, mechanically and vision-assisted — state continuity on declared carryMarks, spatial adjacency (Coverage), Clip QA (identity, anatomy, frozen, morph, pop-character), gag-element presence, the flag-only prompt-law lint (Laws 4/7/8, settle distinctiveness). Julian checks the only things that matter and that no gate owns: does it flow, is it funny, does the four-year-old watch it again. This is a RESERVED VERDICT, not a gap in the machine — no check is ever built to approximate it. The Layered Humour checklist rides above every mechanical gate and answers only to his eye: Fuzzby's chaos beats, Zenny's deadpan with its pause protected (Layer 2 lives in the pause; the settle is where her look breathes), the laugh-so-hard-they-miss-it ambition intact.

    THE WORKFLOW LOOP: fire → machine gates (two-tier join-check, Clip QA, prompt-law flags) → Julian's felt-intent verdict → sign → harvest → cut into the next beat, which declares its own junction type → assemble the next prompt from the skeleton plus its own creative slots → fire. Escorted through Scene 1 by hand; `cb_replicator.walk_scene` walks it thereafter under the identical laws — nothing beat-specific lives in its own code, only in the beat data. Gates per the escorted-run rules; scene boundaries reset to canon (the Scene Bubble Law).

    THE AUDIT MANDATE: a law living in a document can be forgotten; a law living in the emitter cannot. `PROMPT_LAWS_AUDIT.md` is a historical audit of v3's shape, not v4's plus this consolidated doctrine's — a fresh audit is a future ticket, not done automatically by this rule.

    THE ONE-RENDER ECONOMY (locked 2026-07-05, the full-reset night; wired into code the same night as the reset's first follow-up closer): one fire per artifact — the scene plate, a voice take, a keyframe, a beat render. Never a batch of exploratory seeds as the default. On a failed gate: ONE automatic re-fire, never blind. If that re-fire also fails: a HARD STOP with a diagnosis naming the layer at fault (keyframe / brief / reference / take, rule 3) — never a third roll. The voice pass is the same economy, stated explicitly: ONE directed V3 take per beat, never a batch of candidate reads — Julian's ear either approves it, or names the single correction for the one permitted re-fire; there is no "pick a favourite among several" step for voice, the same discipline the felt-intent gate already holds the beat render to. CODE-ENFORCED: `cb_beats.fire_next_beat`'s launch phase fires once, reads back the CLIP QA + JOIN CHECK verdicts `cb_beats.run` persists as `.qa.json`/`.join.json` sidecars, auto-retries once on a failure, and returns a `HARD_STOP` with a layer diagnosis (`cb_beats._layer_diagnosis`) if the retry also fails. `cb_replicator.walk_scene` carries the identical discipline for the scene's opening beat (`cb_beats._fire_gated`) and inherits it from `fire_next_beat` for every beat after. The old multi-seed default (`seeds=2`, "fire N takes, pick a winner") is retired everywhere it flowed — `fire_next_beat`, `walk_scene`, `cb_pipeline.relay_prepare`/`relay_approve`, and the Studio's seed-picker panel (`/api/beat-seeds`, app.html's "candidate seeds" UI) are all gone; a beat has exactly one official clip. Standard tier (`fast=False`) is the production default throughout.

    THE PRODUCTION LINE — THE CANONICAL STAGE MAP (locked 2026-07-05, the full-reset night; full prose version and rationale in `PRODUCTION_DOCTRINE.md`, mirrored in `REPLICATOR.md`): every stage names its gate holder. The machine checks; Julian signs. Nothing self-advances past any gate, ever (rule 1).
    - Stage 0 — Script-in: the script is the SOLE story source.
    - Stage 1 — Beat package → Gate 1: the script becomes the beat package (storyboard). Julian signs Gate 1.
    - Stage 2 — World and cast: the scene plate built then checked (`check_plate`) against the Crystal World Rule; character turnarounds verified against canon; the scene's ambient bed locked (the Scene Bubble Law). The machine checks; folds into Gate 2's review, no separate sign-off.
    - Stage 3 — Voice pass: one directed V3 take per beat, per the Character Voice Bible (the one-render economy, above). Julian's ear approves, or names the single correction.
    - Stage 4 — Gate 2: one anchor keyframe per SCENE (never per beat — a relay beat never gets its own), 2K, centre-safe, per-character action-state QA. Julian signs Gate 2.
    - Stage 5 — Gate 3: the escorted walk (`cb_replicator.walk_scene`) fires one render per beat, standard tier; machine gates (Clip QA, carryMarks-scoped state, Coverage, settle distinctiveness, the prompt-law lint); then the reserved felt-intent verdict, per beat.
    - Stage 6 — Gate 4: a retake fires only on Julian's own verdict, never a machine flag alone; one variable per re-fire (the retake protocol).
    - Stage 7 — Gate 5: post — settle-trim so the cut joins on living motion (the JOIN CONTRACT); beats assembled; the ambient bed continuous across the whole scene; music and grade; two masters delivered — the 16:9 feature master and a centre-safe 9:16 derivative. Julian's final-cut approval.
    A stage marked "the machine checks" is still a real gate — it just doesn't need Julian's own eyes on every single instance, the way Gate 1/2/3/4/5 do.
29. NO POST VOICE SWAP, EVEN IN A FALLBACK (Julian, 2026-07-05, ruled ahead of any architecture work coming out of the surface-research pass — every surface checked so far shows a literal first-frame parameter and a real audio reference as mutually exclusive modes, meaning a future fix may need a two-step generation instead of a single relay call): this does not reopen rule 4. The voice is never added after generation, full stop — that already forbids a lip-sync-overlay-in-post fallback exactly as it forbids one today (`cb_post` has no swap function by design; do not add one, to a two-step pipeline or any other). If a two-step fallback is ever built (e.g. lock the frame first, then a second pass for performance), the SECOND pass must be a generation pass — v2v or an edit-mode call — with `@Audio1` actually driving that generation, the same way it drives every single-step relay beat today. The distinction that matters: `@Audio1` conditions a model call, at every step, always; it never gets stitched onto a silent output afterward. Filed here, ahead of the research verdict, so no future session — human or Claude — sketches the wrong fallback shape while chasing the first-frame problem.
30. THE BLESSED TEMPLATE — v4 (Julian, 2026-07-05, "lock it as the template"): supersedes v3 (`cb_segprompt.emit_prose_v3`/`emit_json_v3`, the shots[]-array shape) as the definitive builder, for EVERY beat now, not just relay ones — `cb_segprompt.emit_v4`/`for_beat_v4`, wired first in `shipped_prompt`'s fallback chain, v3/v2/v1 all retired the same way v2/v1 already were (kept for rollback, loud fallback only). Rule 28's twelve Layer-1 laws still hold in SPIRIT (one beat/one prompt/15s split, opener-vs-relay, the anchor stack, no spoken words, the locked ambient bed, the living settle, the six negatives, style/world verbatim) but v3's CONCRETE shape (shots[], SPEAKER MAP, role-label binding handles) is obsolete; `PROMPT_LAWS_AUDIT.md` is now a historical audit of v3's shape, not v4's — a fresh audit against v4's shape is a future ticket, not done automatically by this rule.
    What changed, mechanically: `references` names every anchor including @Audio1 (now its own numbered reference, "voices" as a separate field is gone) as a terse one-liner, character identity welded directly to its @图N slot by NAME (rule 5, above); the shots[] array is gone, replaced by ONE continuous `prompt` field carrying a labelled "Timing: 0-Ns ...; N-Ms ..." clock built from the SAME per-cut weighted-duration split `_v3_shots` always used, with the settle as its own fixed trailing `HANDLE_SETTLE`-second segment (never folded into the last cut); per-shot camera description is dropped in favour of one whole-beat camera-discipline sentence — a real loss of per-shot camera detail, accepted for the terser whole; speaker binding is a name-plus-ordinal performance sentence ("Fuzzby's line comes first, Zenny's second"), replacing the shot-indexed SPEAKER MAP; `negatives` is renamed `prohibited` and MERGES the beat's own authored `stagingProhibited` list (new Layer-2 field — director-named failure modes specific to this beat's gag) with the six standing negatives (`_v3_negatives`, unchanged, still always exactly six); a new `continuity` field states the beat's persisting visible state (from `endStateStill`, rule 27 — still hand-authored, never auto-derived from `endState`) plus a fixed "carries into the next beat" tail.
    HISTORICAL NOTE (added 2026-07-08, contradiction sweep): both mentions above of "the six negatives" / "`_v3_negatives`, unchanged, still always exactly six" are stale in the same way rule 25's negatives description was — `_v3_negatives` was deleted outright in THE DEFINITIVE BUILD purge (rule 40); the live mechanism (`cb_segprompt._standing_negatives()`) has been an unconditional ELEVEN-item list since rules 43/44 (2026-07-06). Left as dated historical record rather than rewritten, matching rule 25's own HISTORICAL NOTE and rule 28 Law 10's CORRECTED note for the identical fact.
    Law 6 (no spoken words, ever) is UNCHANGED and un-reopenable under v4 — dialogue is still never quoted in the prompt.
    `cb_golden.py`'s content assertions were rewritten the same day to check v4's actual shape (they were hard-coded to v3's `shots[]`/`world`/`constraints`/`negatives` keys and failed across the board the moment v4 shipped — a tooling gap, not a real regression, closed in the same commit).
    Maiden fire: 1.B2, single seed, standard tier — the fourth consecutive frame-lock attempt on this same beat (after the camera-lock text, the bilingual first-line declaration, and subtraction, all three of which failed the join check identically). This rule locks the TEMPLATE regardless of that specific outcome — the structural improvements (name-welding, the timing clock, the merged prohibited list) stand on their own merit; whether v4 ALSO happens to solve the frame-1 problem is a separate, empirical question the same verdict-sheet discipline as every prior attempt answers.
31. THE JUNCTION-TYPE PIVOT (Julian's ruling, 2026-07-05 — "the relay has been enforcing the wrong kind of join"): the whole six-attempt frame-one campaign (rules 15/19-30's history, through the locked v4 template) was solving the wrong problem — treating every relay beat as a SEAMLESS CONTINUATION of the previous instant, when most beats are actually a NEW GAG ARC: an editorial cut to a fresh camera setup, already at the new beat's own energy from its first frame. Every beat now declares its OWN junction type (a beat-level field, `junctionType`):
    - `intentional_next_shot` — THE DEFAULT for every beat unless the director's cut explicitly says otherwise. A new gag arc: a fresh camera setup, the beat's own energy already in frame from its first instant. @图1's job changes from "first frame, locked" to STATE REFERENCE ONLY: identity, temporary marks/wardrobe (a pollen moustache, dirt, a held object), lighting and world-position carry forward exactly; camera, pose, energy and motion do NOT. Worked template: "Fuzzby and Zenny appear exactly as in @图1 — carry the smeared moustache, the pollen, the lighting and their positions in the world — and open on a fresh camera setup for this beat's action."
    - `seamless_continuation` — ONLY where the director's own cut explicitly says the shot continues (an unbroken take spanning a beat boundary). Here, and only here, Layer 1's Law 5 (anti-hold) and Law 3's "@图1 the opening frame, locked" still apply in full: frame one IS @图1 exactly, then motion moves decisively away.
    A beat that does not declare a junction type is `intentional_next_shot` by default — never `seamless_continuation` by omission.
    THE JOIN-CHECK SPLITS TWO-TIER (supersedes rule 19's single check): STATE CONTINUITY — marks, wardrobe, lighting, geography — is the HARD GATE for every join, no exceptions, checked whether the beat is a cut or a continuation. FRAME IDENTITY (does beat N+1's opening frame literally match beat N's ending frame) is checked ONLY on a declared `seamless_continuation` join; an `intentional_next_shot` join is never held to frame-identity, since a cut is expected to look different.
    Harvest and re-mint (rules 21-22) are UNCHANGED by this pivot — the anchor still carries state forward exactly as before; only what the NEXT beat's prompt asks that state to do (be repeated vs. be referenced) and what the join-check demands of it (frame match vs. state match) changes.
    Rule 28's Layer 1 (laws 2, 3, 5) and its workflow loop are updated in the same commit to state the junction-type branch as the current constitution — this rule stands as the record of why.
32. RE-MINT SCOPING (Julian's ruling, 2026-07-05, the same session as the junction-type pivot): rule 22's "re-mint is standard for every link now, not QA-triggered" is SUPERSEDED — re-mint is conditional on the NEXT beat's own declared junction type, not automatic on every harvest. For an `intentional_next_shot` next beat (the DEFAULT, rule 31): the raw HARVESTED settle frame IS @图1 directly — no NB2 re-mint pass, `cb_qa.check_remint` not in the path at all. @图1 is a state reference for this junction type (identity, marks, lighting, position — never a pixel-perfect anchor), so the NB2 cleanup pass (removing compression artifacts and motion blur before something is presented as "frame one, exactly") buys nothing; skipping it saves a generation call, cost and latency for the common case. Harvest itself is UNCHANGED — the sharpest frame in the settle window is still selected exactly as before (rule 21).
    Re-mint is RETAINED, not deleted, for exactly two cases: (1) a next beat declared `seamless_continuation` — its @图1 really is used as literal first-frame pixels, so the cleanup pass still earns its keep; (2) any future image-to-video use where the frame is genuinely literal first-frame pixels (e.g. a true FLF2V call, or the second pass of a rule-29 two-step fallback) — no code path exercises this second case today; when one is built, it must call `cb_scene.remint_settle_frame` the same way the seamless path already does, never skip it.
    STATE CONTINUITY MOVES ENTIRELY TO THE POST-FIRE TWO-TIER JOIN-CHECK (rule 31's `check_join_state`, a hard gate on every join regardless of junction type) for an `intentional_next_shot` beat — there is no more pre-fire drift check to catch a state mismatch before the next beat launches; the join-check on the ACTUAL rendered clip is the only guard, and it runs after the fire, not before. This is a deliberate trade: one less synthetic image in the chain, verification happens on the real result instead of a proxy for it.
    Mechanically: `cb_scene.relay_source_for` now decides harvest-vs-remint by the RESOLVING beat's own junction type (never merely "does a re-mint file happen to exist on disk" — a stale re-mint from before this ruling, or from a differently-typed sibling beat, must never be silently preferred). `cb_beats.fire_next_beat`'s prepare phase decides by the NEXT beat's junction type and returns a new `anchor` key (always populated — the re-mint when one ran, the raw harvest otherwise) alongside the now-sometimes-`None` `remint`/`drift_check` keys; every caller (`cb_pipeline.relay_prepare`, `cb_replicator.walk_scene`, the Studio UI's relay banner) reads `anchor`, never assumes `remint` is the only shape a prepared anchor takes. The Studio UI's Approve-Anchor button previously hard-gated on `driftCheck.ok===true`, which would have left the button permanently disabled for every future intentional-cut beat now that `driftCheck` is legitimately `None`, not failing — fixed the same day this rule was written (`cb-studio/app.html`'s two relay-banner blocks).
    Rule 28's workflow loop is updated in the same commit to state re-mint as conditional, not unconditional.
33. FUZZBY'S ENERGY RETURNS UNDER THE PIVOT (Julian's ruling, 2026-07-05, the same paste as rules 34/35): the junction-type pivot's early `intentional_next_shot` prompts (1.B2's first re-fire) read flatter than the show's own doctrine — Fuzzby's baseline manic energy (the long-standing "always fast, manic, hyper, clumsy" rule, `characters.json`'s Fuzzby.bible.motionRule) wasn't coming through the new state-carry/opens-on mechanics. New staging law: ERRATIC IN CHARACTER, PRECISE IN CHOREOGRAPHY — every manic action is a SPECIFIC NAMED GAG with cause and consequence (a described beat: rockets, brakes too late, loops once, stops); adjective-chaos ("wildly," "crazily," generic frenzy words with no concrete physical beat behind them) is BANNED as unreadable — it reads as noise to the model, not motion. Mechanically, four new/changed pieces:
    - `_v4_state_carry` (rule 31's @图1 clause) REWRITTEN from stuffing the predecessor's full endStateStill sentence inline (read as a prose blob, and "appear exactly as in @图1" contradicted the junction type's own freedom) to a fixed categorical template: "@图1 is the state reference from the previous beat. Carry everything it shows — {names}'s appearance, {marks}, the {scene noun} lighting and their positions in the world — but open on a fresh camera setup within the same space, close to where @图1 shows the characters. The camera moves; the world does not." `{marks}` comes from a new field, `carryMarks` — a SHORT phrase ("the pollen on their legs," "the smeared moustache") naming what specifically persists, hand-authored parallel to `endStateStill`, never a full sentence. `{scene noun}` is mechanical, from the scene's own `locationId` (`crystal_cove_meadow` -> "meadow").
    - A new per-beat field, `opensOn: {who, action}` — WHO the camera opens on and their immediate mid-motion state, rendered as a fixed "OPEN ON {NAME} mid-motion: {action}." sentence, immediately after the character bindings and before the timing clock. This is the Coverage Law's (rule 34) own eyeline-bridge mechanism, stated concretely per beat rather than left to a generic filler sentence — the OLD generic "This beat opens on its own fresh camera setup, already at this beat's own energy..." line is DROPPED for an `intentional_next_shot` beat (superseded by @图1's own reference text plus this concrete opens-on sentence; restating the same instruction a third time generically is the "reference re-description reads as drift" mechanism this session already found and fixed once, test #3/subtraction).
    - The timing clock's closing segment is now labelled "settle in character:" (not "settle:") and reads the beat's own authored `endState` VERBATIM (rule 27's directing prose, already existed — previously only consumed by the retired v3 `_v3_settle`; v4 had been using a flat, generic idle-life sentence instead) — so a manic character stays visibly manic even at rest ("vibrating with pride, hovering with tiny eager bounces") instead of flattening into the same boilerplate every beat shared. Falls back to the old generic idle-life sentence when a beat has no endState authored yet — never dead air (Handle Doctrine, rule 20).
    - A new top-level `acting_rules` field: per-character acting essence from a new, tightly-scoped one-line `characters.json` bible field (`actingRule` — distinct from the much longer essence/cadence/mannerisms prose, the same condensed-companion-line convention as WING_LAW/`_WING_LAW_ONE_LINE`) plus a new per-beat `actingContrast` field (which characters play off each other and how — a director's call, never hardcoded per-cast in Layer 1, since a future non-Fuzzby/Zenny cast has a different dynamic). Empty/omitted when no cast member has one authored yet.
    Also dropped the redundant "for children aged 4 to 8" from the prompt's own opener sentence (`style` already states the audience once — restating it was a Law-7-class duplication), and the camera-discipline sentence now names the SPECIFIC eyeline character and action character from `opensOn` (falling back to generic "character eyelines and action" when there's no 2-character opensOn to draw from) and is species-scaled ("bee-scale," mechanical from the cast, not hardcoded).
34. THE COVERAGE LAW (Julian's ruling, 2026-07-05): a scene is ONE CONTINUOUS SPATIAL BUBBLE. Every beat opens on a NEW ANGLE WITHIN the same space, close to where the previous beat ended, motivated by eyeline or motion — a foil character's look or turn (Zenny's head-turn tracking Fuzzby) is the STANDARD BRIDGE when the cut travels. Never a relocation, never a fresh establishing wide mid-scene — the wide belongs to the scene's OPENING beat only. @图1's state-carry clause (rule 33) already states this spatial leash explicitly ("open on a fresh camera setup within the same space, close to where @图1 shows the characters. The camera moves; the world does not") — this rule is the doctrine that clause exists to serve, and the new `opensOn` field (rule 33) is its per-beat mechanism.
    THE TWO-TIER JOIN-CHECK'S STATE TIER GAINS A FOURTH CRITERION, COVERAGE, alongside STATE/LIGHT/GEOGRAPHY (`cb_qa.check_join_state`) — checked on EVERY join, cut or continuation, same as the other three: does the next beat's opening frame read as a continuation within the same immediate space (a plausible new angle close to where the characters already were), rather than a fresh, pulled-back establishing wide that re-introduces the whole location from scratch, or a sense of having relocated? Distinct from GEOGRAPHY (which only asks "is this the same world at all") — a technically-same-location shot can still fail COVERAGE by reading as a re-establish.
35. THE SCENE BUBBLE LAW (Julian's ruling, 2026-07-05): a scene's identity is its THREE LOCKED CONSTANTS — the scene plate, the ambient bed, the style law — held VERBATIM across every beat inside it. A scene boundary is a FULL, DELIBERATE RESET: new plate, new ambient bed, a new canon-generated anchor keyframe for the scene's own opening beat, relay depth back to zero, no visual continuity owed across the boundary. Beat laws (rules 28/31/33/34) apply IDENTICALLY inside every bubble — nothing about them is scene-specific.
    This formalizes guarantees that were ALREADY structurally true before this rule was written, rather than requiring new code: `cb_scene.relay_source_for` can only ever see the beats it's handed (every caller filters by `sceneNumber` first), so a scene's own first beat always resolves `"first"` — no code path lets it relay off a different scene's settle frame (confirmed live, REPLICATOR.md). The three constants were already mechanically guaranteed too: `_v3_style()` reads the show's ONE profile-level style line (not per-scene, so trivially verbatim everywhere); `_v3_ambience(scene)`/`_v4_references`'s plate reference both read straight from THIS scene's own `ambientBed`/plate file, unchanged beat to beat within the scene (rule 25's "locked ambient bed" ruling already established this for ambience specifically). This rule is the DOCUMENTED, code-checked-against version of what was previously true only by construction.
36. THE STATE TIER HARD-GATES ON DECLARED MARKS ONLY (Julian's ruling, 2026-07-05, watching 1.B2's first `intentional_next_shot` fire — "hand-held pollen is NOT a carry mark — incidental set dressing, audience-invisible across a cut"): the join-check's STATE criterion (rule 31, `cb_qa.check_join_state`) previously hard-gated on ANY visible temporary substance or prop matching between the two frames — the first real fire under the pivot flagged a BROKEN join because a bee was holding a small cluster of pollen in the anchor and not in the rendered opening frame, even though that pollen was never a formally declared continuity item, just incidental business a director never asked the model to carry. REFINED: STATE hard-gates ONLY on the beat's own declared `carryMarks` field (a short phrase, rule 33 — e.g. "the moustache, the pollen on their legs" for 1.B2) — present in both frames or absent in both, never present in one and gone in the other. Any OTHER visible prop/substance discrepancy is EXPLICITLY EXCLUDED from the hard gate and instead surfaces as an advisory `flags` entry (`check_join_state`'s new `flags` return key) — logged for the record, never blocking, never affecting `ok`. LIGHT, GEOGRAPHY and COVERAGE are unaffected — they concern the world's own continuity, not a declared prop, and stay unconditional hard gates. When a beat has no `carryMarks` authored yet, STATE passes automatically rather than falling back to the old any-prop behaviour — the hard gate only ever checks something a director actually declared.
    Mechanically: `check_join_state(prev_end_frame, next_open_frame, carry_marks=None)` and `check_join(..., carry_marks=None)` both gained the parameter; `cb_beats.run`'s automatic join-check and `cb_replicator.walk_scene` both pass the beat's own `carryMarks` field and print any returned `flags` as non-blocking advisory lines, never halting on them.

37. THE MANIFEST — TWO CONTRACTS, STRICT REGIME (Julian's ruling, 2026-07-06 — "blanks BLOCK on both contracts — remove every generic fallback; a missing field halts with the field named"). Full field-by-field spec lives in `MANIFEST.md` at repo root (read that first — this rule is the dated record of why, not the working reference). Summary: every beat/scene/package must satisfy a TECHNICAL contract (endState, endStateStill, carryMarks, junctionType, a pauseHold stated and ≤1.5s, opensOn, actingContrast, speaker order, a best-effort single-gag-arc flag — per beat; plate/check_plate, ambientBed, locations-cache sync, vocab pass — per scene; actingRule — per character) and a CREATIVE contract (humourLayer 1-4, kidRead, adultRead, want/need, an emotion-as-mechanic statement — per beat; Fuzzby/Zenny ratio, a laugh beat per non-Heart pillar, one parent-layer line — per scene; the North Star questions answered in full — per package). Several CREATIVE fields (humourLayer, emotionMechanic, parentLine, the North Star package field) are NEW — not yet authored anywhere in the current package — and cb_preflight checks them for PRESENCE ONLY, never quality: whether a beat's humour actually LANDS at its stated layer stays the reserved showrunner verdict rule 28 already protects ("no check is ever built to approximate it") — the Manifest checks that the field exists, never that the joke works.
    THE FALLBACK SWEEP (same ruling): a multi-agent sweep of every prompt-authoring module found and fixed five generic-fallback sites in `cb_segprompt.py`'s live v4 template (`_v4_state_carry`'s "any temporary marks or wardrobe", `_v4_settle_text`'s idle-life boilerplate — the same bug diagnosed live on 1.B1's own settle this session, `_v4_continuity`'s "This mark carries into the next beat.") plus two retired-v3 sites, and six more in `cb_seedance.py`'s kept-intentional second validator (rule 10) — `pauseHold`, `continuity.in`/`continuity_out`, `visibility_rule` and camera-movement fallbacks. All raise `ManifestFieldMissing` now instead of shipping invented prose; `cb_beats.run`/`gate3_prepare` catch it as a hard BLOCK, distinct from a genuine emitter crash (which still falls back to an older builder, unchanged). Three sites in `cb_director.py` (the style-law-missing fallback, a character's missing `sizeRank` defaulting to 99, a static `"see config/continuity.json"` placeholder) are a DIFFERENT category — system/config-level defaults, not per-beat creative-content substitutes — found in the same sweep, deliberately left alone, named here so "every fallback removed" doesn't silently mean "except the ones we decided were a different kind."
    GATE ORDERING IN CODE: Gate N cannot arm without Gate N-1 signed AND both manifests green, for `walk_scene`, `fire_next_beat`, and every Studio button that fires or approves a gate (`cb_pipeline.approve`, `/api/fire`, `/api/approve`, `/api/regen`, `/api/relay-approve` in `cb-studio/serve.py`) — `cb_preflight.manifest_ok(pkg_path, scene, episode)` is the shared choke-point every one of these calls before proceeding.
    `shows/crystal-bears/bible/` — North Star, Layered Humour Model, Character Voice Bible, Five Pillars — is READ-ONLY canon the package builder (`cb_director.py`) must consult; Julian is adding these files, they do not exist yet as of this rule.
    THE GATE-1 EXTERNAL REVIEW RULE (same ruling): Gate 1 is never signed inside the Studio alone — the storyboard exports as a single document for Julian's own creative review outside the studio first; his sign-off follows that review, every scene, every time. The export mechanism itself is not yet built — recorded as standing doctrine now, a follow-up ticket for the code.

38. THE CAMERA LAW IS SCOPED TO SPOKEN LINES (Julian's ruling, 2026-07-06, Gate 1 review of 1.B1): rule 28's Layer 1 Law 8 ("camera law: locked during any vocal beat") is AMENDED — the lock applies to SPOKEN LINES only. A continuous hum or sing-song vocalization (no shaped mouth performance requiring a static frame to read) is motion-exempt; the camera may move under a hum exactly as it would under any other non-speaking action.
