45. THE CHARACTER VOCABULARY LAW + THE RE-MINT CORRECTION (Julian's rulings, 2026-07-06, live footage review of 1.B1 finding real scar tissue in shipped camera/action text — "where's the action... the cut saying what camera it is... where's the story beat?" — followed by the fix's own regression, "we've got to get it... the camera behaves like Fuzzby's overexcited wingman"):
    THE SHOT LIST, RESTORED (superseding the v5 engine's own flattened Beat-Story block, rule 42/43): `cb_segprompt._v5_beat_story` no longer compresses a beat's `cuts[]` into one summary sentence — it walks every cut, one paginated "Shot N (framing): action. Speaker speaks." line per cut (joined by `\n`, not the outer block-separator `\n\n`, so `cb_qa.check_gate3_lint`'s block-index parsing stays valid), ending on `endState`'s living settle. The doctrine's own 80-word sub-fence on this block is RETIRED — a real per-cut shot list cannot fit multiple cameras and actions in 80 words; the whole-prompt 400-word hard cap (`cb_preflight.py`) is the only backstop now, and beats will legitimately run into the 500s-600s. `_strip_spoken_words` (shared, Law 6) also strips a directly-preceding "with " before a quote, so removing inline dialogue from a camera note (e.g. "...pose, with 'Nailed it.' landing...") doesn't leave a dangling preposition.
    THE CHARACTER VOCABULARY LAW: every character's `characters.json` entry gains a `lexicon` field (`verbs` it owns, `banned` words that must never appear in a cut naming it) plus a `cameraRegister` note — populated for all 11 named characters (Fuzzby/Zenny by Julian's own dictated words; the other 9 mechanically extracted from each character's OWN existing bible/mannerisms/dos/donts text by a workflow, then adversarially re-checked against that same source — 2 invented words were caught and dropped this way, proving the check earns its keep). `cb_qa.check_character_vocabulary(beat)` scans each cut's framing+action for a named character and flags any of that character's banned words found in the same text — a real, stated heuristic limitation: a cut naming two characters checks both lists against the same combined text, so a word legitimately describing character A can false-flag against character B's ban if both are named in one cut (confirmed live, several times — never silently "fixed" by loosening the check, always judged case by case). Wired into `cb_qa.check_gate3_lint` as a hard BLOCK. Also wired INTO AUTHORING, not just linted after the fact: `cb_director.py`'s `_roster()` surfaces each character's lexicon in the system prompt's CAST LOCK, and a new Law 14 ("THE FOURTEEN STAGING LAWS") states the rule for the Director itself — proven live: a full-episode regeneration under Law 14 came back 42/43 beats vocabulary-clean on the first pass, vs. hand-scrubbing being the only prior tool. That regeneration was NOT kept (see below) — but Law 14 stays live in `cb_director.py` for all future authoring regardless.
    THE SCRUB: real scar tissue — soft/calm words describing FUZZBY's own action or the camera covering him, left over from "the camera fights" — found and fixed directly in the shipped beat data (never just in the compiled prompt): 1.B1 cuts 1-2 ("gently tracks"/"tracking gently" → "chases and banks"/"chases... banks hard... locks"), 5.B1 cut 3 ("Fuzzby slowly raises" → "Fuzzby snaps... up"), 8.B2 cut 4 ("Fuzzby slowly turns" → "Fuzzby whips around"). One case (1.B5 cut 1, "gently breathing camera" on a shared suspense two-shot) stayed a flagged judgment call, not silently rewritten — it isn't clearly "camera covering Fuzzby," it's an atmospheric beat, and only Julian's own ear decides that one.
    THE REGENERATION DECISION (a real fork, decided live): Law 14 was proven by regenerating the FULL episode via `cb_director.direct()` — genuinely better vocabulary (42/43 clean) and improved Scene-1 wording, but it also reset the entire separately-authored MANIFEST layer (junctionType/opensOn/endStateStill/ambientBed/carryMarks/parentLine/northStarAnswers — 0/43 beats clean vs. the old package's 22/46) and introduced a new duplicate-uppercase-pseudo-character bug, and the Director's own automatic Gate 1.5 review flagged 12/43 beats with real canon breaks (off-canon Crystal Call wording, bees wrongly given crystal glow, a pronoun drift, unfinished placeholder shots) — NOT CLEARED. Verdict: REVERTED to the old package (backed up, never touched by the regeneration) and applied the same vocabulary sweep + scrub surgically instead — cheaper, and the manifest layer + Scene 1's already-approved-pending render both survive intact. Law 14 itself was NOT reverted — it's permanent, for next time. The same canon spot-check also caught two PRE-EXISTING violations in the reverted-to package, unrelated to today's work: 6.B4/9.B2's Crystal Call lines weren't verbatim canon (fixed, now exact) and 6.B4 wrongly gives Zenny (a bee) a crystal glow in an invented "ALL" group call (flagged, NOT fixed — a staging decision, not a word-swap, left for Julian). 6.B3's "activate your crystal powers" and 9.B1's "chose courage" (Aida naming the lesson) are flagged the same way.
    THE RE-MINT CORRECTION (Julian, same day, after the vocabulary fix's own regression risk was named): rule 32's junction-type-only re-mint trigger is SUPERSEDED — "do not re-mint the last frame every time by default... use the approved raw harvested settle frame... re-mint only if the harvested frame is degraded, blurred, off-model, or unsuitable... constant re-minting can introduce drift because Nano Banana may 'beautify' or subtly change the character." A `seamless_continuation` next beat is UNCHANGED (still always re-mints — @图1 is literal first-frame pixels there, the cleanup pass earns its keep regardless of quality). An `intentional_next_shot` next beat (the default) now decides by the ACTUAL HARVESTED FRAME'S QUALITY, not by junction type alone: `cb_scene.harvest_needs_remint(path)` scores it via the same Laplacian-variance sharpness metric `harvest_settle_frame` already uses to pick the sharpest candidate (`SETTLE_SHARPNESS_MIN = 40.0`, calibrated conservatively against exactly one real sample — 1.B1's own harvest scored 316.2, clears it easily) — below the floor triggers a re-mint, at or above it uses the raw harvest directly, even if a stale re-mint happens to exist on disk. FLAGGED, NOT YET BUILT: an off-model/identity-drift check on the raw harvest itself (comparing it to the character turnarounds BEFORE deciding to re-mint, the same class of vision call `cb_qa.check_remint` already makes AFTER one) — the project's vision-QA API is currently quota-blocked end to end (see the CB_VISION_MODEL note in `cb_qa.py`), so this can't be built and verified in the same sitting; sharpness alone is a real check, not the whole one. `cb_scene.relay_source_for` and `cb_beats.fire_next_beat` both updated to the same gate.
46. THE FRONT-TO-BACK AUDIT (Julian, 2026-07-07, "you know exactly what this thing needs to do... check every gate... ensure everything works... no gaps, no mistakes"): a full audit of every gate's code path, the Studio UI, cross-module consistency, and live API health, run the night after the manifest-authoring pass (rule 45's own package). Found and fixed real bugs; found and explicitly left others for Julian's own judgment. Method: a parallel multi-agent audit (one finder per gate + UI file + a canon-drift sweep + a schema-consistency sweep) with every finding adversarially re-verified by an independent agent before being trusted, plus direct manual verification (a real functional test with stubbed I/O, not just a code read) on the highest-stakes fix.

    THE CRITICAL FIND — A GUARANTEED CRASH ON EVERY RELAY BEAT: `cb_beats.fire_next_beat`'s PHASE 2 (`approved=True` — the exact call `cb_replicator.walk_scene` makes to launch every beat after a scene's opener, and the same call `cb_pipeline.relay_approve`/the Studio's relay-approve button make) read a local variable `needs_remint` that was ONLY ever assigned inside the `if not approved:` branch (PHASE 1 — prepare). Since prepare and approve are genuinely SEPARATE function calls (per the function's own documented two-call contract), PHASE 2 could never see PHASE 1's local — a 100% reproducible `UnboundLocalError` on literally every relay beat launch, meaning Julian's own stated plan for tomorrow ("fire a keyframe, then animate it... walk the beats") would have crashed the instant it reached 1.B2. FIXED: PHASE 2 now re-derives the same decision by reading the settle frame PHASE 1 already left on disk (`cb_scene.harvest_needs_remint(settle_path)`) — the artifact persists across the two calls even though the local variable doesn't. Verified with a real functional test: a scratch package + fake media files + stubbed `cb_scene.harvest_needs_remint`/`cb_preflight.manifest_ok`/`cb_beats._fire_gated` (so no real API call fired), confirming PHASE 2 now completes end to end without crashing. This is the single most important fix of the night — everything else found was a correctness/completeness gap; this one was a guaranteed, immediate failure of tomorrow's own stated plan.

    THE MANIFEST_OK GAP ON THE PER-BEAT CASCADE: rule 37 states manifest_ok is the choke-point for "every Studio button that fires or approves a gate" — but the PER-BEAT buttons (`cb_pipeline.build_beat`/`gen_audio`/`render_beat`, behind serve.py's `/api/gen-keyframe`/`/api/gen-audio`/`/api/render-beat`) never called it at all; only the whole-scene `gate3()`/`regen()` path did. A beat with an outstanding manifest BLOCK could still have its keyframe built, audio generated, or clip rendered through the per-beat UI. FIXED: a shared `_manifest_gate_beat(scene, code)` helper added to `cb_pipeline.py`, called at the top of all three functions — same refusal message and choke-point pattern already used elsewhere. Verified: still proceeds cleanly for Scene 1 (fully manifest-clean), so tomorrow's actual plan is unaffected; correctly refuses for any scene still carrying a BLOCK.

    THE STALE BLOCK-INDEX MODEL (cb_qa.check_gate3_lint): when the camera+ambience paragraph was retired from `emit_v5` (2026-07-06, the thunder-leak bug), the shipped prompt's shape went from 8 parts to 7 — but this function's own block-index variables (`camera_amb_idx`, and its citation-map/exemption-set usage) were never updated, so `camera_amb_idx=5` silently started pointing at the TECH LINE instead of a now-nonexistent block, and the citation map kept citing `scene.ambientBed` as a source for content no longer in the prompt at all (the exact same "cites something not actually there" bug class, coincidentally). FIXED: renamed to `tech_idx`, removed the false citation, corrected `beat_story`'s citation from the retired `storyBeat` field to the real one (`cuts[] + endState`), corrected `_standing_negatives`' count from "ten" to the actual eleven, and added a self-check that flags (never blocks) if the index model drifts stale again. `cb_golden.py`'s own module docstring and two content assertions (the storyBeat-fragment check and the camera/ambience-verbatim check) had the identical staleness — the storyBeat check was failing UNCONDITIONALLY on every beat the moment every scene had a real `ambientBed`/beat had a real `endState` authored (rule 45's own manifest pass), since neither field is what the current shot list actually reads from; fixed to check the beat's own first-cut action text instead, and the docstring's numbered list corrected end to end.

    THE CAST-SIZE WORD-COUNT FIX (closing the long-open ticket): root-caused precisely, not guessed at — Block 2 (References) and Block 3 (Acting DNA) gave every cast member a full individual line regardless of whether that beat's own text ever named them doing anything. Measured: large-ensemble beats (scenes 6/8/9/10, 7-10 cast members) had as few as 1-2 of them actually mentioned in the beat's own cuts/dialogue/opensOn — the rest were purely "present in the scene," never acting in this specific beat, yet each still cost ~15-20 words of dedicated reference+DNA text. FIX: `cb_segprompt._v5_active_cast(beat, cast)` splits cast into ACTIVE (named in this beat's own cuts/speakers/opensOn) and BACKGROUND (present but doing nothing named here). Active members keep the full doctrine-exact "@图N Name — match exactly" + Acting DNA line; background members are consolidated into one shared reference line (still individually @图N-numbered — the image-upload order and count are completely untouched, only the TEXT describing them is terser) and get NO Acting DNA line at all (nothing in the shot list ever calls on a register that isn't being performed). Result: 36/43 beats now clean on both manifests (up from 31/43), word-count BLOCKs down from 12 to 7. The remaining 7 (6.B2, 6.B5, 7.B6, 8.B3, 9.B3, 10.B1, 10.B2) were checked individually and are NOT compiler waste — in every one of them, most or all cast members ARE genuinely named doing something distinct in that beat's own cuts (a real ensemble moment, not padding); cutting further would mean trimming Julian's own authored shot-list action text (rule 33: "leave the action-prose flavour language as-is," explicitly protected) or asking him to bless a cast-size-scaled word cap in place of the flat 400 he set explicitly — neither is mine to decide unilaterally, so both are named here, not quietly resolved either way. `cb_qa.check_gate3_lint`'s reference-congruence check and citation map, and `cb_golden.py`'s own assertions, were updated to match the active/background split — verified 0 golden-assertion failures and 0 vocabulary-law violations after every fix.

    THE SCENE-2 "LAUGH BEAT" FALSE BLOCK: `cb_preflight.check_scene_creative`'s "laugh beat per non-Heart pillar" check required a `comedyMode=BIG` beat in EVERY scene individually — but the Five Emotional Pillars (canon §2) are TIMECODE segments of the whole episode, and more than one scene can share one (Scene 1 and Scene 2 both fall in "The Everyday Spark," 0:00-2:00). Scene 2 is a single quiet vision beat with no comedic content of its own; Scene 1, its pillar-mate, already delivers five BIG beats — the pillar's own laugh requirement was already met, just not redundantly by Scene 2 itself. Reclassifying Scene 2's pillar to dodge the check would have been dishonest (pillar is a timecode fact, not a content tag to game); the check itself was checking the wrong scope. FIXED: `check_scene_creative` now accepts the full set of beats sharing a scene's pillar across the WHOLE package (`run()`'s new `beats_by_pillar` grouping) and checks that pool, not just the one scene's own beats — resolves Scene 2's gap honestly, without inventing fake comedy content or a fake pillar reassignment.

    THE SWEEP-THE-PATTERN FINDS (rule 11, applied again): the front-to-back audit's canon-drift sweep found the SAME class of bug as the Zenny-crystal find from the manifest-authoring night — a fix landing in a beat's `cuts[]` (the source of truth) but not sweeping every sibling field that duplicates the same content. Found and fixed: 8.B4's `cuts[1].action` + `i2vPrompt` both had Squeaky (canonically female) referred to as "his"; 7.B2's `i2vPrompt` + `script_truth_lock` both still spelled "HOWIE" after `cuts[]` was already corrected to "HOWEY." Both fixed in every sibling field found, not just the first one located.

    DIRECTOR'S EYE, RUN TO CONVERGENCE (six full passes across the two nights, each fixing what it found and re-running clean): confirmed genuine, fixable finds kept surfacing round over round — 3.B3's Mum line ("I still feel him… every day") stated her grief aloud against her own bible ("she never names that grief aloud... carries it in the hand that lingers on the wristbands") — made wordless, the hand lingering on the wristbands instead, and the DOWNSTREAM 3.B4 continuity note (which literally quoted that now-deleted line as "opens after Mum says...") was caught and fixed in the same pass, since it referenced content that no longer existed. 9.B2's crystalGlow described Keen's Crystal Call as "blazing" — reading as a power-up surge rather than the doctrine's required "surrender, not a power-up... sincerity, never volume" — softened to "warms fully on the call — sincere, not a surge." RESERVED, NOT CHASED FURTHER: by the sixth pass, remaining finds were increasingly SUBJECTIVE CRAFT CALLS rather than factual/mechanical errors (whether 6.B4/6.B5's Aida Crystal Call reads too much like a rescue-activation power-up, whether 7.B4's "He's not alone" line states the nadir's feeling too plainly, whether 1.B2's "officially nuts" tips from affectionate tease into name-calling, whether 8.B3's chorus line flattens the ensemble's personalities) — these are exactly the reserved showrunner verdict rule 28 protects ("Julian checks the only things that matter and that no gate owns: does it flow, is it funny... no check is ever built to approximate it"), named here for his own Gate-1 review rather than autonomously resolved. 8.B3's chorus-line finding specifically is MECHANICALLY already correct (chorusMembers/voiceTreatment exclude the bees, confirmed and fixed earlier the same night) — it keeps surfacing because the literal text label "ALL:" still reads ambiguous to any reader (human or LLM) who doesn't cross-reference chorusMembers; renaming that label risks breaking `cb_preflight`'s own hardcoded `"all"` magic-string exemption for the speaker-order check, so it was deliberately left as a named, understood residual rather than patched under time pressure.

    A SAFETY NET, NOT A FIX: `cb_pipeline.gate4()`/`gate5()` wrapped in try/except (an uncaught exception inside `cb_retake.process_retakes`/`cb_post.run` used to propagate as a raw traceback through `fire()`, whose own docstring promises it "never sys.exit itself... only returns True/False" — the ONE gate pair where that promise didn't actually hold). The audit's own root cause (`cb_retake.process_retakes`'s per-retake loop has no guard around a bad/stale package path) is named but NOT fixed tonight — Gate 4/5 are several steps beyond tomorrow's actual plan (fire a keyframe, walk Scene 1's beats), so the wrapper is the safety net for now, the real fix is a follow-up ticket. Also found, NOT fixed (same reasoning — real, but not on tomorrow's critical path): `cb_post.assemble_conformed` (the settle-trimmed "join on live motion" doctrine, rules 19/20) is fully implemented but has ZERO live call sites — Gate 4/5 both still use `assemble_picture` (the raw butt-join) exclusively, meaning the documented conform behaviour is not what actually ships; and `cb_director.py`/`cb_director_schemas.py` (the Director's own LLM output schema) never define or request ANY of the manifest's newer fields (`junctionType`/`opensOn`/`carryMarks`/`endStateStill`/`actingContrast`/`humourLayer`/`emotionMechanic`/`parentLine`) — meaning the CURRENT package is manifest-clean only because of tonight's and last night's separate manual/scripted authoring passes, and a future Gate-1 re-fire (`cb_director.direct()`, e.g. via `redirect()`) will silently wipe every one of them, re-opening 400+ manifest BLOCKs that will look like a new bug rather than an expected consequence of re-authoring Gate 1. Recorded here explicitly so that surprise never happens without warning.

    API HEALTH, CONFIRMED LIVE, ZERO COST: Gemini (`gemini-3.5-flash`, the current `CB_VISION_MODEL`), ElevenLabs, and fal.ai all authenticated successfully — verified via the SAME endpoints/auth patterns the real code paths use (not a guessed REST path), each with the cheapest possible non-generating call (a one-word text completion, a voices-list, a fal queue-status lookup on a fake id cross-checked against a deliberately-bad key to prove the distinction was real auth, not endpoint noise). No image/audio/video generation was triggered by this check.
47. THE MANIFEST LAYER IS NOW AUTHORED BY GATE 1 ITSELF (Julian, 2026-07-07 — "surely the stuff that you've done needs to be written into some form of script or code for it to be used in every single project going forward, not just this one"): rule 46's own schema-drift finding named the exact gap — `cb_director_schemas.py`'s Beat/Scene models never defined `junctionType`/`opensOn`/`carryMarks`/`endStateStill`/`actingContrast`/`humourLayer`/`emotionMechanic`/`ambientBed`/`parentLine`, so the CURRENT package was manifest-clean only because of two separate manual authoring passes, and any future Gate-1 fire (this episode or any other) would silently produce a package needing the same manual rescue all over again. Closed at the source, not patched downstream:
    - `cb_director_schemas.Scene` gains `ambientBed` (the scene's one locked ambient-sound-bed line) and `parentLine` (the scene's adult-layer read) — both required (OpenAI strict structured outputs cannot omit a required field; there is no "silently missing" state).
    - `cb_director_schemas.Beat` gains `endState`/`endStateStill`/`carryMarks`/`actingContrast`/`humourLayer`/`emotionMechanic` (required on every beat) and `junctionType`/`opensOn` (nullable — null is valid ONLY for a scene's own first beat, which has no predecessor to join from; a new `OpensOn{who, action}` model backs the latter).
    - `cb_director.episode_to_scenes`'s and `cb_director.scene_to_beats`'s own prompts were extended with the EXACT field definitions this session already worked out by hand — the same Layered-Humour 1-4 scale, the same endState-vs-endStateStill distinction (directing prose vs. a static photograph, no temporal verbs), the same carryMarks/actingContrast/opensOn definitions — so a future Gate-1 fire produces the SAME quality bar this session hand-authored, not a fresh reinvention. `pauseHold`'s own prompt line was tightened to require the exact machine-parseable "N second(s)" form and state the <=1.5s cap explicitly (closing the SAME formatting gap that needed 15 beats hand-reformatted last night).
    - `cb_director._finalize_beat_manifest_fields(beats)`, called right after `beatCode` assignment (so the opener is identifiable) and before `validate_scene_beats`: DEFENSE IN DEPTH, not a substitute for the prompt — an LLM can still drop or misfire a field despite clear instructions (the same belt-and-braces principle the verbatim gate already uses for dropped dialogue). A non-opener beat with a missing/invalid `junctionType` is defaulted to `"intentional_next_shot"` (rule 31's own named default — mechanical, never invented prose); a non-opener beat with no `opensOn` gets one mechanically derived from its own cut 1 (who + a short action phrase), the SAME extraction technique already used when this field was first hand-backfilled. An EXPLICIT `"seamless_continuation"` or a complete, real `opensOn` from the Director is never touched — this only fires when the Director's own output is missing or invalid, never overriding a real creative choice.
    - `cb_director_schemas.beat_problems` (the existing business-rule validator, already wired into the retry/repair loop) gained checks for `humourLayer` (must be 1-4), `pauseHold`'s exact format and 1.5s cap, `junctionType`'s valid values, `opensOn` presence on non-openers, and `carryMarks` presence — so a bad VALUE (not just a missing field, which Pydantic already catches) drives the SAME repair call other business-rule violations already trigger, at authoring time, rather than surfacing only later as an unrepaired `cb_preflight` BLOCK.
    - `cb_director._derive_north_star_answers(all_beats, scenes)`, wired into `direct()`'s final package assembly: builds the package-level `northStarAnswers` field ENTIRELY MECHANICALLY from the package's own already-authored data (comedyMode=BIG counts per pillar, the wordlessHeld beat count and identity, structural presence of want/need/kidRead/adultRead/parentLine) plus the actual canon text (CRYSTAL_BEARS_LOCKED_CANON.md §0's real 4 test questions + 8 craft laws) — NO LLM call, so this costs nothing extra and can never invent content or drift from what canon actually says. Explicitly states the same caveat cb_preflight's own check already names (canon defines 4 questions + 8 laws, not a literal "six" — Julian's own definition of the exact six is still needed for a stricter future check).
    - VERIFIED LIVE, not just read: a real `scene_to_beats` call fired for Ep1 Scene 2 (its smallest scene, kept cheap) using the actual script/theme/cast, with zero manual correction afterward — both returned beats passed `beat_problems()` clean AND `cb_preflight.check_beat_technical`/`check_beat_creative` with ZERO BLOCKs, straight out of the LLM. One beat correctly left `junctionType`/`opensOn` null (the scene's own first beat); the other correctly self-authored `junctionType: "intentional_next_shot"` and a real, well-formed `opensOn` — the defensive fallback never had to fire. `pauseHold` on both beats came back in the exact required format, first try. The live production package (`cb-output/Ep1_The_Adventure_Begins_beat_package.json`) was never touched by this test — confirmed unchanged (still 36/43 clean) before and after.
    - NOT DONE, named honestly at the time — since closed (see the "100% right" pass below): the Scene-level fields (`ambientBed`/`parentLine`) were first verified only by the Pydantic schema's own guarantee of presence.

    THE "100% RIGHT" PASS (Julian, same day, immediately after — "do whatever you gotta do to get this 100% right"): three more real gaps found and closed, none of them hypothetical — all found by tracing the ACTUAL repair/retry code paths, not just re-reading the happy path.
    - THE REPAIR-CALL GAP: `validate_scene_beats`'s repair call (`cb_llm.repair_call`) returns entirely NEW beat objects when `beat_problems` finds something wrong — but `_finalize_beat_manifest_fields` had only ever been called ONCE, on the ORIGINAL draft, before `validate_scene_beats` even ran. A repair triggered by an UNRELATED problem (say, a bad `humourLayer`) could return beats whose `junctionType`/`opensOn` were STILL missing or invalid, with no defensive layer left to catch it — the only backstop left was the final `beat_problems` re-check, which would then raise `SceneBreakdownError` and HALT the whole Gate-1 fire over something mechanically fixable. Fixed: `_finalize_beat_manifest_fields(fixed)` now runs again on the repaired beats, in the same spot, before the final re-check.
    - THE BLANK-STRING GAP (beats): a `str` field being *required* in Pydantic only guarantees the KEY is present — nothing stops an LLM from satisfying "required" with `""`. `beat_problems` didn't check `endState`/`endStateStill`/`actingContrast`/`emotionMechanic` (or, for that matter, the pre-existing `want`/`need`/`kidRead`/`adultRead`) for blank content at all — a blank value would sail through Pydantic AND the original `beat_problems` list, surfacing only much later as an unrepaired `cb_preflight` BLOCK with no retry left to fix it. Fixed: `beat_problems` now checks all eight for non-blank content, driving the same repair call every other business-rule violation already does.
    - THE BLANK-STRING GAP (scenes): the identical risk one level up — `Scene.ambientBed`/`Scene.parentLine` were plain `str` fields, so an empty string would satisfy Pydantic without raising `ValidationError`, meaning `structured_with_repair`'s own repair loop (the ONLY validation `episode_to_scenes` has — there is no scene-level `beat_problems` equivalent) would never even fire. Fixed at the schema level: both fields now carry `Field(min_length=1)`, turning a blank value into a real, catchable `ValidationError` that the existing repair mechanism already knows how to handle — verified directly (an empty-string `Scene` now raises `ValidationError` with 2 errors; real content still validates clean).
    - THE FULL EPISODE, FIRED FOR REAL, SANDBOXED: rather than stop at the single-scene test above, a COMPLETE Gate-1 fire (`cb_director.direct()`, all 10 scenes, theme_lock through Director's Eye) was run against the real Ep1 script — the single biggest possible verification of this whole fix. Made safe two ways before firing: `cb_director.OUT` was monkeypatched to a scratch directory (the real `cb-output/` package is never touched, confirmed by hash before/after), and the episode label was set to `"Ep1_SCHEMA_TEST"` (never `"Ep1"`) so the three shared, merge-only config files (`config/locations.json`/`continuity.json`/`episode_arc.json`, each keyed by episode string) get an isolated new key instead of overwriting the real Ep1 entries — the exact protection those files' own code comments already describe ("only this episode's own key is ever replaced").
      THE RESULT: ran for real, 3403s, exit code 0, 41 beats across 10 scenes. `cb_preflight.py` against the fresh, wholly unassisted output: **35/41 beats fully manifest-clean on both contracts** (up from 0/43 before any manifest work this session, and clean WITHOUT either of the two manual authoring passes rule 46/47 opened by describing). The remaining 7 BLOCKs were individually checked, not just counted — all confirmed pre-existing, already-documented, unrelated issue categories: 6 are the cast-size word-count bloat on genuine large-ensemble beats (rule 46's own "left for Julian" list, a cast-cap policy question, not a manifest-authoring bug), 1 is the recurring name-misspelling pattern ("HOWIE") already fixed multiple times elsewhere in the live package. The character-vocabulary lint on the same fresh package flagged 2 minor violations (both "gently" false-flagged against Fuzzby's ban list in an ensemble cut that also names another character) — the exact cross-attribution heuristic limitation rule 45 already documents, not a new bug. The real production package was confirmed byte-identical (MD5) before and after the entire test, and the three `Ep1_SCHEMA_TEST` config keys were deleted from `locations.json`/`continuity.json`/`episode_arc.json` immediately after verification, leaving no test cruft in shared production config.

48. THE PIXAR-CRAFT GATE (Julian, 2026-07-07 — "I want you to create the software that ensures that we are five out of five on 'Would a Pixar shorts team be proud of this text.'"): the manifest gate (rule 37) proves every field exists and is non-blank; it deliberately never judges whether the content is any good (rule 37's own words: "the Manifest checks that the field exists, never that the joke works"). A same-night independent audit (a 20-agent read-plus-skeptic pass over all 10 scenes, scored against a concrete rubric — character-voice swap test, gag escalation vs. adjective-chaos, surrender-not-power-up North Star fidelity, bible traceability, a cold Pixar-benchmark verdict) found the storyboard genuinely disciplined in places (North Star fidelity, 3.8/5 average — the strongest dimension) but "competent" far more often than "Pixar" (2.95/5 average on the benchmark question), with one repeated, independently-confirmed failure mode: named ensemble bears with rich, specific bibles reduced to interchangeable reaction verbs the moment 3+ share a beat. The SAME audit, cross-checked directly against the parsed script, also surfaced a genuine mechanical bug, not a taste call: Scene 9 shipped two invented Aida lines and a dropped word in Keen's Crystal Call despite each beat's own `script_truth_lock` field explicitly claiming verbatim use.

    THIS RULE TURNS THAT AUDIT'S METHOD INTO PERMANENT SOFTWARE, not a one-off finding:

    - `cb_preflight.check_scene_dialogue_verbatim` (new, hard BLOCK, zero LLM): compares every scene's shipped cut dialogue against `cb_script.dialogue_lines` — the SAME deterministic ground truth `cb_director.enforce_verbatim` already uses at Gate-1 AUTHORING time — but as a STANDING, RE-RUNNABLE check, callable at any gate-arming point, not a one-shot guarantee that only holds until the next manual edit. This is the exact gap that let Scene 9's drift ship undetected: `enforce_verbatim` only ever ran once, inside `direct()`'s own authoring pass; nothing re-checked a package hand-edited afterward (a manifest-authoring pass, a scrub, a future Studio edit). Uses `difflib.SequenceMatcher` LCS alignment, NOT a blind positional zip — a naive zip cascades a false mismatch onto every later line once ANY one line is legitimately dropped (Scene 3's deliberate, already-approved cut of Mum's "I still feel him... every day" line shifted every subsequent index out of phase, turning six already-CORRECT beats into false positives before the fix). Verified live: correctly re-discovers Scene 9's exact three drifts with zero false positives, and correctly reports Scene 3's one genuine cut as a single MISSING line (with an explicit caveat that a deliberate creative cut is not the same as an accidental drop, per the T2 ruling) rather than a cascade.
    - THE SAME AUDIT ALSO FOUND A REAL PARSER BUG, SWEPT AND FIXED: `cb_script.py`'s dialogue-collection loop swallowed two trailing ACTION sentences ("Fuzzby zooms right into Keen's face.", "Aida smiles.") onto the end of the preceding character's dialogue, because the source screenplay omits a blank line before them — meaning the "ground truth" `enforce_verbatim` itself compares against was, in these two spots, already polluted with action prose. Fixed with a narrow, transparent heuristic (a new sentence starting with another roster character's own name, directly after a completed line, with no blank line in the source, stops dialogue collection) — logged via `warn()` every time it fires, so a false application would be visible, never silent. Verified: fires exactly twice across the whole script (the two known cases), nothing else.
    - `cb_preflight.fix_scene_dialogue_verbatim` — the MECHANICAL auto-fix, safe to apply automatically (zero LLM, zero creative judgment) because the script IS the ground truth by the Faithful Director law; there is no version of "the script says X" that is a taste call. Uses the SAME difflib alignment as the check (an earlier draft used the old naive zip and would have MISCORRECTED every beat after a legitimate drop — a fixer less precise than its own check is a real hazard, caught before it ever ran for real). Only auto-fixes a clean 1:1 replace; a delete/insert/uneven-replace is logged and left for a human decision, never guessed at. Verified on a scratch copy: 6 safe fixes applied automatically, the 3 genuinely ambiguous cases (Scene 3's deliberate cut, Scene 6/9's multi-line rewrites) correctly left untouched. NOT YET applied to the real production package — built and proven, not run for real, pending Julian's own review of what it would change.
    - `cb_craft.py` (new module) — `CRAFT_RUBRIC`, the audit's exact 5-criterion bar, as ONE canonical constant so no future caller reinvents a vaguer one (rule 17's own standing lesson: a vague "is this good" question is what a generous grader waves through every time). `score_scene_craft()` runs the SAME dual-read design the one-off audit used — an independent first pass plus a deliberately skeptical second pass, MINIMUM score per criterion across both — now callable from code, not a throwaway workflow. Deliberately NOT wired into `cb_preflight.run()`'s default path: it costs real API calls, and `cb_preflight` runs on every gate-arming check, potentially many times a session — the exact reason `cb_qa.py`'s own vision checks (`check_plate`, `check_clip`) already live outside `cb_preflight` and run at their own natural point instead, per that module's own stated design. Verified live on Scene 1: produces coherent, evidence-cited scores in the same range as the one-off audit (a beat structural bug: the earlier value used a shared `model=cb_llm.VALIDATOR_MODEL` for both reads — cheaper and consistent with this codebase's existing "validator model does the checking work" convention).
    - `cb_craft.check_ensemble_individuation()` — deterministic, zero-LLM, FLAG-only (a best-effort heuristic, the same "report, never hard-block a computed proxy" convention `cb_preflight`'s own "single gag arc" check already uses): for a beat naming 3+ characters, checks whether each one's action-text window touches a verb from their OWN `characters.json` `lexicon.verbs`, and separately flags when 2+ characters are given the exact same verb in one beat — a concrete, checkable proxy for the audit's "wallpaper ensemble" finding. Deliberately does NOT flag a character simply being ABSENT from a beat's action text — rule 46's own active/background cast split (`cb_segprompt._v5_active_cast`) already established that a background member correctly staying silent is the deliberate, correct fix for the cast-size word-count bug, not a fresh finding to re-litigate. Wired into `cb_preflight.run()` as `check_beat_ensemble` (cheap, so it belongs alongside the rest of the manifest's zero-cost checks, unlike the LLM-based score). Verified live: 5 genuine flags against the real package, all independently consistent with the audit's own findings (e.g. Scene 9's Luna/Zenny "hold" duplication), zero false positives from the corrected active/background design.
    - `cb_craft.propose_ensemble_fix()` — a targeted repair PROPOSAL, never auto-applied, matching Law 3's discipline ("no output is ever hand patched... a fault routes through a retake") extended to un-rendered storyboard text: rewrites ONLY the flagged characters' action clauses, each grounded in a QUOTED bible field (`citedSources`), leaving everything else untouched, for a human to review and apply. Verified live on 9.B3: "Zenny holds a flat look" → "Zenny gives a flat look" (removing the duplicate verb) and "Luna holds a soft smile" → "Luna settles into a soft smile" (drawing on her own bible's "settling, lowering, smoothing" language) — a genuinely specific, minimal, cited improvement. ONE real, honest limitation found in the same test: the model re-emits the WHOLE action text rather than a true in-place edit, and introduced one incidental typo in an untouched cut during the live test — a real risk of this shape of call, caught by the fact that this is a proposal for human review, never an auto-apply, exactly the safeguard this design already has for precisely this failure mode.

    WHAT THIS RULE DOES NOT DO, STATED EXPLICITLY (rule 28's reserved verdict, unchanged): no check here ever auto-signs Gate 1 on a craft score, and none was built to approximate "is it funny, does it fly" — that stays Julian's own reserved verdict. What changed is that every CONCRETE, NAMEABLE sub-finding a human reviewer could point to (a swapped-verb ensemble, an invented dialogue line, a dropped word) is now caught by re-runnable software instead of living only in a one-off audit's transcript — so the next storyboard, this episode or any other, ships with these specific, checkable gaps already surfaced, not rediscovered from scratch by another manual pass.

49. THE CONTRADICTION AUDIT (Julian, 2026-07-07, immediately after rule 48 — "when we find things, we have to go software-wide, but we also have to be careful we don't have contradicting old code and everything else. We just need to audit it all the time."): every software-wide fix now gets swept, the SAME session, for contradiction/duplication against existing code — not treated as done once it's built. Ran a 5-question adversarial audit against rule 48's own new gates; two questions came back as unusable placeholder output on the first pass (correctly caught and rejected by the independent verify stage rather than reported as fact) and were re-run for real. Findings:

    - CONFIRMED CLEAN: rule 48's own CLAUDE.md prose matches the actual code line-for-line (no drift in what was just written); the v1-v4 emitter purge (rules 40/42) still holds, re-verified exhaustively against a 53-name list with zero live survivors.
    - THREE GENUINE ORPHANS closed: `cb_craft._WORD_RE` (a truly dead constant, deleted) and two fully-built, fully-tested mechanisms with ZERO call sites anywhere — `cb_preflight.fix_scene_dialogue_verbatim` and `cb_craft.propose_ensemble_fix` — given real CLI entry points (`cb_preflight.py --preview-dialogue-fix`, dry-run only, never writes, matching this module's own "never edits anything" contract; `cb_craft.py --propose-fix=<beatCode>`) so they are deliberately invocable instead of silent dead code. Both re-verified safe after wiring (production package hash unchanged).
    - TWO DOCUMENTATION STALENESSES fixed: `MANIFEST.md` never got the two new rule-48 checks added to its field tables, and separately still described a `actingRule`/`actingDNA` per-character check that rule 28's own THE ACTINGDNA RETRACTION had already replaced with `actingNote`/`bible.mannerisms`+`dos`+`donts` in the SAME session it was introduced — both corrected, with the correction dated and reasoned inline, not silently overwritten. `PRODUCTION_DOCTRINE.md`'s Stage 0 line mis-attributed the new dialogue-verbatim hard-block to `cb_qa` (which has no such function) — fixed to correctly name `cb_preflight.check_scene_dialogue_verbatim` as the standing check, distinct from `cb_director.enforce_verbatim`'s one-shot authoring-time snap.
    - A REAL, REPRODUCIBLE HEURISTIC COLLISION, REPORTED NOT FIXED: `cb_craft.check_ensemble_individuation` (new) scopes its lexicon-verb match to a ~120-char window after a character's OWN name; `cb_qa.check_character_vocabulary` (existing, rule 45) scopes its banned-word match to the WHOLE cut's text whenever that character is merely named anywhere in it — its own docstring already states this coarse heuristic explicitly. Because some characters' `lexicon.verbs` and OTHER characters' `lexicon.banned` genuinely overlap (Luna's "slow" / Fuzzby's "slowly"; Howey's "reach" / Sunny's and Misty's "preach"), a single cut naming both can be judged "well-individuated" by the new check and "a banned-word violation" by the old one, for the exact same word, at the same time. Deliberately NOT auto-fixed by narrowing `check_character_vocabulary`'s scope, despite that being the obvious-looking fix: rule 45 already explicitly ruled this exact coarse-scope trade-off is "never silently 'fixed' by loosening the check, always judged case by case" — narrowing it could just as easily cause the check to MISS a real violation (the camera-covering-Fuzzby case rule 45's own comedy-vocabulary law was built to catch). Named here as new information for Julian's own judgment, not resolved unilaterally.
    - THE MOST SIGNIFICANT FIND, VIA DIRECT CODE READING AFTER TWO WORKFLOW ATTEMPTS FAILED: investigated by hand (`cb-studio/serve.py`, `cb_pipeline.py`) after the audit workflow twice failed to produce usable output on "does every Studio endpoint route through the manifest choke-point." Found real, in two layers:
      1. `cb_pipeline.rebuild()` (all-keyframes clean rebuild) and `cb_pipeline.approve_beat()` (the per-beat stage sign-off) never called `manifest_ok`/`_manifest_gate_beat` at all — the exact same bug class rule 46 already fixed for `build_beat`/`gen_audio`/`render_beat`, just two more functions the earlier sweep missed. Fixed using the SAME existing `_manifest_gate_beat(scene, code)` helper, gating `approve_beat` only on `value=True` (locking) — unlocking, like the scene-level `unapprove()`, stays ungated as a safe, reversible action.
      2. A DEEPER bug, found while fixing the first one: `build_beat`/`gen_audio`/`render_beat`/`approve_beat` all HAD working manifest refusals inside their Python bodies (rule 46's own fix), but `cb_pipeline.py`'s own `__main__` dispatch never checked their return value and never called `sys.exit(1)` on refusal — so the CLI subprocess always exited 0, and `cb-studio/serve.py`'s job-status poll reads ONLY the subprocess return code (serve.py:323-324) to decide "done" vs "failed." A refused per-beat action was therefore silently reported to the Studio UI as SUCCESSFUL — the exact failure mode `fire()`'s own docstring already named and fixed once for whole-gate actions ("a refused fire was silently reported as 'done'"), never swept into this whole sibling command family. Fixed for all five (`build-beat`, `gen-audio`, `render-beat`, `approve-beat`, `rebuild`): each now returns an unambiguous `True`/`False` (not a bare `None`), and `__main__` calls `sys.exit(1)` on refusal — EXCEPT `gen-audio`, checked via `is False` specifically rather than a plain truthiness test, because a wordless beat legitimately returns `None` as a valid, non-error result ("that's valid, not an error," per the function's own pre-existing comment) and must not be confused with a manifest refusal.
      Verified live against the real, currently-BLOCKed Scene 9 (its own genuine dialogue-verbatim BLOCKs from rule 48, not a fabricated test case): `build-beat`, `gen-audio`, and `approve-beat` (locking) each now correctly exit 1 with the refusal message printed; `approve-beat` (unlocking) still succeeds unconditionally, exit 0, confirming the lock/unlock asymmetry is preserved. Production package hash confirmed byte-identical before and after every test in this rule.

    METHOD NOTE: two of the five original audit questions returned unusable placeholder content (literal "test"/"one"/"two" stand-ins) rather than real findings on the first pass — caught correctly by the independent verify stage, which refused to pass them through as fact. Both were re-run; one produced a real, well-evidenced finding (the lexicon-collision item above) on retry, the other failed a SECOND time even with a hardened schema — at that point the investigation was done by hand instead of retried a third time, which is what actually found the exit-code bug. Lesson for future audits: two consecutive workflow failures on the same question is the signal to switch to direct investigation, not to retry blindly.

50. MINING "SEEDANCE PROMPT ENGINE" (Julian, 2026-07-07, sharing a reference tool he built): a browser-only Claude Artifact (uses `window.storage`, calls the Anthropic API unauthenticated — meant to run inside the Artifact sandbox, not deployable as-is; its hardcoded model id, `claude-sonnet-4-6`, doesn't match any current Claude model name either) compiling the same `seedance-20` skill doctrine this project already has access to into a single-clip prompt compiler. Not integrated as code — no beat-package/character/script awareness, different runtime entirely — but its baked-in doctrine was worth cross-checking against the v5 engine's actual shipped output.

    THE COMPARISON (real v5 output for 1.B1 vs. a faithful hand-compile of what the artifact's own Director Formula — Subject+Action+Scene+Camera+Light+Audio+Constraints, MULTISHOT mode's "Shot 1:/Shot 2:/..." labelling — would produce for the identical beat): mode gates, reference roles (one job per reference + an exclusion), and continuation invariants all independently validate what this project already does, usually more rigorously — the Four/Five-Anchor Grounding Set (rules 24/26) IS that same "one role per reference" principle; the harvested-settle-frame doctrine (rules 21/26/27) is the SAME "start from the observed final frame" rule, done with an actual video frame instead of a typed description. Two things in it were genuinely missing:
    - An anti-slop lexicon — a concrete banned list of generic AI-video filler (cinematic, stunning, masterpiece, 8K...) versus the Character Vocabulary Law's per-character bans, which say nothing about GENERIC filler independent of any character.
    - The allocation model's own core rule — "identity fidelity, motion boldness, and scene density compete for one budget... never demand a perfect face + a backflip + a crowd + a spoken line in one call" — which names the exact missing piece behind rule 48's own ensemble-wallpaper finding: no beat ever explicitly designated who it's actually spending its craft budget on.

    BOTH BUILT AS SOFTWARE, per rule 49's own standing directive (never a one-off), immediately:
    - `cb_qa.ANTI_SLOP_WORDS` / the (9)th check inside `check_gate3_lint`: hard BLOCK in the Beat Story block (freely-authored dynamic prose — no ruling stands behind a slop word landing there), FLAG-only everywhere else (a hit in the style law or the tech-close line names a LOCKED constant, not this beat's own authoring). Immediately useful the day it was built: the tech-close line's own locked constant, `"...smooth cinematic motion..."`, trips the list on every single beat — a real, honest finding the check surfaced about the show's own existing prompt template, not silently exempted. Verified against all 43 real beats: zero hard BLOCKs (the beat-story prose is genuinely clean, consistent with rule 48's own audit finding that per-beat action text is the storyboard's strongest layer), 86 FLAGs (2 locked-constant hits × 43 beats, exactly as predicted) — and a synthetic mutation test confirmed the BLOCK path fires correctly on real slop injected into a beat's action text.
    - THE FIDELITY-ALLOCATION LAW: a new Layer-2 field, `fidelityAllocation: {primary, secondary, economized}`, authored per beat (`cb_director_schemas.FidelityAllocation`, `Beat.fidelityAllocation`) — an explicit, non-optional choice of who this beat's craft budget spends on, closing the exact gap the audit found rather than just detecting it after the fact. `cb_director.scene_to_beats`'s prompt states the field's exact definition (primary is ALWAYS a real name, even for a solo beat; economized names every OTHER present character deliberately kept generic this beat); `_finalize_beat_manifest_fields` mechanically defaults a missing/blank primary from the beat's own speakers/cast (never invented prose) — proven on synthetic data (a scene-opener beat and a 3-character beat both produced correct, sensible defaults). `beat_problems`/`cb_preflight.check_beat_creative` both BLOCK on a blank primary/secondary/economized, mirroring every other Layer-2 field's validation. `cb_craft.check_ensemble_individuation` was REFINED to read this field: a character the beat's own author marked `economized` is excused from the lexicon-trace requirement (that's the field doing its job, not a gap) — falling back to the original, coarser "check everyone mentioned" behaviour when a beat has no `fidelityAllocation` authored yet (never a regression for unmigrated data). Verified: identical output to the pre-existing check on the real, unmigrated production package (5 flags, byte-identical wording); a synthetic beat with two characters marked `economized` correctly suppressed the "nobody traces to their lexicon" flag while STILL correctly flagging a duplicate-verb pairing that included the beat's own PRIMARY character (an economized-economized pairing is fine by design; a primary sharing a generic verb with anyone is not).
    - Running `cb_preflight.py` against the real, live production package now shows 0/43 clean (a brand-new required field, not yet authored anywhere in the current package — the same, expected transitional state `humourLayer`/`emotionMechanic` were in before their own manifest-authoring pass). NOT backfilled tonight — building and proving the software was the ask; authoring 43 real beats' worth of `fidelityAllocation` is separate, substantial creative work, deliberately left for its own pass rather than rushed in as a side effect.

    A SERIOUS, UNRELATED BUG FOUND AND FIXED WHILE PROVING THIS LIVE (`cb_llm._openai_call`): the installed `openai` SDK (2.41.1) now requires each message's `content` as a list of typed parts (`[{"type":"input_text","text":...}]`), not a plain string — meaning the Director's OpenAI call was failing on EVERY invocation, a regression from whatever SDK/environment state let the earlier full-episode sandboxed fire (rule 47, "ran for real, 3403s, exit code 0") succeed. This is not a beat/schema/prompt bug — it would have blocked Gate 1 firing at all, for any episode, starting from whenever the SDK changed underneath the code. Confirmed the exact fix directly against the real API (a tiny dummy-schema `.parse()` call) before applying it, not guessed at; swept the whole engine for any other `.responses.parse`/`.create` call site using the old plain-string shape — `cb_llm._openai_call` is the ONLY one (the Gemini fallback uses a completely different REST shape, `parts` not `content`, unaffected).

    THE LIVE-FIRE PROOF, RUN AGAINST THE FIXED PIPELINE: a real, sandboxed fire (theme_lock -> episode_to_scenes -> scene_to_beats for Scene 2 only, a scratch OUT path, episode label `Ep1_FIDELITY_TEST` — never touching the real package or the real "Ep1" config keys) succeeded end to end. Scene 2 produced 2 beats, both with sensible, first-try-clean `fidelityAllocation`: 2.B1 (Aida's vision beat) -> `{"primary": "Aida", "secondary": "Keen's Mum", "economized": "Keen"}` — correctly naming Aida as the beat's real performer, Keen's Mum as the secondary figure inside the vision, and Keen (also present in the vision fragment) as deliberately economized; 2.B2 (a solo Aida beat) -> `{"primary": "Aida", "secondary": "none", "economized": "none"}` — correctly all-none for a genuinely solo beat. `beat_problems()` returned ZERO problems on both beats — no repair call needed, the Director's own prompt produced clean values on the first pass. No test residue found in `config/locations.json`/`continuity.json`/`episode_arc.json` (this test called the individual authoring functions directly, not the full `direct()` orchestrator that syncs those files, so nothing was ever written to them). Real production package confirmed byte-identical (MD5) before and after the entire session's testing.

51. THE FIRST LIVE FIRES + THE FIFTH ANCHOR RETIRED (Julian, 2026-07-07). Two real production events the same night, both acted on directly rather than left as findings:
    THE WING-TURNAROUND CONFLICT, FOUND AND FIXED: reviewing 1.B1's actual signed keyframe (rendered before this fix), Fuzzby's wings read symmetric/at-rest despite the LAUNCH-READY wing law (2026-07-03) explicitly asking for an asymmetric mid-downstroke pose. Root cause, confirmed by viewing the turnaround sheet directly: `CB_Fuzzby.jpeg` shows all four wings in a static, evenly-spread rest pose in every one of its four views — there is no asymmetric/flight reference anywhere in his canon assets. The compiled keyframe prompt then said "match the turnaround exactly" in the SAME sentence as "ASYMMETRIC," and a separate CLEAN BASE IDENTITY clause repeated "render EXACTLY as its turnaround reference shows it" immediately after — two competing "match the reference" signals (one a stronger reference IMAGE) pulling against the one asymmetry instruction, the same bug class as the rule-26 anti-hold fix. Fixed in `cb_prompts.py`'s `wings` clause: "match the turnaround" is now scoped to MATERIAL properties only (colour, translucency, vein pattern, edge quality); the turnaround's own resting pose is named explicitly as the thing NOT to copy, with an explicit precedence statement over the later clean-base line. Verified against the real 1.B1 fire: the re-rendered clip's QA sidecar came back clean — `PASS`, zero notes — with the `ANATOMY_DEFECT` flag the previous take carried gone entirely.
    1.B1 SIGNED, THEN A REAL RETAKE, DONE BY THE BOOK: Julian approved 1.B1's first real clip live in chat ("Beat One landed"), then — after actually reviewing it — corrected himself: the praised take was 1.B1, but the position-drift/physics/moustache problems he'd separately named belonged to 1.B2's existing footage, not 1.B1. Both approvals were corrected exactly as the doctrine intends: `cb_beats.record_approval` un-approved and re-approved 1.B1 (restoring the archived clip), and separately rejected 1.B2 with his stated correction, archiving it to `media/archive/Ep1_scene1_rejected/`. Root-caused 1.B2's actual defect rather than just re-firing: its OWN script had regressed during the earlier storyboard regeneration, losing the precise "body/wings/legs stay outside the petals" and "shaped like a handlebar moustache, not a full-face coating" language an earlier draft had — vague enough ("dives whole-face," "completely dusted... like a moustache") that nothing told the model to keep Fuzzby's body outside the flower or shape the pollen mark distinctly. Rewritten across every field carrying the description (cuts, endState/endStateStill, storyBeat, contact/physics rules, the legacy i2vPrompt) — sweep-the-pattern, rule 11 — verified clean at 399/400 words, 0 manifest BLOCKs.
    THE FIFTH ANCHOR, RETIRED: watching 1.B2's actual footage, Julian ruled directly against rule 26's @Video1 mechanism (2026-07-04, added on fal's own field guidance that a video reference keeps motion/audio context better than a still-frame chain) — "I don't think we should use approved... nearby within the same shot... the video I don't like it either, I think it confuses things... ref 1 which is the final best frame shot, the character references, then the audio." Two motion/position signals (a still frame AND a video clip) read as confusing on the actual render, not clarifying. REMOVED entirely, not narrowed: `cb_segprompt._v5_references` no longer emits an @Video1 line at all, opener or relay; the relay @图1 line dropped "approved" and "nearby within the same space" for a plainer replacement (see below). `cb_beats.py` no longer builds or uploads a trimmed video reference for any beat's fire (`_trim_video_tail`, now dead, deleted outright — this codebase's own no-dead-code discipline). Swept for contradiction the same session (rule 12's discipline, applied again): `cb_qa.check_gate3_lint`'s structural-congruence check and `cb_golden.py`'s content assertions both hard-required the old wording and the presence of @Video1 in relay mode — both updated in the same pass, or every relay beat would have failed the lint with a fake BLOCK the moment the emitter changed. `GATE3_ANIMATION_DOCTRINE.md`'s §4b worked example and its opener/relay reference-stack description updated to match. Verified: `beat_preview.py` (the Studio's own `/api/beat-prompt` endpoint) recompiles clean, `READY_TO_RENDER`, zero hard fails; `cb_preflight.py` shows 0 BLOCK across all 5 Scene 1 beats.
    SAME DAY, SECOND PASS — PROVENANCE VS. JOB: the first pass's own replacement wording, "begin from this, the final frame of the previous shot," was itself flagged the same day — Julian, watching the actual behaviour: "I think what it does is it picks up the final frame and actually puts the first frame as the last frame... we just need to say we want you to start from this frame... we don't have to mention the final shot... it doesn't know the final shot anyway." The phrase named the reference image's PROVENANCE (that it came from a prior shot's ending) rather than its JOB (where THIS shot starts) — the exact self-referential-phrase failure mode rules 26/27 already diagnosed, just with a fresh instance ("final frame of the previous shot" read as a claim about this shot's own structure rather than a description of the past). Simplified to name the job only: `"@图1 — start from this frame; carry the characters, their marks and the light; open on a fresh camera setup."` No "final," no "previous shot," no history the model can't verify anyway. Same sweep discipline applied again: `cb_qa.check_gate3_lint`, `cb_golden.py`'s relay assertion, and `GATE3_ANIMATION_DOCTRINE.md`'s §4b all updated in the same pass. Verified: `READY_TO_RENDER`, zero hard fails, 0 manifest BLOCKs, unchanged.
    SAME DAY, THIRD PASS — DROPPING THE REDUNDANT CAMERA CLAUSE: Julian questioned "open on a fresh camera setup" itself. Checked directly against `seedance-prompt-engine.jsx` (the reference tool he built, mined earlier the same session, rule 50) rather than guessing: its own CONTINUATION-mode doctrine keeps the continuity clause and the camera clause as two entirely separate slots in the Director Formula (Subject/Action/Scene/**Camera**/Lighting/Audio/Constraints) — camera direction never appears inside the continuation-state clause at all, it's driven by whatever the shot's own action needs. Our shot list already carries that per-shot camera direction (each Shot's own framing text) — stating "fresh camera setup" a second time in the reference block was pure duplication. Final line: `"@图1 — start from this frame; carry the characters, their marks and the light, matched exactly as the opening frame."` — an identity/state instruction only, camera left entirely to the shot list. `cb_qa.check_gate3_lint`'s and `cb_golden.py`'s congruence checks needed no change (their substring match, `"@图1 — start from this frame"`, is a prefix of both the old and new endings); `GATE3_ANIMATION_DOCTRINE.md`'s §4b updated to match.
    THE ADVERSARIAL CHECK ON JUNCTION TYPE, SAME NIGHT: Julian separately surfaced a different-source example prompt (labelled "S01_B02," not our own beat-code convention) written in pure `seamless_continuation` style — "CONTINUITY FRAME IS TRUTH. COPY EXACTLY... Begin from this exact frozen instant: same camera, framing, character positions, poses... Continue naturally with no reset, re-entry, repositioning or time jump" — and asked whether it made sense for 1.B2. Run through a 3-agent adversarial workflow (two independent reads against rules 26/31 verbatim from this file plus 1.B2's actual `junctionType` field, one deliberately arguing the prompt was safe): both converged — it is NOT safe for 1.B2. 1.B2's own `junctionType` is `intentional_next_shot`; the shown prompt uses the opposite instruction set (hold/repeat the exact pose) with none of rule 26's required anti-hold counter-instruction, and would very likely reproduce the documented bug (Fuzzby frozen in his opening pose for the full 15s). Confirmed against the actual story data too: 1.B1 ends on a settle/button; 1.B2's own `opensOn` ("Fuzzby... rocketing toward the next tall flower, humming louder") and `startState` ("chest still proud from the previous button") both describe a NEW gag arc already in motion, not a resumption of the identical instant — exactly what `intentional_next_shot` is for. The frozen-instant style stays reserved for a beat someone explicitly declares `seamless_continuation` (a real, rare case — an unbroken take spanning a beat boundary), never applied by default.

52. JULIAN'S FIVE-POINT REVISION TO THE RELAY PROMPT, AFTER THE ADVERSARIAL PUSHBACK (2026-07-07): responding directly to rule 51's own S01_B02 finding and to a first-principles Law-6 objection raised in chat, Julian gave five explicit, worded decisions rather than either simply accepting or overriding the pushback blind — a worked compromise, not a reversal.
    DECISION 1 — THE ANTI-HOLD-SAFE RELAY WORDING, SUPERSEDING "start from this frame": rather than avoid naming @图1's provenance (rule 51's second/third passes), Julian re-added it — "@图1 is the approved final frame of the previous beat and must be matched exactly as the first frame only" — but this time paired with an explicit "first frame ONLY" qualifier and a direct anti-hold counter-instruction ("Do not hold the previous pose, replay the previous action, reset the characters or introduce unexplained repositioning"), addressing the exact risk the S01_B02 adversarial check flagged head-on rather than by omission. `cb_segprompt._v5_references` rewritten; `GATE3_ANIMATION_DOCTRINE.md` §4b, `cb_qa.check_gate3_lint`'s structural-congruence check (now also requiring "Do not hold the previous pose" and exempting the references block from the negation lint, since this negation is now lawful/deliberate) and `cb_golden.py`'s relay assertion all updated in the same pass.
    DECISION 1b/3 — TWO NEW OPTIONAL LAYER-2 FIELDS: `beat.relayOpeningNote` (who starts where, what breaks first — 1.B2's is Julian's own dictated text) and `beat.spatialAxis` (a fixed blocking law — lanes, "never swap sides") are appended to the references block when authored, mechanically, no new code branch per beat. Only 1.B2 carries them today.
    DECISION 2 — THE DELIVERY LAW: `_v5_cut_speaker_note` rewritten so a cut with an authored `delivery` field renders "{Name} performs {his/her} vocal beat from @Audio1 {delivery}." instead of a bare "{Name} speaks." — Law 6 (no spoken words, un-reopenable per rules 4/28/29) stays fully intact: `delivery` is acting direction (tone/intent), never quoted words, and `_strip_spoken_words` still runs on it as defense in depth. A new `_v5_possessive(name)` helper reads `characters.json`'s existing `gender` field for his/her/their. 1.B2's cuts 2/3 `delivery` text rewritten to Julian's own fuller phrasing so the mechanical template reads exactly as his worked examples.
    DECISION 4 — STYLE LAW LEANED, ATMOSPHERE MOVED TO THE SCENE: `laws/style.txt` trimmed to Julian's literal replacement text (dropping "warm golden hour sunlight," "glowing magical particles," the hyper-saturation language). A new OPTIONAL scene-level field, `sceneLook`, holds the moved atmosphere content (Scene 1's: warm morning light, pollen, meadow appearance) and is appended as a second sentence inside BLOCK 1 by a new `_v5_scene_look(scene)` — joined with a space, never a new `"\n\n"` block, so `cb_qa.check_gate3_lint`'s block-index model (rule 46's own "stale block-index" fix) stays unchanged. Distinct from the scene's pre-existing `look` field (the establishing-plate composition text `cb_seedance.py`'s older validator reads separately, rule 10) — not touched.
    DECISION 5 — WORD BUDGET: the one genuine "repeated technical boilerplate" found and cut was "16:9" appearing twice (HEADER and the tech line) — `_v5_tech_line()` now omits it. No standing negative was found to be redundant with another; none were touched (all eleven stay, per rules 43/44's "nothing here is dropped").
    THE BUDGET CONFLICT, MEASURED AND FLAGGED, NOT SILENTLY RESOLVED: decision 1's wording alone costs +39 words over what it replaced, on every relay beat, before any beat-specific content. Measured live against the real package: 1.B1 (opener — unaffected by decisions 1/2/3, only by decision 4's scene-look sentence) is 431 words, up from 409 without it — and 409 was ALREADY over the 400 cap before tonight, a pre-existing condition from rule 45's own shot-list restoration ("beats will legitimately run into the 500s-600s"). 1.B2 (relay, carrying its own `relayOpeningNote` + `spatialAxis` + two delivery-law rewrites) is 572. `cb_preflight.py`'s 400-word hard BLOCK fires on both. Decision 5's own scope (boilerplate + redundant negatives) does not cover a gap this size — closing it would mean cutting the spatial-continuity/performance-direction content decision 5 explicitly protected, which this session did NOT do unilaterally. Held for Julian's direction rather than force-fit; see the session record for the options put to him (trim the relayOpeningNote/spatialAxis overlap, which do restate the same lane assignment from two angles; or treat 400 as a target/flag rather than a hard BLOCK, matching how rule 45's own shot-list ruling already reasoned about real per-shot content outweighing a flat cap).
    GATE3_ANIMATION_DOCTRINE.md's §2 (Budgets note), §3 (Fidelity table — three new source rows) and §4b (worked example + full history) all updated in the same pass; `cb_qa.check_gate3_lint` and `cb_golden.py` both updated and re-verified structurally (lint failures now ONLY the pre-existing/new word-budget BLOCKs on 1.B1/1.B2 — every other check passes clean on all 5 beats).

    THE BUDGET RESOLVED, SAME SESSION (Julian, delegating the call directly — "what do you tink"): two changes, both applied. ONE — `1.B2`'s `relayOpeningNote` trimmed to drop its own restatement of the lane assignment `spatialAxis` already states (both fields were saying "Fuzzby begins frame-left, Zenny begins frame-right" from two angles) — real, content-preserving deduplication, not a cut of anything decision 5 protected; 1.B2 now compiles at 556 words, down from 572. Dated historical entries elsewhere in this file describing PAST word counts under the old cap (rules 42/48's own worked totals) were deliberately left untouched — they are accurate records of what was true when written, not live documentation. Verified: `cb_preflight.py`'s Scene-1 word-count BLOCKs cleared for 1.B3/1.B4/1.B5 (379/355/367, all now comfortably under target); 1.B1 (431) and 1.B2 (556) sit under the new hard cap but still above the new 400-word target, correctly surfaced as FLAGs, not BLOCKs — 1.B1's own overage is pre-existing (rule 45), not something this session introduced or is asked to fix tonight.
53. THE DELIVERY LAW GOES SOFTWARE-WIDE (Julian's ruling, 2026-07-08 — "if we're going to do some of these changes that we've just done, we need to be able to bolt them into the law as well. This needs to be software-wide."): rule 52's five decisions lived only in 1.B2's hand-authored JSON and the emitter. This rule closes that gap at the SOURCE — the Director's own Pydantic schema (`cb_director_schemas.py`) and authoring prompt (`cb_director.py`'s `episode_to_scenes`/`scene_to_beats`) — so every future Gate-1 fire, this episode or any other, natively produces the fields decision 1-4 introduced, matching rule 47's exact precedent ("the stuff that you've done needs to be written into some form of script or code").

    THE SURPRISE FINDING, FIRST: a research pass (before any code was touched) found `delivery` was ALREADY a schema field (`Cut.delivery: Optional[str]`) and ALREADY in `scene_to_beats`'s authoring prompt — and a live package audit found all 84 dialogue cuts across the whole 43-beat episode ALREADY had it authored, not just 1.B2's two cuts as assumed. The real gap was never the DATA — it was that `cb_segprompt._v5_cut_speaker_note`, before rule 52's fix, silently discarded it and shipped every cut as a bare "{Name} speaks." regardless of what was authored. This means rule 52's emitter fix was ALREADY fully software-wide the moment it landed — confirmed live by spot-checking three untouched beats in three different scenes (3.B3, 7.B1, 9.B2), each correctly rendering its own already-authored delivery text (e.g. Keen's Mum: "hushed, reverent, with a tiny catch on father's wristbands") the instant its prompt was next compiled. Nothing to build there; only `Cut.delivery`'s prompt instruction was strengthened with Julian's own two worked examples as the literal quality bar, plus a new hard BLOCK (`beat_problems`, `cb_preflight.check_beat_technical`) catching a spoken cut with a blank delivery — a gap that existed even before tonight.

    A REAL, LIVE CONTENT GAP FOUND AND CLOSED THE SAME NIGHT: `sceneLook` (decision 4's scene-atmosphere field) had NO schema/prompt presence at all — only Scene 1 had one, hand-authored the same night as decision 4. Checking a Scene-3/7 beat's actual compiled prompt confirmed the real cost: since the universal style law was leaned the same session (dropping "warm golden hour sunlight," pollen, etc.), every scene from 2 through 10 was shipping its beats with ZERO atmosphere language at all — a regression decision 4 introduced without anyone noticing it wasn't just a Scene-1 change. Fixed two ways: (1) `Scene.sceneLook: str = Field(min_length=1)`, same required pattern as `ambientBed`/`parentLine`, added to the schema and `episode_to_scenes`'s prompt, so every future scene authors one; (2) Scenes 2-10 backfilled THE SAME NIGHT, not left as a future-only fix — derived MECHANICALLY from each scene's own already-authored `lighting` field (THE FIDELITY LAW: never invented, only condensed/trimmed to one clean sentence), never hand-composed fresh prose. Verified live: 7.B1's shipped prompt now reads "Cold underwater blue-grey and storm shadow, pierced by the distant beacon above and the first inner aqua glow from Keen's vacant cuffs" where it previously had nothing; `cb_preflight.py` shows all 10 scenes' `sceneLook` present, zero new word-budget BLOCKs introduced by the backfill.

    `relayOpeningNote`/`spatialAxis` (decisions 1b/3) added to `Beat` as genuinely OPTIONAL, nullable fields (`Optional[str] = None`, the same pattern as `junctionType`/`opensOn`) plus matching prompt instructions — most beats correctly leave these null; no mechanical default is invented for either, since (unlike `junctionType`) there is no safe generic value to fall back to for a genuinely creative, situational field.

    LIVE-FIRE PROOF (matching rule 47's exact sandboxing protocol — scratch OUT dir, `Ep1_SCHEMA_TEST_53` episode label, MD5-verified before/after): a real, billed Gate-1 fire against Scene 2 (the smallest scene, same one rule 47's own proof used) produced a clean `beat_problems()` result — zero problems, zero manual correction — with the LLM producing, unprompted beyond the new schema/prompt text: a real `sceneLook` ("soft morning gold over the cove sanctuary, rose-quartz light breathing gently across stone and water"), a real `relayOpeningNote`/`spatialAxis` pair on 2.B2 (the beat with a predecessor) correctly null on 2.B1 (the opener), and a delivery note ("with soft, private certainty, letting the paw over her heart carry the weight of what she has seen") that hits the same tone-plus-physical-behaviour bar as Julian's own worked examples. Production package MD5 confirmed byte-identical before and after (`d385daf0b017628d7e16aa18cd9b71a4`); no config-file cleanup was needed since calling `episode_to_scenes`/`scene_to_beats` directly (not the full `direct()` orchestrator) never reaches the config-merge step at all.

    THE CONTRADICTION SWEEP (rule 49's standing discipline, applied again): found one real, confirmed regression — `test_gate_cascade.py`'s scratch fixtures (`_scratch_scenes`, missing `sceneLook`; `_manifest_compliant_beat`, missing `fidelityAllocation` — the latter pre-existing, not caused by tonight, but compounding the same crash) now made `cb_pipeline.approve()` silently refuse inside the test, crashing it with a raw `KeyError` instead of reporting pass/fail — the same class of test-fixture drift rule 46 already fixed once for this exact file. Fixed both gaps; `python3 test_gate_cascade.py` now runs clean end to end again: "CASCADE ASSERTION PASSED." Everything else checked clean: `cb_qa.check_gate3_lint` and `cb_golden.py` both read scene/beat as plain dicts via `.get(...)` throughout (no `KeyError` risk, citation map already correct), `cb-studio/serve.py`/`app.html` don't enumerate beat/scene fields anywhere (nothing newly hidden — though this also means the whole manifest layer, old and new, is currently invisible in the Studio UI, named here as a pre-existing gap, not a regression from tonight — SUPERSEDED 2026-07-08, same day: rule 55's software-wide audit built exactly this UI, see rule 55's "confirmed clean" list; this sentence is kept as the dated record of the gap that prompted that build, not a description of the current state), `MANIFEST.md`/`GATE3_ANIMATION_DOCTRINE.md` both updated in the same pass to cite the schema as the source of truth rather than describing these as one-off, hand-authored 1.B2 fields.

54. THE TECH-LINE RETIREMENT + THE SHOT-TIMING LAW (Julian's ruling, 2026-07-08, reviewing 1.B2's compiled prompt a second time — "This is a proper production prompt... 9/10, with three final adjustments"): a full worked rewrite, three named risks, one genuine convergent finding, and one thing NOT applied without his explicit call.
    THE CONVERGENCE: Julian's own rewrite dropped the standalone tech line ("smooth cinematic motion, shallow depth of field") and folded "24fps" into the HEADER — independently arriving at the exact fix this session's own `cb_qa.ANTI_SLOP_WORDS` check (rule 50) had already flagged that phrase for on every single beat. `cb_segprompt._v5_header()` now returns `"{HANDLE_TOTAL}s, 16:9, 24fps, 3D CGI beat."`; the standalone tech line is deleted outright (`_v5_tech_line` retired), `emit_v5`'s parts list drops from 7 to 6. Swept everywhere the block-index model or the tech line's own text was checked, matching the exact discipline rule 46 established for the same bug class before: `cb_qa.check_gate3_lint`'s `tech_idx`/self-check replaced with a part-count check (6, not 7), `cb_golden.py`'s HEADER/tech-line assertions updated to read `_v5_header()` directly. The `cinematic` anti-slop flag still fires — now from the style law's own "cinematic lighting," a second locked-constant source, not the retired tech line.
    THE SHOT-TIMING LAW: Julian's third named risk — "four shots in 15 seconds need time ranges, otherwise Seedance may spend too long on the flower entry and rush or omit the wipe" — closed as a real, software-wide mechanism, not a one-beat patch. `cb_segprompt._v5_shot_time_ranges(n)` divides `HANDLE_TOTAL` across N cuts via cumulative `round()` (round-half-to-even), no per-cut authored weight needed; verified it reproduces Julian's own hand-computed boundaries for 4 cuts/15s exactly (0-4, 4-8, 8-11, 11-15). Each shot in Block 4 is now two lines — `"{start}-{end}s - {framing}:"` then the action/speaker-note line — replacing the old single-line `"Shot N (framing): action."` form; `"Shot N"` numbering is DROPPED (the same "already the number, don't double-count" logic rule 45 applied to the old numeral now applies to the time range instead). This mechanically revives what the v4 template's `"Timing: 0-Ns...; N-Ms..."` clock (rule 30) used to do via the now-deleted `_v3_shots` — lost when the shot list was restored to full per-cut detail (rule 45) without carrying the per-shot time budget along with it.
    THE NEGATIVES: Julian's own worked example prefixed every gag-specific negative with "No" to match the standing items' own cadence — `_v5_negative_line` now mechanically prefixes any `stagingProhibited` item with "no " if it doesn't already start with a negation word (`_NEGATION_LEAD_RE`), a pure formatting normalisation, never a content change. 1.B2's own `stagingProhibited` authored with the 5 gag-specific items Julian named (no Fuzzby disappearing into the flower, no full-body entry into the petals, no full-face pollen coating, no clean face after the wipe, no removal of the moustache or goatee) — using the EXISTING per-beat mechanism (`_v5_negative_line` already puts `stagingProhibited` first, ahead of the 11 standing items), no new field needed. Julian's own pasted example had trimmed 3 of the 11 standing negatives (floating/sinking, body inflation, full-body deflation) — NOT adopted; those stay locked per rules 43/44's "always exactly eleven, nothing here is dropped." Also applied to 1.B2 directly (data, not code): `relayOpeningNote`/`spatialAxis` re-split so the two fields read as one flowing paragraph (his own merge of what were two disconnected one-liners); `cuts[0].framing` dropped "camera barrels" for "fast, readable follow" (his critique point 1 — an unstable-chase risk to the flower gag); `cuts[1].action` gained the "legs" detail (already stated elsewhere in `contact_rule`/`physics_rule`, now consistent in the shot text too) and clearer moustache/goatee geography; both dialogue cuts' `delivery` text adopted Julian's own refined phrasing.
    FLAGGED, NOT APPLIED: Julian's rewrite of Block 3 (Acting DNA) — "Fuzzby is manic, staccato and hyper: fast, chaotic, pompous and fully committed until the moment goes wrong" — reads more smoothly as prose than the current "bumping into things... commits 100% then crashes," but Block 3 is THE FIDELITY LAW's own locked, verbatim-quoted field (Fuzzby's `cadence` in `characters.json`), not per-beat authored text — rewriting it in the compiled prompt would silently fork the shipped text from the character's own real store, exactly the drift rule 28's ACTINGDNA RETRACTION already ruled against once. Also flagged: his rewrite specifically drops "bumping into things" (a concrete, physical, filmable detail) for "the moment goes wrong" (a vaguer abstraction) — the opposite direction from rule 33's own adjective-chaos ban ("name the physical beat, never a generic frenzy word").
