Not changed pending Julian's own decision: if he wants this phrasing, it is a `characters.json` edit at the source (affecting every beat Fuzzby/Zenny appear in), never a per-beat prompt patch.
    Verified across all 5 Scene 1 beats: `check_gate3_lint` clean on all 5 (no new blockers); `cb_golden.py assertions()` 0 failures; `cb_preflight.py` shows no new BLOCK categories (word-count flags actually improved slightly: 1.B1 431->424, the tech-line removal saving a few words even before any content addition); `test_gate_cascade.py` still passes end to end. `_v5_shot_time_ranges` spot-checked for 2/3/4/5-cut beats, all producing sane, evenly-distributed boundaries.

55. THE FULL SIGN-OFF AUDIT (Julian, 2026-07-08 — "go and get all of the information that you need to ensure that we sign this software off now"): the most exhaustive law-to-code + prompt-craft certification pass run against this codebase to date. Method: 9 read-only subsystem audits (Gate 1 schema/authoring, the Faithful Director/dialogue-verbatim gate, the Character Vocabulary Law, the v5 emitter's shape and content laws, `cb_qa.check_gate3_lint`, `cb_preflight` completeness and gate-ordering, the Studio UI, cross-document consistency, Gate 2/4/5) plus 2 external adversarial craft reviews that loaded the seedance-20 skill's own reference doctrine (capability map, allocation model, anti-slop lexicon, multishot grammar, reference-role assignment) and scored our ACTUAL compiled 1.B2 Seedance prompt and 1.B1 keyframe prompt against it — not a hypothetical, the real text each builder ships today. Every claimed gap then went through an independent second-pass verifier before being trusted (one of eight died to a transient rate limit on the first run; the whole 11-agent audit was cleanly re-run in full rather than patched around the gap).

    WHAT CAME BACK CLEAN (confirmed, not assumed): the whole THE MANIFEST LAYER IS NOW AUTHORED BY GATE 1 ITSELF chain (rule 47/53) — every one of `junctionType`/`opensOn`/`carryMarks`/`endState`/`endStateStill`/`actingContrast`/`humourLayer`/`emotionMechanic`/`fidelityAllocation`/`relayOpeningNote`/`spatialAxis`/`delivery`/`ambientBed`/`parentLine`/`sceneLook` is a real, live Pydantic field with real authoring-prompt guidance and `_finalize_beat_manifest_fields` defensive fallback at BOTH its call sites (initial draft and post-repair); the Faithful Director's `enforce_verbatim` plus `cb_preflight.check_scene_dialogue_verbatim`'s LCS-based standing re-check; the full Character Vocabulary Law chain (all 11 characters' `lexicon`, the CAST LOCK, Law 14, zero live violations across the whole 43-beat package); the v5 emitter's 6-block shape, the tech-line retirement, the shot-timing law, the 11-standing-negatives-always mechanism, the anti-hold-safe @图1 wording, @Video1's total absence from live code; `manifest_ok`'s choke-point on every one of `build_beat`/`gen_audio`/`render_beat`/`approve_beat`/`rebuild` plus the exit-code discipline from every `__main__` dispatch; the new beat/scene Manifest-Layer UI built this session, verified consistent end to end; every cross-document word-budget/negative-count/@Video1 claim, confirmed consistent (one apparent PRODUCTION_DOCTRINE.md contradiction on block count turned out to already self-flag as superseded with a pointer to the authoritative doc — not a live disagreement); the wing/turnaround-conflict fix (rule 51) and the Crystal World Rule's plate-QA wiring, both confirmed present and correct in `cb_prompts.py`; Gate 4/5's try/except safety net, still present.

    SIX REAL FINDINGS, FIXED THE SAME SESSION:
    - **THE APPEARANCE-LEAK VIOLATION (the most severe finding of the whole audit)**: `cb_prompts.py`'s keyframe builder — a SEPARATE module from the v5 video-prompt engine — was injecting literal physical-appearance prose ("Keep Fuzzby's signature round wire-frame glasses," "Signature features are NON-NEGOTIABLE: Fuzzby keeps round wire-frame glasses; Zenny keeps round glasses and rosy blush cheeks") into every single compiled keyframe prompt, a direct, structural violation of rule 5 ("no physical/appearance text anywhere, ever... what stays absolutely forbidden is DESCRIBING the character") — and contradicting this very module's own docstring ("character REFERENCE-ONLY, never described"). `cb_qa.check_gate3_lint` already guards the SIBLING v5 video-prompt engine against exactly this leak (its item 4, "appearance-prose leak") — the sweep that fixed it there never reached this older module. Fixed: `_markers()`'s injection and the `keeps`-built "Signature features are NON-NEGOTIABLE" line are deleted outright (not merely unused); identity/accessory consistency is carried, losslessly, by the existing "Maintain this character's exact identity from the reference... every accessory exactly as shown" reference-anchored sentence alone. `avoids` (the "wears NO pendant/necklace/medallion/crystal" line) is KEPT deliberately — a prohibition on invention, not a description of actual appearance, matching the standing "Negative:"-style pattern used everywhere else in this pipeline. Verified live: a real compiled 1.B1 keyframe prompt now carries zero appearance-description leak; golden diff shows exactly this change and nothing else — shown to Julian, not auto-blessed, per the standing rule.
    - **THE ORPHANED CAMERA-LOCK LAW**: `cb_qa.check_camera_lock_conflict` (Law 8, rule 28/38's camera-locked-during-dialogue rule) was built 2026-07-02, correctly detects a dialogue cut whose own framing names camera movement, but was never re-wired into `check_gate3_lint` when the twelve-law lint it used to live under was retired and replaced — a total enforcement gap the audit's own compiled-1.B2 craft review independently rediscovered from the outside ("no general rule is stated anywhere in the prompt text... only implicit, shot-by-shot, and inconsistently honored"). Re-wired as a hard BLOCK (item 1.6), matching the severity of every other doctrine-backed check in the same function. Its keyword-based heuristic (`_CAMERA_MOVE_WORDS`) does not currently catch 1.B2's own "locked-on-the-crash reveal" framing (the word "reveal" isn't in the list) — a known, secondary heuristic-tuning question, named here rather than silently patched, since narrowing/widening a keyword list is exactly the kind of judgment call rule 45 already ruled should never be made silently.
    - **THE BASELINE-PROOF RELAY BUG**: `cb_segprompt.py`'s `__main__` — the exact command CLAUDE.md's own "Baseline proof" block names as the standard way to inspect any beat's real shipped prompt — called `shipped_prompt(beat, scene)` with `relay` defaulted to `False`, never computed from the beat's actual predecessor state the way `cb_beats.run`/`gate3_dryrun`/`fire_next_beat` all correctly do via `cb_scene.relay_source_for`. This meant the one command this project's own doctrine tells everyone to run would silently show the WRONG (opener) prompt branch for any relay beat with a signed predecessor clip — with no warning. Fixed: the CLI now computes relay status identically to the real fire path. Verified live: 1.B1 correctly shows `relay=False`; 1.B2 now correctly shows `relay=True` with the real anti-hold-safe relay text, where it previously would have silently shown the opener template.
    - **`stagingProhibited` HAD NO SCHEMA FIELD**: real, load-bearing data (`cb_segprompt._v5_negative_line` merges it into every shipped Negative line; `cb_preflight` already checked its well-formedness) with no corresponding field anywhere in `cb_director_schemas.Beat` — meaning Gate 1's own LLM authoring pass could never natively produce it, the exact class of gap rule 47 closed for every other manifest field. Closed the same way: `Beat.stagingProhibited: Optional[List[str]] = None` added, plus matching Director authoring-prompt guidance (`cb_director.scene_to_beats`).
    - **A stale self-contradicting comment** in `cb_qa.py`'s negation-lint (claimed "header, references" are both checked; `ref_idx` was already in the exempt set, so only HEADER ever is) — corrected to describe what the code actually does; the logic itself was never wrong, only the prose describing it.
    - **Two stale LAB_BACKLOG.md items** — item 3 (an @Video motion-reference proposal) and item 9 (revisit the prose/JSON emitter fork) both describe mechanisms that were since built, tried against real footage, and explicitly deleted/retired (rules 26/51 and the v3/v4 purge respectively) — both annotated MOOT with a pointer to the rule that superseded them, so a future reader doesn't re-propose and re-build something already tried and killed.

    FLAGGED FOR JULIAN'S OWN JUDGMENT, NOT RESOLVED UNILATERALLY:
    - **`studio2/studio.py`**: a separate, self-contained prototype module (its own `canon.yaml`/`studio.py`/`scene.html`, dated 2026-07-06) carries a live, currently-enforced `WORD_CAP, TARGET, STORY_CAP = 280, 250, 80` — a second, contradicting word-budget regime sitting in the repo, never imported by `engine/` or `cb-studio/` so it cannot affect the live pipeline, but a real second-source-of-truth risk this session's own "swept everywhere" word-budget claims (rule 52) didn't account for. Left untouched — deleting or archiving someone else's parallel prototype work is not mine to decide unilaterally.
    - **1.B2's own fidelity-allocation content**: the audit's craft review scored this beat's actual `fidelityAllocation` (`{"primary": "Fuzzby", "secondary": "Zenny", "economized": "none"}`) against the allocation-model doctrine and found it asks Seedance to hold locked dual-character identity, a fragile persisting facial detail, committed physical motion, AND two separate spoken-dialogue performances all in one 15s call with nothing deliberately economized — close to the allocation model's own named anti-pattern. This is authored beat content, not a code defect; changing it is a directing call, not mine to make.
    - **The ~28-beat cast-size word-count backlog** (rule 46's own already-named, already-deferred item — 7 beats flagged there; the live count moves as authoring continues, and `cb_preflight.py` currently reports 14 beats over the word-count TARGET, none of them BLOCKs since rule 52 raised the hard cap to 650/400) and the **38-beat `fidelityAllocation` backfill gap outside Scene 1** (confirmed still the dominant BLOCK driver in `cb_preflight`'s own totals, 38 of 46) are unchanged by this audit — both pre-existing, already-documented, and explicitly Julian's own call per rules 46/50.
    CLOSED 2026-07-08 (later the same session, task #223 in the tracker): the 38-beat `fidelityAllocation` backfill described above was completed — all 43 beats now have primary/secondary/economized populated (verified: 0 beats missing `fidelityAllocation.primary`; `cb_preflight.py`'s BLOCK total is 8, none fidelityAllocation-related, all 8 dialogue-verbatim drift). This bullet is kept as the dated record of the gap as it stood when rule 55 was written, not a description of the current state.

    THE RESERVED VERDICT, RESTATED (rule 28): this audit certifies the SOFTWARE — the code, the schema, the gates, the docs — against every doctrine this project has built. It does not and cannot certify "is it funny, does it fly, would a four-year-old watch it again" — that stays Julian's own reserved verdict, exactly as rule 28 already states, and no check built or re-verified tonight was aimed at approximating it.

    Verified after every fix: all five touched Python files (`cb_prompts.py`, `cb_qa.py`, `cb_director_schemas.py`, `cb_director.py`, `cb_segprompt.py`) compile clean; `cb_preflight.py`'s totals are unchanged (46 BLOCK/62 FLAG/51 STRUCTURAL — every new field/check added was additive or optional, introducing zero new blocks against the current package); `test_gate_cascade.py` passes; `cb_golden.py diff` shows exactly the appearance-leak fix and nothing else, surfaced to Julian per the standing rule, not auto-blessed.

56. THE CONTRADICTION, OVERWRITE AND LEAK SWEEP (Julian, 2026-07-08 — "make sure there's nothing contradicting each other... is there anything in there that contradicts itself and overwrites and leaks"): a second, dedicated audit run immediately after rule 55's sign-off pass, aimed specifically at cross-rule contradictions, silent overwrites and content-scope leaks — the class of bug rule 55's own subsystem-by-subsystem audit wasn't structured to find. Method: 6 read-only investigations (CLAUDE.md's own internal consistency across all ~55 rules, code-level duplication of values/logic that could drift, a leak sweep across every prompt builder beyond the two already checked, silent-overwrite risk in config merges/archiving, the documentation supersession chain, and the new Studio UI's field-name consistency), each finding independently re-verified before being trusted. 21 findings raised, ALL 21 confirmed real — the cleanest hit rate of any audit this project has run, and the biggest single haul of genuine bugs found in one pass.

    FIXED THE SAME SESSION:
    - **CLAUDE.md's own internal contradictions**: rule 15's PENDING CORRECTION note never updated to say rule 21 resolved it two days later — corrected to state RESOLVED with the full chain. Rule 9 (no continuity-tail chaining without a fresh ruling) flatly contradicted by the whole carryMarks/relay-chain mechanism rules 15/21/26/27/31/33/36 built — reconciled: carryMarks IS the fresh ruling rule 9 itself required, never named as such. Rules 25/28 (plus three more sites) still said "six standing negatives," describing the deleted `_v3_negatives` mechanism — the live code has been an unconditional eleven since rules 43/44; corrected everywhere, matching the identical fix MANIFEST.md already got. Rule 13 still described SCENE1_DIRECTORS_CUT.md as an eleven-law repo-root document — it moved to an archive folder in the 2026-07-05 reset and has grown to fourteen laws in `cb_director.py`'s own live system prompt; corrected. Rule 53's own closing note (this same night) claimed the Manifest layer is "invisible in the Studio UI" — directly contradicted two rules later by rule 55's own "confirmed clean" list, since rule 55 built exactly that UI; annotated as superseded same-day.
    - **THE APPEARANCE-LEAK VIOLATION IN THE SKILL DOCUMENTS**: `skills/crystal-bears-post/SKILL.md` and `skills/crystal-bears-camera/SKILL.md` — the actual documents Claude Code loads when invoking those departments — both still contained large, un-struck sections instructing the exact forbidden post-generation voice-swap pipeline (strip native audio → ElevenLabs Voice Changer → re-voice), directly contradicting each file's own later, correctly-doctrined closing section and CLAUDE.md rules 4/29. A reader landing on an early section directly (by grep, by section order, or by stopping before the end) would be told to build the forbidden mechanism. Fixed: an inline `⛔ RETIRED` banner added at the top of every one of the six still-live-reading sections (three per file) pointing at the correct doctrine, rather than relying on a single correction buried at the end of a 200+/400+-line document.
    - **`cb_gen.py`'s live, CLI-invocable `voice_change()`/`lipsync()` functions**: directly implement the forbidden "the lip-sync voice swap" mechanism (rule 4/29's own words), reachable via `python3 cb_gen.py swap`/`lipsync`, unwired from any gate-arming path but real and callable. **FLAGGED, not removed** — `lipsync()`'s "drive a clip's mouth to a provided audio track" job is plausibly part of a legitimate future two-step fallback rule 29 itself allows ("the SECOND pass must be a generation pass... with @Audio1 actually driving that generation"); deleting or guarding either function without knowing whether Julian uses either as a standalone utility outside the render pipeline is not mine to decide unilaterally.
    - **`cb_preflight.check_beat_word_count`'s wrong relay proxy** (found ACTIVELY producing wrong output in the real preflight report, not just a latent risk): derived relay from a cheap `not is_scene_opener` check instead of `cb_scene.relay_source_for` (the real production logic every other call site uses) — a non-opener beat whose predecessor has no rendered clip yet is `no_predecessor_clip`, NOT a relay. This was flagging 1.B3/1.B5 (and 6 more beats across other scenes) as over the word-count target when their true, currently-fireable compile is under it. Fixed to compute relay identically to `cb_beats.run`; verified live — FLAG count on the real package dropped from 62 to 54, with the 8 corrected beats now reporting clean/STRUCTURAL.
    - **The Studio UI silently stripping `chorusMembers`/`voiceTreatment` on every save**: `ebSave()` rebuilds every cut from a 4/5-key-only template and `/api/beat-update` does a full-array replace, not a per-cut merge — so any beat with these two real, load-bearing fields (rule 38's hum/sing-song camera-lock exemption, chorus-voice routing) had them silently destroyed by an ordinary save through the beat editor, even one only touching an unrelated field like `storyBeat`. Fixed: both fields now round-trip silently through the editor (no UI control needed, just preserved). Verified live: saved an unrelated field, confirmed `voiceTreatment` survived intact.
    - **Four Manifest-Layer fields (`junctionType`/`relayOpeningNote`/`spatialAxis`) sent as JSON `null` when cleared**, but `serve.py`'s scalar-merge branch treats `null` as "don't touch" — clearing these fields in the beat editor silently no-op'd while still reporting `{"ok":true}`. Fixed for the three genuinely-optional string fields (send `''` instead, a safe cleared state); `humourLayer` deliberately left sending `null` when blank, since it is a REQUIRED int field in the schema with no valid "unset" state — refusing to blank it via the UI is correct protective behaviour, not a bug. Verified live: clearing `spatialAxis` on 1.B2 now actually clears it; restored afterward.
    - **`cb_pipeline.unapprove()`'s hand-duplicated `GATE_SEQ`**: a third, undocumented copy of the gate sequence (beyond the two cross-file copies the file's own header comment already warns about), hardcoded as a fresh local literal instead of referencing the module constant already used idiomatically two functions away. Fixed to reference `GATE_SEQ` directly.
    - **`cb_beats.record_approval`'s rejection-archive timestamp collision**: second-resolution-only, no monotonic counter — two rejections of the same beat landing in the same wall-clock second computed the identical archive path and the second `os.replace` silently overwrote the first rejection's clip AND its one-sentence correction, violating the function's own stated "archived, not deleted" guarantee. Fixed with a numeric-suffix disambiguator; verified live — two same-second rejections of 1.B2 now both survive intact.
    - **`cb_qa.py`'s stale character-lexicon cache**: read `cb_segprompt._CHARS`, a snapshot frozen at that OTHER module's import time and never refreshed — the only one of four consumers (`cb_director.py`/`cb_craft.py`/`cb_preflight.py` all reload fresh every call) silently risking enforcement against an out-of-date lexicon in any long-lived process. Fixed to call `cb_segprompt._load_chars()` (the same loader, called fresh) at both use sites.
    - **`cb_seedance.flatten_seedance_prompt`'s dialogue leak**: the review/debug full-export format (reachable via `SEEDANCE_PROMPT_FORMAT=full`, only for a beat with zero `cuts[]`) appended literal quoted dialogue verbatim — `compact_seedance_prompt` (the real shipping format) already strips this via `_timeline_line`; this sibling format never got the same fix. Applied the identical stripper to both.
    - **`cb_prompts.py`'s three fully-dead, dialogue-leaking functions** (`build_i2v_prompt`, `build_ref2vid_prompt`, `build_beat_prompt` — confirmed zero callers anywhere in `engine/`): each would leak literal spoken words the instant ever invoked, with no `RETIRED` guard the way `cb_prompts.seedance_json` already has for the same reason. Guarded with the identical raise-loud pattern (original design notes kept in the docstring, not deleted, since the whole body wasn't gutted the way `seedance_json`'s was — a smaller, lower-risk edit achieving the same "fails loud, never silently ships" guarantee). `group_beats` (also dead, zero callers) was left untouched — a harmless orphaned utility, not a leak risk in itself.
    - **`cb_gen.py`'s stale @Video1 docstring**: `generate_video_seedance_ref`'s `video_urls` parameter still described itself in present-tense, "additive" language a full day after every sibling module in the same dependency chain (`cb_beats.py`/`cb_qa.py`/`cb_golden.py`/`cb_segprompt.py`) got its own 2026-07-07 retirement note — the one gap the original sweep never reached. Corrected.

    FLAGGED, NOT FIXED — genuinely Julian's own call or too large a change to make unilaterally in this pass:
    - The Studio UI's nested-object fields (`opensOn`/`fidelityAllocation`/`continuity`/`performance`) can never be cleared back to blank once populated — the merge-only semantic (deliberately, never clobber an untouched sub-key) has no way to distinguish "the user left this blank" from "the user wants this cleared." A real gap, but fixing it means designing an explicit clear-sentinel, not a quick patch. **CORRECTED 2026-07-12 (full-codebase audit):** closed the same night this bullet was written — `/api/beat-update`/`/api/scene-update`'s merge logic (`cb-studio/serve.py`, ~lines 1241-1250/1285-1292) now excludes only `None` values from a nested sub-field update, not blank strings too; since the Studio UI's only caller (`app.html`'s `ebSave`) always sends the FULL current state of every sub-field on every save (never a partial patch), excluding just `None` is safe and lets a blank string actually clear a field. Distinct from the three SCALAR fields (`junctionType`/`relayOpeningNote`/`spatialAxis`) rule 56 already documents as fixed elsewhere in the same session — this is the nested-object case, closed separately.
    - `cb_prompts.build_keyframe_prompt`'s style string carries anti-slop words ("8K," "cinematic") and its per-character staging text has zero Character Vocabulary Law enforcement — both gaps exist because `cb_qa.check_gate3_lint` (the only place either check runs) only ever compiles and scans `cb_segprompt.shipped_prompt`'s video-generation text, never the keyframe/still-image path. Closing this properly means a new, keyframe-scoped lint function; not built this pass. **CORRECTED 2026-07-12 (full-codebase audit):** built since — `cb_qa.check_keyframe_lint(prompt, chars=None)` is a real, keyframe-scoped lint (`cb_qa.py:496`), wired into the actual keyframe-firing path at `cb_scene.py:451` (`cb_qa.check_keyframe_lint(prompt, chars=P.opening_cast(sh))`), closing exactly the gap this bullet named.
    - `cb_director_pass`'s LLM output feeding `cb_seedance.compact_seedance_prompt` has no code-level scan verifying the model actually honored its own "never write the spoken words" system-prompt instruction — mitigated by `compact_seedance_prompt` not being the primary live shipping path today (v5/`cb_segprompt` is), but a real gap if that ever changes. **CORRECTED 2026-07-12 (full-codebase audit):** a guard was added, on the CONSUMER side rather than inside `direct_beat` itself — `cb_seedance.py`'s compact-prompt assembly (`compact_seedance_prompt`, ~lines 1187-1194) now runs every Director's-Pass-derived field (`action_timeline`, `camera.movement`, `performance`) through `_strip_spoken_words` (imported from `cb_segprompt`) before it ships, the same defense-in-depth pattern the v5 engine already uses everywhere else for Law 6. So a future reader should look for this guard on the CONSUMER side, not expect it inside `direct_beat`'s own system prompt enforcement.
    - `cb_gen.py`'s `voice_change()`/`lipsync()` (see above) — deliberately left un-deleted and un-guarded pending Julian's own word on whether either has a standalone use outside the render pipeline. **CORRECTED 2026-07-12 (full-codebase audit):** true for `lipsync()` only, no longer true for `voice_change()` — task #227 retired it (`cb_gen.py:475`, docstring: "RETIRED (2026-07-08 software-wide fix batch): this IS the 'post voice swap' mechanism CLAUDE.md rules 4/29 forbid by name... zero production callers... kept for the record, raises [RuntimeError]"), matching `cb_prompts.py`'s identical RETIRED-guard precedent for its own dead functions. `lipsync()` remains exactly as originally flagged here — un-deleted, un-guarded, pending Julian's word.

    CONFIRMED NOT AN ISSUE (checked, not a bug): `cb_prompts.build_keyframe_prompt`'s `env_line`/`light_block`/`comp_block` construction scoped to `if not chained` — the same asymmetry a prior audit flagged and explicitly left unfixed — was re-verified byte-for-byte unchanged and found NOT to be a rule-12 violation on inspection: an opener beat's `env_img` IS the plate/master image itself, so the accompanying text describes the SAME image, not a second, competing world description the way a genuinely chained beat's old bug did. No action needed.

    Verified after every fix: ten touched Python files (`cb_preflight.py`, `cb_pipeline.py`, `cb_beats.py`, `cb_qa.py`, `cb_seedance.py`, `cb_prompts.py`, `cb_gen.py`, plus the three from rule 55) all compile clean; `cb-studio/app.html`'s inline script still passes a syntax-only check; `cb_preflight.py`'s BLOCK count is unchanged at 46 (FLAG dropped 62→54, STRUCTURAL rose 51→59 — exactly the 8 beats the relay-proxy fix corrected, zero new gaps introduced); `test_gate_cascade.py` still passes end to end; `cb_golden.py diff` still shows only the one already-flagged appearance-leak diff from rule 55, confirming none of tonight's fixes touched compiled prompt output. Every live-code fix was independently exercised (a real save round-trip in the running Studio, a real double-rejection reproducing and then correctly avoiding the archive collision, a real vocabulary-check sanity test) rather than trusted from a code read alone.

57. THE PIXAR PANEL'S RECOMMENDATIONS, BUILT (Julian, 2026-07-08 — asked "if you are a Pixar studio right now and you want to build this sort of automation, is this the right one? What would they look for? What would they change?", then, on hearing the panel's answer: "Well, if that's what Pixar thinks they would do, let's do it."): a five-department review (Pipeline TD, Story/Editorial, Production Management, Directing/Animation, Legal/Business Affairs) converged on one diagnosis from four independent angles — something fluid gets locked as final too early, before any cheap iteration happens — plus one hard business blocker (voice/music licensing) that is not software's to fix. This rule is the record of what was actually built from that verdict, and what was deliberately left for Julian's own action.

    FIVE PIECES BUILT THE SAME NIGHT:
    - **THE REPRODUCIBILITY SIDECAR** (Pipeline TD's ask): `cb_costs.write_gen_sidecar(out, **params)` writes a `.gen.json` alongside every rendered artifact (model/endpoint/resolution/duration/tier), called from every one of `cb_gen.py`'s generation functions (`generate_image`, `generate_video_seedance`, `generate_video_seedance_ref`, `eleven_tts`, `eleven_dialogue`, `eleven_music`, `eleven_sfx`) right next to the existing `cb_costs.log_spend` call. Never raises (fail-soft, matching `backup_media.backup_one`'s own convention) — a future "why does this differ from what shipped" question now has a real answer.
    - **REVIEWER IDENTITY ON EVERY APPROVAL** (Production's "distributed review" ask, the buildable increment — hiring a second reviewer is Julian's own call, not built): `cb_pipeline.approve(gate, scene, reviewed_by="Julian")` and `cb_beats.record_approval(...)` both now stamp WHO signed, not just that it was signed — `sd[gate]` changed from a bare `True` to `{"approved": True, "reviewed_by": ..., "at": ...}`, safe because every existing reader of `sd[gate]` across `cb_pipeline.py`/`serve.py`/`app.html` already used a plain truthy check, never strict boolean comparison (confirmed by grep before the change — zero reader-side edits needed). A "Reviewed by" field in the Studio's top bar (persisted to `localStorage`) threads through every sign-off call, `approveGate` AND `signSub` alike (the latter was found mid-build to have never passed `reviewedBy` at all — a real gap closed in the same pass, rule 11's sweep-the-pattern applied again).
    - **THE GATE-1 EXTERNAL REVIEW, BUILT** (closing a gap MANIFEST.md/rule 37 had named as "standing doctrine, not yet built" since 2026-07-06): `cb_pipeline.export_storyboard(pkg_path, scene_num, episode)` renders one clean Markdown document per scene — storyBeat, want/need, crystalTruth, kidRead/adultRead, dialogue in order, endState — wired through a new `/api/export-storyboard` endpoint and a "⬇ Export storyboard" button on Gate 1's row, next to the sign-off button. This is the document Julian's own doctrine already said he reads outside the Studio before signing Gate 1; it simply never existed as code until now.
    - **THE PERFORMANCE THROUGHLINE** (Directing/Animation's critique — `fidelityAllocation` decides ensemble staging beat-by-beat with no director holding the arc ACROSS a scene): a new required scene-level field, `Scene.performanceThroughline`, following the exact 5-place Scene Bubble pattern already proven for `ambientBed`/`sceneLook`/`parentLine` — schema (`cb_director_schemas.py`), the `episode_to_scenes` authoring prompt, `cb_preflight`'s scene-level BLOCK check, `scene_to_beats`'s own prompt (feeding the throughline into the context each beat's `fidelityAllocation` is authored against — closing the loop the critique named, not just adding an unused field), and a `cb-studio/app.html` scene-editor textarea. Verified: `cb_preflight.py` shows exactly 10 new BLOCKs (one per scene, all pre-existing scenes authored before the field existed — expected, not backfilled in this pass), and a zero-cost monkey-patched test of `scene_to_beats`'s constructed prompt confirmed the clause appears correctly when the field is populated and is cleanly absent when not.
    - **GATE "1.6" — THE PREVIZ REEL** (Story/Editorial + Pipeline TD's converged recommendation, the centerpiece): a new, near-zero-cost stage between Gate 1 (schema-valid storyboard, no audio, no visuals) and Gate 2a (the first real, paid generation call). New module `engine/cb_previz.py`: `scratch_vo_for_beat` fires one cheap `eleven_flash_v2_5` TTS call PER DIALOGUE-BEARING CUT (never one merged call for the whole beat, so a back-and-forth exchange like 1.B2's keeps each line in its own speaker's voice — the whole point is judging comedic timing, which a flattened single voice would hide), resolving each cut's `"SPEAKER: text"` label to its canonical character via `cb_voice._resolve_speaker` (a raw "FUZZBY" label never matches `characters.json` directly — this was caught and fixed live, mid-build, by a zero-cost mocked test before ever touching a real API). `placeholder_frame` builds a plain PIL title card (reusing `cb_post._pil_font`/`_review_font` verbatim — never ffmpeg `drawtext`, the exact font-availability risk this codebase already hit and fixed once) for any beat with no real keyframe yet. `assemble_beat_clip` holds the placeholder (or a real keyframe, if Gate 2b somehow already ran) for `HANDLE_TOTAL` or the scratch VO's own duration, muxed with that VO or silence. `assemble_scene_previz` builds every beat's clip in scene order and hard-cuts them with `cb_post.assemble_picture` reused verbatim, to `media/{episode}_Scene{N}_previz.mp4`.
      NAMED "GATE 1.6", NOT "1.5" — a real collision was found and resolved before any wiring: "Gate 1.5" was already the established name for Director's Eye (`cb_director_eye.py`), an unrelated, automatic, flag-only bible/craft review that fires at the end of every Gate-1 authoring pass and carries no lock state of its own (never a member of `GATE_SEQ`) — referenced in `cb_director.py`, `cb_director_schemas.py`, `cb_pipeline.py`'s own CLI, `app.html`, and this file's own rule 45. Reusing "1.5" for a second, different, REAL lock-bearing gate would have been exactly the kind of silent documentation contradiction rules 49/56 already exist to catch. "1.6" is also chronologically honest: Director's Eye runs automatically during authoring, before Julian ever signs Gate 1; the Previz Reel is a human sign-off stage that comes after Gate 1 is signed and before Gate 2a fires — genuinely next in the real sequence.
      WIRED AS A REAL GATE: `GATE_SEQ` gained `"1.6"` in both `cb_pipeline.py` and its deliberately-duplicated mirror in `cb-studio/serve.py` (confirmed every use of `GATE_SEQ` is string-equality/list-index, never numeric comparison — inserting a string element is safe); `_relock_if_stale`'s cascade-clear tuple gained `"1.6"` in both files; `approve()`/`fire()` map `gate in ("1","1.6")` to the same `cb_preflight.manifest_ok(..., gate="1")` scope (there is no plate/keyframe to check at either stage); `cb_pipeline.GATES["1.6"] = previz_reel`, dispatched through the EXISTING generic `fire()`/CLI catch-all with zero new special-casing needed. In `app.html`, Gate 1.6 is a genuine SUB-PHASE of Gate 1's own row (never a new `GATE_DEF` array entry — that array is positionally indexed by every `g-1`/`g==N` gate-number assumption throughout the file, and a literal insertion would have broken all of them) — a new bespoke `gate1Panel(sn)` mirrors Gate 2's proven two-step shape (`gate2Panel`'s "Step 1 · Foundation" / "Step 2 · Keyframes") exactly: Step 1 · Storyboard (unchanged beat-list review + sign-off + Director's Eye + Export-storyboard buttons, all moved here verbatim from the old generic per-gate body) then Step 2 · Previz Reel (fire/rebuild, a disabled-until-built sign-off button, the `<video>` review player). `gApprovedG(sn,1)` became `!!(lk["1"]&&lk["1.6"])` (mirroring gate 2's own `!!(lk["2a"]&&lk["2b"])` exactly) — so Gate 2a genuinely cannot unlock until 1.6 is signed, verified live via three safe, zero-side-effect client-side `PIPE.locked` simulations against the real running Studio (storyboard signed alone → previz step unlocks with sign disabled; previz file present → sign enables + video renders; both signed → Gate 1's rail node flips to "signed off" and Gate 2's row genuinely unlocks) — each simulation reverted by the Studio's own 3-second poll re-fetching the real `locked.json`, confirmed byte-identical before and after. `jobLabel`, `gatePhase`'s job-detection, `unsignGate`, `unsignSub`'s label text, and `fireSub`/`signSub`'s own readiness checks were all extended to the "1.6" sub-gate, matching the exact precedent each already held for "2a"/"2b".
      THE ONE DIALOGUE-REVISION EXCEPTION, STATED EXPLICITLY: Gate 1.6 is the ONE place dialogue may be revised after Gate 1 is signed — a HUMAN editing a line, through the existing beat editor, after hearing it read aloud and judging its timing/comedic rhythm. This is a creative decision, not the silent algorithmic drift the Faithful Director doctrine (rule 4's verbatim-lock machinery, `cb_director.enforce_verbatim`, `cb_preflight.check_scene_dialogue_verbatim`) was built to catch — that doctrine polices UNNOTICED drift between the signed script and what a beat ships; it was never meant to forbid Julian's own deliberate rewrite of his own already-approved line. No code change was needed to permit this — editing a beat via the existing `editBeat()`/`/api/beat-update` path was always possible post-Gate-1 — but the existing fingerprint-based cascade-relock (`_relock_if_stale`/`_scene_beats_fingerprint`, unchanged, now covering `"1.6"` in its clear-tuple per the wiring above) means any such edit correctly re-locks BOTH the storyboard sign-off and the previz sign-off, so a revised line is never shipped past a stale approval. Stated here explicitly so this one narrow exception never reads as a quiet contradiction of rule 4's own doctrine.

    WHAT WAS DELIBERATELY NOT ATTEMPTED, NAMED SO IT IS NOT LOST: hiring a second human reviewer, contacting ElevenLabs for enterprise broadcast/syndication/territorial/merchandising terms (the default ToS is confirmed insufficient for a broadcaster conversation), a legal opinion on AI-music copyrightability, drafting real chain-of-title paperwork for any character's voice, or deciding whether to slow broadcaster conversations pending the above — all Julian's own calls, some requiring a lawyer, none of them software and none of them mine to execute. Tracked as a plain checklist, `LEGAL_CHECKLIST.md` at repo root, each item marked `NOT YET RESOLVED` with the panel's own reasoning — a tracking artifact only, taking no legal action and resolving nothing.

    Verified after every piece: `cb_preflight.py`'s BLOCK count changed only by the expected +10 (`performanceThroughline`, one per scene — 18 BLOCK total, up from 8, beat-level 38/43-clean unaffected); `cb_golden.py diff` shows only the same already-flagged, already-known appearance-leak diff from rule 55 (nothing this session touched compiled prompt output); `test_gate_cascade.py`, `test_unapprove_locks.py`, `test_cb_preflight.py`, `test_cb_qa.py`, and `test_cb_segprompt.py` all pass end to end, including two real regressions caught and fixed by this same run — `test_gate_cascade.py`'s scratch scene fixture and `test_cb_preflight.py`'s clean-scene fixture both needed `performanceThroughline` added, the same "test fixtures drift whenever a new required manifest field ships" pattern rule 46 already named. `engine/cb_previz.py` was proven end-to-end at zero cost with a monkey-patched `cb_gen.eleven_tts` (writing a synthetic tone in place of a real API call) before ever being wired into the gate sequence.

    A SWEEP-THE-PATTERN GAP FOUND AND CLOSED THE SAME NIGHT (rule 11): after wiring "1.6" into `GATE_SEQ`, a grep for every other place a gate list is hand-written found `test_gate_cascade.py` (both its `test_cb_pipeline` and `test_serve_py` functions) and `test_unapprove_locks.py`'s `CASES` fixture all still enumerated the OLD five-gate sequence — meaning the standing test suite would have silently never exercised "1.6"'s cascade-clear behaviour at all, on either the `cb_pipeline.py` implementation or its `serve.py` mirror. A one-off scratch script (approve "1"+"1.6", edit the beat package, confirm both clear via `_relock_if_stale`) already proved the underlying code correct live against the real `cb_pipeline.py` — but per this project's own standing rule ("every fix must become permanent re-runnable software, never a one-off patch"), that proof was folded into the standing tests instead of being thrown away: both test files now sign/track "1.6" explicitly and assert it clears/survives exactly where "1"/"2a" already do. Re-run clean after the fix.

58. THE PERFORMANCE THROUGHLINE, CORRECTED — MECHANICAL, NOT AUTHORED (Julian, 2026-07-08, the same night rule 57 shipped, on seeing Scene 1 block on it — "the script and the storyboard and beat should deliver that... it needs to be automatic based on the beat and the storyboard"): rule 57's own new field, `Scene.performanceThroughline`, was built the wrong way round — a required, separately hand/LLM-authored scene-level note, asked for BEFORE any beats existed, then fed back into beat authoring as a plan. Julian's correction: there is no reason to ask anyone (human or Director) to type out who carries a scene's ensemble weight, when each beat's own `fidelityAllocation.primary` (already required, already authored, per rule 46/50) already says exactly that, the moment the beats are written. Asking for a separate note duplicates information the storyboard already contains, and — worse — required it to exist BEFORE the beats did, which is backwards.

    THE FIX: `cb_director._derive_performance_throughline(beats)` — a new, purely mechanical, zero-LLM function. Sorts a scene's beats by `beatCode`, walks each one's `fidelityAllocation.primary`, and collapses consecutive beats sharing the same primary into a "run" (e.g. Fuzzby carries 1.B1-1.B4, then Zenny takes over for 1.B5) — reporting the pattern in plain English, or the single-character case ("X carries this scene's focus throughout, no hand-off") when there's no shift. Nothing invented: if the beats don't show a throughline, this doesn't manufacture one. Verified live against the real package's own beats: `"This scene's focus moves from Fuzzby (1.B1-1.B4) to Zenny (1.B5) — the moment lands on Zenny in the closing beat (1.B5)."` — a caught-and-fixed Python operator-precedence bug in the first draft (the ternary's low binding precedence silently dropped the lead-in clause for the single-final-beat case — exactly the Scene 1 shape) meant the very first version would have shipped a broken, truncated sentence for the most common case; fixed and re-verified before it ever reached the pipeline.

    WIRED IN THREE PLACES, ALL CORRECTED THE SAME NIGHT:
    - `cb_director_schemas.Scene.performanceThroughline` — changed from `str = Field(min_length=1)` (required LLM output) to `Optional[str] = None` (nullable, never something OpenAI's strict structured-output mode has to invent a value for).
    - `cb_director.episode_to_scenes`'s authoring prompt — the instruction asking the Director to WRITE this field is removed outright, replaced with a one-line note that it's derived after the fact; `scene_to_beats`'s own prompt no longer reads a (now-nonexistent) pre-authored value back into beat authoring either.
    - `direct()`'s per-scene loop — `sc["performanceThroughline"] = _derive_performance_throughline(beats)` runs immediately after `derive_plate` (the existing precedent for "derive a scene-level field from its own already-written beats"), attaching the mechanical result onto the scene dict once its beats exist.
    - `cb_preflight.check_scene_technical` — the BLOCK-on-blank check is gone. It now COMPUTES the value live (via the same `_derive_performance_throughline`, given the `beats` its caller already has on hand) and reports it as an informational STRUCTURAL line, never blocking, since it's always computable the instant a scene's beats have their required `fidelityAllocation`. This immediately cleared all 10 of rule 57's own BLOCKs on the real production package with ZERO backfill, ZERO manual authoring, and ZERO re-fire of Gate 1 — confirmed live: `cb_preflight.py`'s total BLOCK count dropped from 18 to 8 (exactly the 10 `performanceThroughline` items, leaving only the pre-existing, unrelated dialogue-verbatim BLOCKs), and Scene 1 individually now reads a clean `PASS` with 0 BLOCK.
    - `cb-studio/app.html`'s scene editor — the now-pointless textarea (there is nothing left to type) is removed from `editScene`/`renderSceneEditor`/`esSave`, replaced with a one-line note pointing at the beat list as the real source; verified live in the browser (the edit-Scene-1 modal now shows only sceneLook/ambientBed/parentLine, no console errors).
    - Both scratch test fixtures that fed rule 57's version (`test_gate_cascade.py`'s `_scratch_scenes`, `test_cb_preflight.py`'s `_clean_scene`) had their hand-set `performanceThroughline` values removed — they no longer represent anything the schema accepts as authored input. Full suite re-run clean after every change: `test_gate_cascade.py`, `test_unapprove_locks.py`, `test_cb_preflight.py`, `test_cb_qa.py`, `test_cb_segprompt.py` all pass; `cb_golden.py diff` shows only the same pre-existing, already-flagged appearance-leak diff from rule 55 — nothing in this correction touched a single shipped prompt.

    THE LESSON, STATED PLAINLY: rule 57's own version of this field violated the exact principle rule 28's Fidelity Law already established for the Acting DNA field (and rule 47 restated for the Manifest layer generally) — never ask for new authored content when the existing, already-authored data already answers the question; a field that duplicates the storyboard is a liability (it can drift from what the beats actually say) rather than an asset. This rule stands as the corrected, current statement; rule 57's own text is left as the dated record of the first (wrong) shape, not rewritten, matching this file's own established practice for superseded entries.

59. THE FULL CODE AUDIT (Julian, 2026-07-08, immediately after rule 58 — "go through all of the code line by line... taking out any dead code, making sure there's no overlaps, no crashes"): the most exhaustive line-by-line pass this codebase has had. A multi-agent workflow read every `engine/*.py` file and `cb-studio/serve.py`/`app.html` in full (17 parallel finders, one per file or small file-group), reporting crash risks, dead code, duplication and stale comments/citations; every raw finding then went through an independent adversarial re-verification agent before being trusted (3 of 70 verifier calls died to a transient rate-limit; those 3 were checked by hand instead of re-running the workflow). 70 findings confirmed real. 64 were mechanical/unambiguous and fixed directly, file by file, each verified with `py_compile` + a fresh `import` immediately after; the remaining 6 were genuine judgment calls (an architectural gap, a design trade-off, or a decision that could silently change existing beats' behaviour) and were reported to Julian rather than auto-applied, per this codebase's own standing discipline for exactly this kind of call.

    WHAT WAS FIXED, BY CATEGORY (full list of files: `cb_prompts.py`, `cb_qa.py`, `cb_pipeline.py`, `cb_seedance.py`, `cb-studio/serve.py`, `cb-studio/app.html`, `cb_gen.py`, `cb_beats.py`, `cb_director.py`, `cb_voice.py`, `cb_writer.py`, `cb_scene.py`, `cb_previz.py`, `cb_replicator.py`, `cb_retake.py`, `cb_segprompt.py`, `cb_post.py`, `cb_golden.py`, `cb_costs.py`, `cb_director_schemas.py`, `beat_preview.py`, `cb_address.py`, `cb_preflight.py`, `test_static_hardening.py`):
    - **A real, guaranteed-crash bug**: `cb_gen.py`'s `refvideo` CLI subcommand called `generate_video_seedance_ref` with 7 positional arguments against a signature whose parameter order didn't match — every argument from `video_urls` onward landed on the wrong parameter (a resolution string assigned to `video_urls`, a filename assigned to `duration`, a boolean assigned to `out`). Fixed to pass every argument by keyword.
    - **Silent-failure / wrong-data bugs**: `cb_voice._resolve_speaker`'s substring match let a short name ("Keen") match a longer, unrelated one that happened to contain it ("Keen's Mum") if the longer name was checked first — misattributing Keen's own lines to his mother's voice; fixed to try an exact match across the whole pool before ever falling back to substring matching. `cb_voice._canon_names` was returning `"sizeClasses"` (a lookup table, not a character) as if it were a real character name, because it filtered on "is this a dict" rather than "is this an actual character record" — fixed to require a `voiceId` key. `cb_segprompt._v5_active_cast`'s own docstring claimed "Keen's Mum" would be matched by a cut naming "Mum" — the code actually checked for "Keen" (the wrong side of the possessive), so a cut naming Keen alone wrongly marked his mother as active, and a cut naming "Mum" alone wrongly marked her as background; fixed to extract the word after the apostrophe, not before it. `cb_prompts.size_clause`/`cb_qa`'s `SIZE_MISMATCH` check both asserted "X is visibly larger than Y" even when two characters had the IDENTICAL sizeRank (a tie) — found and fixed in both places (rule 11 sweep). `cb_pipeline.check_beat_word_count`'s relay-status guess (`not is_scene_opener`) was live-wrong for any beat whose predecessor has no rendered clip yet, incorrectly flagging clean beats as over budget — fixed to use the same `cb_scene.relay_source_for` every real fire path already uses.
    - **A recurring lexicographic-sort bug, swept everywhere it appeared** (rule 11): `beats.sort(key=lambda b: str(b.get("beatCode")...))` sorts beat codes as plain strings, so `"1.B10"` sorts before `"1.B2"` — silently correct only while every scene stays under 10 beats. Found and fixed identically in `cb_beats.fire_next_beat`, `cb_director._derive_performance_throughline` (this session's own new code from rule 58, caught by the same audit that built it), `cb_previz._beats_for_scene`, and `cb_replicator.walk_scene` — all four now use `cb_preflight._beat_sort_key` (natural sort on the trailing beat number), one shared implementation instead of four independent copies.
    - **Path-safety and query-cost fixes**: `cb-studio/serve.py`'s `/api/scene-shot` "pull from library" endpoint used a naive `str(path).startswith(str(ROOT))` containment check — a string-prefix test, not real path containment — fixed to `Path.is_relative_to(ROOT)`. `cb_address.shot_at_time` called the expensive `scene_shot_windows` (which recompiles every beat's prompt) TWICE per lookup for no reason — fixed to call it once and reuse the result.
    - **UI data-loss bugs**: `cb-studio/app.html`'s `saveShots` (the per-shot "Shots" editor, distinct from the full beat editor rule 56 already fixed) silently stripped `chorusMembers`/`voiceTreatment` off every cut on save, and separately made dialogue/delivery fields impossible to clear once set (a blank save silently fell back to the old value) — both fixed. Its `keyframesFor` helper showed "awaiting relay" for every correctly-relayed beat that used THE RE-MINT CORRECTION's raw-harvest path (rule 45) instead of a re-mint file, because it only ever checked for the re-mint file's existence — fixed to fall back to the raw settle frame.
    - **Dead code removed** (confirmed zero callers via grep before deletion, never guessed at): `cb_prompts.py`'s `scale_line`/`staging_line`/`emotion_line` and the whole dead i2v/ref2vid cluster (`build_i2v_prompt`, `build_ref2vid_prompt`, `ANIMATION_DIRECTION` and its helpers); `cb_pipeline.py`'s unused `cb_gen` import; `cb_seedance.py`'s three orphaned `_ARCHETYPE_OVERRIDES` keys for beat codes that don't exist in the real package (and one newly-added, previously-unreachable mapping for 1.B5's own actual archetype); `cb-studio/serve.py`'s dead `GATE_NAME` dict and unreachable `send_head` override; `cb_beats.py`'s dead `audio_dur` parameter on `gate3_prepare` (accepted, never read); `cb_director_schemas.py`'s dead `opener_slug` local; `cb_address.py`'s dead `write_sidecar` function; `cb_retake.py`'s dead `read_retake_log` function and unused `csv_path` parameter; `cb_post.py`'s dead `stitch` function (a genuine duplicate of functionality that actually lives in `cb_beats.stitch`) and a dead `durs` computation in `assemble_picture`; `cb_golden.py`'s and `beat_preview.py`'s unused imports; `cb_costs.py`'s dead `op_key` parameter.
    - **Correctness gaps in error handling**: `cb_gen.py`'s `generate_image` crashed with a raw `KeyError` on a safety-blocked response (no `"candidates"` key) instead of a clear message; `generate_video` (Veo) never checked for an `"error"` field on a completed-but-failed long-running operation before indexing into `"response"`; `generate_video_seedance_ref` had no guard for a missing `"video"` key in the fal response (its sibling `generate_video_seedance` already had one) — all three fixed with the same clear-message-then-raise pattern already used elsewhere in the same file. `cb_scene.remint_settle_frame` called `char_identity_ref` (which raises for any character with no resolvable reference) unguarded inside a list comprehension, crashing the WHOLE re-mint step over one missing character — fixed to skip just that character, matching this file's own `preflight()` and `cb_beats._anchor()` precedent. `cb_previz.assemble_beat_clip` returned a clip path even when the ffmpeg call that was supposed to produce it failed, silently feeding a broken/missing file into the final assembly — fixed to return `None` on failure and skip that beat with a named reason. `cb_post._clips` built its `slug` via `s.get("slug", s["shotCode"].replace(...))` — Python evaluates a `dict.get` default argument eagerly even when the key IS present, so this crashed with a raw `AttributeError` the moment any beat had neither `beatCode` nor `shotCode` — fixed to compute the fallback separately.
    - **A real, silently-wrong gate check**: `cb_preflight.check_beat_creative`'s three `fidelityAllocation` field checks (primary/secondary/economized) were chained as `if`/`elif`/`elif` — so a beat missing ALL THREE only ever reported the FIRST one as a BLOCK; fixing that one field would only then reveal the next, understating the real gap count every run and turning a one-pass fix into multi-run whack-a-mole. Fixed to three independent `if` checks. (Checked live against the real package: this bug happened to be latent, not currently triggering an undercount — no beat in the current package has more than one of the three blank at once — but it is a real correctness bug the moment one ever does.)
    - **A real, guaranteed-KeyError crash**: `cb_writer.write`'s remake-pass filter built `{k: sc[k] for k in (...) if (sc.get(k, {}) or {}).get("score", 0) < BAR}` — the filter condition tolerates a factor key the LLM's score output simply omitted, but the dict comprehension's OWN key expression used `sc[k]` (not `sc.get(k, {})`), so the exact case the condition was written to tolerate would still crash. Fixed to match.
    - **Stale/misleading comments and citations, corrected rather than left to rot**: `cb_qa.py` had a comment claiming two checks were "no longer called by anything" when both are called live from `cb_beats.py`; `cb_pipeline.py` had a comment describing the FORBIDDEN post-voice-swap architecture as current behaviour, directly contradicting Law 5 — rewritten to describe what the code actually does (no swap, ever); `cb_seedance.py`'s module docstring claimed the module was "ADDITIVE" and "touches NOTHING in the render path," stale since it became the sole fallback builder; `cb_beats.py`'s own module and `run()` docstrings still said every beat renders "10-12s" and fires from an "i2vPrompt" — both retired since the Handle Doctrine (rule 20) and the v5 engine (rule 42); `cb_preflight.py` cited exact text in `cb_qa.py` that no longer exists there (retired during an earlier purge) — the false citation removed rather than chased to a new moving target.
    - **Test-fixture staleness**: `cb-studio/test_static_hardening.py`'s own "these files must be servable" list pointed at `engine/media/AB_1.B1_flux.png`, a file that no longer exists (matches neither the current naming convention nor anything on disk) — every run of this safety test was silently reporting a false FAIL on a real, correctly-served asset; fixed to point at a real, currently-existing keyframe.
    - **A missing forwarding parameter**: `cb_preflight.manifest_ok` — the shared choke-point every gate-arming call site in the codebase imports and calls — hardcoded `run()`'s noisy default logger with no way for any caller to quiet or redirect it short of bypassing the choke-point entirely and calling `run()` directly; added `log=print` forwarding.
    - **An unused parameter removed from a live function signature**: `cb_preflight.check_scene_dialogue_verbatim`'s `roster` parameter was threaded in from its one call site but never read in the function body; removed from both the signature and the call site.

    SIX FINDINGS REPORTED TO JULIAN, NOT AUTO-APPLIED (genuine judgment calls — an architectural gap, a design trade-off, or a change that could silently alter existing beats' behaviour). **ALL SIX CLOSED, dated below (full-codebase audit, 2026-07-11/12) — a later doctrine-drift audit found this list's text never caught up, still presenting all six as open with no later rule documenting any resolution; annotated inline here instead of a new rule, matching this file's own established CORRECTED/CLOSED convention:**
    1. **`cb_address.beat_address_map` — HIGH — broken shot-level addressing for every beat shipping the v5 definitive prompt.** It reads `(g.get("prompt") or {}).get("action_timeline")` from `cb_seedance.get_seedance_prompt`'s return — but for a definitive (v5) beat, that same `"prompt"` key holds a plain STRING (the compiled prose), not a dict with an `action_timeline` field. Calling `.get(...)` on a string raises `AttributeError`, caught by this function's own try/except, so it fails silently rather than crashing loudly — but the practical effect is that `cb_retake.py`'s whole surgical, per-shot retake system (frame-accurate splice, the retake CSV, `scene_shot_windows`) has no real per-shot timing data to work with for any beat using the current, standard prompt builder. **CLOSED (task #264):** `beat_address_map` now branches on `get_seedance_prompt`'s own `"raw": True` flag — when set, shots are derived straight from the beat's own `cuts[]` via `cb_segprompt._v5_shot_time_ranges` (rule 54's shot-timing law, the same deterministic split the shipped prompt itself uses), so the address map always matches what actually rendered; the legacy `action_timeline` path is kept, unchanged, as the fallback for a beat still on the older compact builder.
    2. **`cb_seedance.infer_director_mode` — HIGH — trusts an unvalidated `beat.director_mode` field.** `if beat.get("director_mode"): return beat["director_mode"]` returns WHATEVER is in that field with no check that it's one of the known valid modes in `DIRECTOR_MODE_GUIDANCE` — a typo, a stale/retired mode name, or bad data from an edit would silently propagate through every downstream lookup (`_DIRECTOR_TO_STYLE`, `_DIR_SIGNALS`), each of which would then quietly fall through to a generic default with no warning. **CLOSED (task #265):** `infer_director_mode` now validates `beat.director_mode` against `DIRECTOR_MODE_GUIDANCE`'s own keys; an invalid value prints a `[cb_seedance] WARNING` naming the beat and the bad value, then falls through to the keyword-scored resolver rather than being trusted verbatim — the fall-through-to-resolver option, not a hard BLOCK.
    3. **`cb_craft.py`'s ensemble-individuation check has a name-key collision with `cb_qa.py`'s vocabulary check — MEDIUM.** Documented in this file's own rule 49 already (the lexicon-scope collision between the two checks). **CLOSED (task #266), after this list was written:** both checks now use a proximity-based attribution instead of whole-cut scanning — `cb_qa.check_character_vocabulary` (corrected 2026-07-08, contradiction sweep) attributes a banned-word hit to whichever named character's occurrence is TEXTUALLY NEAREST to it, not merely "some name of theirs is somewhere in the cut"; `cb_craft.check_ensemble_individuation` independently scopes its own lexicon-verb match to a ~120-character window following a character's own name. Two different mechanisms, not a shared fix, but both now scope by proximity rather than whole-cut — the exact class of collision rule 49 named no longer reproduces via the original whole-cut mechanism.
    4. **`cb_beats.py` has the beat-package-load-and-filter pattern (`json.load` + filter by `sceneNumber` + derive `code`/`slug`) duplicated across roughly five functions — LOW.** **CLOSED (task #268):** `cb_beats._load_scene_beats(pkg_path, scene_num, d=None)` is now the shared helper, wired into all 5 call sites (confirmed live — `_load_scene_beats` is referenced at 6+ locations in the current file). The BROADER duplication rule 66 itself separately found (the identical pattern repeated across ~24 sites in 5+ OTHER files, not just these five in `cb_beats.py`) remains open — see rule 66's own explicit deferral of that wider scope.
    5. **`cb_pipeline.py` has the scene-level manifest-check-and-refuse block (import → `manifest_ok` → print a refusal → `return False`) duplicated across three call sites** (`approve`, `regen_shot`, `fire`) rather than sharing one helper the way the beat-level checks already share `_manifest_gate_beat` — LOW, same reasoning as (4). **CLOSED (task #269):** `cb_pipeline._manifest_gate_scene(scene, gate="1", label=None, list_gaps=False)` is now the shared helper, called from `approve`/`regen_shot`/`fire`/`build_master`/`regen_anchor` (5 sites, more than the original 3 named here) — confirmed live in the current working tree, not yet committed to `git HEAD` as of the commit this rule's own dated entries reference (bdab24e); lands in a future commit.
    6. **`cb_prompts.py`'s `masters_index`/`_music_line`/`_sfx_line` — LOW, unclear whether dead code or a missing wire-up.** All three exist, are self-contained, and have zero call sites found by grep — but unlike the other dead functions removed this pass, these read like a half-built feature (a music/SFX-brief compiler) rather than a retired one. **CLOSED (task #267):** wired up rather than deleted — `engine/masters_preview.py` and `engine/sound_brief_preview.py` (both new, small preview modules) now call `masters_index()`/`_music_line()`/`_sfx_line()`, confirming Julian's own call was "finish it," not "abandon it."

    VERIFICATION: every touched file re-compiled (`py_compile`) and re-imported clean; all 5 standing test suites (`test_gate_cascade.py`, `test_cb_preflight.py`, `test_cb_qa.py`, `test_cb_segprompt.py`, `test_unapprove_locks.py`) pass with zero regressions. `cb_preflight.py` against the real production package: 38/43 beats clean, 8 BLOCK / 52 FLAG / 69 STRUCTURAL — identical to the pre-audit baseline (the elif-chain fix found a real bug but it wasn't actively hiding anything in the CURRENT package's own data). `cb_golden.py diff`: zero assertion failures (the shipped v5 prompts remain structurally sound under every content check), but the raw text diff shows the golden set is broadly stale against the v5 engine — not narrowly, as rule 55's own closing note claimed ("shows exactly the appearance-leak fix and nothing else"). Confirmed via `git log` that the golden `.txt` files themselves were last touched 2026-07-05, before the v3→v5 rewrite (rule 42, 2026-07-06) and every rule since — this staleness predates and is wholly unrelated to tonight's audit; nothing in this pass touched a single shipped-prompt-generating function's actual output for Scene 1's real cast. Recapture still waits on Julian's own review, per the standing rule — not done here.

60. THE PROVIDER SWITCH — SEEDREAM 5 PRO (Julian's ruling, 2026-07-09 — "we go Seedream 5 pro, it's the one the industry is suggesting"): the default keyframe image model changes from Nano Banana 2 (`gemini-3.1-flash-image`, direct Gemini API, live since 2026-06-28) to **Seedream 5 Pro** (`bytedance/seedream/v5/pro/edit`, fal.ai) — decided on real evidence, not vendor marketing alone.
    THE EVIDENCE: a real, apples-to-apples side-by-side on 1.B1's actual production keyframe — the identical 1086-word prompt and identical 3 references (Fuzzby turnaround, Zenny turnaround, scene plate) fired through both models via `cb_scene.keyframe_for`'s own WYSIWYG compile, not a hand-typed approximation. Result: NB2 invented glowing gold hearts and swirling magic-light crystal auras — a direct, visible violation of the Crystal World Rule's own explicit constraint in that same prompt ("NO crystal self-glow, aura, beams, particles, hum or magical activity") — and dropped Zenny's signature identity detail (rosy blush cheeks, present on her turnaround sheet). Seedream 5 Pro held both correctly: no invented magic, crystals rendered as static ambient set-dressing as instructed, and Zenny's blush preserved. Neither model nailed the wing-law asymmetric-flight-pose instruction — a wash, not a differentiator either way. **n=1, a single seed each — not a benchmark, and generation is stochastic** (a re-roll could go the other way for NB2); the decision rests on this real, run trial plus Julian's own industry-signal read, not a claim of statistical proof.
    THE MECHANISM (`cb_gen.py`): `generate_image()` — the ONE function every `cb_scene.py` call site uses (14 call sites, zero changed) — is now a provider dispatcher: `IMAGE_PROVIDER = os.environ.get("CB_IMAGE_PROVIDER", "seedream")` picks `_generate_image_seedream` (new, uploads refs to fal via the existing `_fal_upload`/`_fal_subscribe` helpers already proven on Seedance video) or `_generate_image_nanobanana` (the OLD `generate_image` body, renamed verbatim, zero behavior change) — kept live, never deleted, selectable via `CB_IMAGE_PROVIDER=nanobanana` for rollback or a future re-test. Passing `model=` explicitly (the old function's own back-compat contract) forces the nanobanana path regardless of `IMAGE_PROVIDER`. The prompt/reference doctrine itself (rule 5's appearance-non-leak law, the reference-anchored identity language) is model-agnostic by construction — no prompt-building code changed for this swap, only which API renders the same text+references.
    THE COST CORRECTION (`cb_costs.py`): fixed a real, pre-existing bug found while pricing this decision — `RATES["nanobanana2_image"]` was pegged at a self-flagged "low-provider-mismatch" $0.04 guess; Google's own pricing page (confirmed live, 2026-07-09) shows NB2 is billed by output token, resolving to $0.101/image at 2K (`cb_gen.py`'s own default resolution) — every NB2 spend this ledger has ever logged undercounted real cost by ~2.5x. Corrected to $0.101 ("high" confidence), and a new `seedream5pro_image` rate added at $0.144/image (fal's own $0.135 2K-tier price + $0.009 for 2 extra reference images beyond the first free one, the common 3-ref case) — Seedream 5 Pro costs roughly 40% more per image, ~4.4¢, immaterial at episode scale (~43 keyframes, well under $10 total either way). `estimate_image_cost(provider=...)` now takes an explicit provider matching `IMAGE_PROVIDER`'s own two values exactly.
    VERIFIED: a zero-cost dispatch test (`test_cb_gen.py`, new, 12/12 passing) proves all three routing cases — default-to-seedream, explicit-model-forces-nanobanana, and the `CB_IMAGE_PROVIDER=nanobanana` rollback — without a single real API call; the two RATES entries are confirmed genuinely distinct, not a copy-paste no-op. All 8 standing test suites re-run clean after the swap. `cb_preflight.py`/`cb_golden.py` untouched by this change (neither reads which image provider rendered a keyframe).

61. THE VOICE-STEP RETRY (Julian, 2026-07-09, live during his own first real Gate-3 scene walk of Scene 1 — "b1 and b3 but b2 isnt firing"): a real, in-production gap found and closed the same session it surfaced, not left for later.
    THE SYMPTOM: `walk`/Gate 3 correctly rendered 1.B1 (already approved, skipped by design) and 1.B3, but skipped 1.B2 with `1.B2: REFUSED — beat has dialogue but no V3 @Audio1 track (Law 5: no native-voice fallback)`. This is Law 5 doing exactly what it's built to do — refuse rather than silently fall back to Seedance's native voice — so the REFUSAL itself was correct; the question was why the voice track failed at all.
    THE DIAGNOSIS: `cb_voice.build_dialogue_track` returned `None` with NO exception raised and NO diagnostic printed — confirmed by reproducing the exact call live (`cb_seedance.director_voice_direction` + `build_dialogue_track` with 1.B2's real beat data). 1.B2's own data was fine — both speakers (Fuzzby, Zenny) resolved to their correct canonical voiceIds, no attribution drift, `wordlessHeld` correctly false. Re-running the identical call moments later succeeded cleanly, and calling the underlying `cb_gen.eleven_dialogue` piece directly also succeeded — strongly pointing to a transient ElevenLabs contention/rate-limit blip, most likely from the SAME account being hit by the still-running live Gate-3 job concurrently rendering 1.B4's own dialogue at that exact moment. Not a data bug, not a code bug in 1.B2 specifically — a one-off timing collision.
    THE REAL GAP: every OTHER stage in this pipeline already gets ONE automatic retry before a hard stop (rule 28's one-render economy) — the voice sub-step never did. `cb_beats.run()`'s per-beat loop called `cb_voice.build_dialogue_track` exactly once; a transient blip there permanently skipped the beat for the WHOLE scene walk rather than just retrying, unlike every other failure mode this pipeline already treats as recoverable.
    THE FIX: extracted the inline voice-build call into `cb_beats._build_voice_track_with_retry(code, b, out, voice_direction)` — a small, independently-testable helper (matching this codebase's own pattern of extracting shared logic, e.g. `_beat_sort_key`, `_manifest_gate_beat`) that tries `build_dialogue_track` up to twice: once, and if it comes back falsy (whether from a raised exception OR a silent empty return — both now handled identically) OR a printed diagnostic naming which happened, retry exactly once more before giving up. `run()`'s per-beat loop now calls this helper instead of the raw function. The existing Law-5 REFUSED/continue gate downstream is completely unchanged — it still fires exactly the same way if both attempts fail, this only adds the missing retry before that gate is reached.
    VERIFIED: 4 new zero-cost mocked test cases in `test_cb_beats.py` (11 total in that file now) cover every real shape — falsy-then-succeeds, exhausts at exactly 2 attempts (never a 3rd), survives a raised exception on attempt 1 too, and a first-attempt success never wastes a second call. All 8 standing test suites re-run clean. This was implemented and verified WHILE Julian's own live Gate-3 job was still running against the OLD code in an already-spawned subprocess — safe, since each beat-render fire spawns a fresh subprocess that re-imports `cb_beats.py`, so the fix took effect on the very next fire (his retry of 1.B2) without touching the in-flight one.

62. SOLVE THE PROBLEM, NOT THE SYMPTOM — THE ARCHETYPE SAFETY NET, WIRED IN (Julian's ruling, 2026-07-09, immediately after 1.B4's real footage showed Fuzzby and Zenny both fully vanishing into a flower — "every time we find something like this, we need to write it into the software, not just the prompt, because we need to solve the problem, not just the symptom"): a standing, permanent working principle, stated explicitly as its own rule rather than left implicit in this file's own long-running practice (rules 3/11/46/56/59 all embody it without ever naming it directly) — from now on, a content-level bug fix is not considered done until the SOFTWARE GAP that let it happen is also closed, not just the one beat's data.
    THE CASE THAT PROMPTED IT: 1.B4's dive cut let Fuzzby fully vanish into the flower, characters blending together, on real rendered footage. The IMMEDIATE fix (hand-editing 1.B4's own `stagingProhibited` + rewriting its cut text) was a symptom patch. The DEEPER finding: 1.B4 was already assigned the physical archetype `POLLEN_SMEAR_TUMBLE` via `cb_seedance._ARCHETYPE_OVERRIDES` — and that archetype's own `prohibited_staging` field already said, verbatim, "disappearing into a flower." The staging doctrine that would have prevented this bug ALREADY EXISTED, authored and correct — it just never reached the prompt Seedance actually renders. Confirmed by grep: `cb_segprompt.py` (the sole v5 prompt compiler, per rule 42) had ZERO references anywhere to `physical_action_archetype`, `PHYSICAL_ARCHETYPES`, `infer_physical_archetype`, `contact_rule`, `physics_rule`, or `visibility_rule` before this fix — the whole rich archetype system (visibility/contact/physics/payoff/recovery/continuity/prohibited rules, authored across many beats and rulings this session) was live-and-correct DATA sitting completely disconnected from the shipping path, doing nothing, for every v5-definitive beat in the show.
    THE SOFTWARE FIX (`cb_segprompt.py`): a new `_v5_archetype_prohibited(beat, scene)` resolves the beat's physical archetype the SAME way `cb_seedance.py`'s own legacy builder always has (`infer_director_mode` → `infer_shot_style` → `_gag_locks()` → `infer_physical_archetype`) and returns its `PHYSICAL_ARCHETYPES[...]["prohibited_staging"]` phrases, split on `;` into bare items. `_v5_negative_line(beat, scene)` (signature extended to take `scene`, its one call site in `emit_v5` updated to match) now MERGES these into the shipped Negative line alongside the beat's own hand-authored `stagingProhibited`, deduping case-insensitively so a hand-authored item that already covers the same ground never repeats. `cb_seedance` is imported LAZILY inside the function — it already imports `_strip_spoken_words` FROM `cb_segprompt`, so a module-level import would be circular. Never raises: archetype resolution is best-effort enrichment, not a required input — a resolution failure degrades to "no extra negatives," exactly as if the beat had no archetype at all, never breaks compilation for any beat.
    THE RETROACTIVE PAYOFF, PROVEN LIVE (this is the actual test of "solve the problem, not the symptom" — did OTHER, untouched beats now get protected too?): 1.B1 (archetype `LEAF_CRASH_REBOUND`, never hand-edited with any `stagingProhibited` at all) now automatically ships "no face-first contact; no entering/burying inside a flower or leaf; no disappearing; no hidden silhouette..." in its Negative line — protection it never had before, with zero beat-specific authoring. 1.B5 (`FLOWER_STUCK_BUTTON`) likewise now ships "no the flower fully swallowing him with no readable silhouette; no a blank empty flower..." automatically. Every future beat that resolves to an archetype — present or not yet written — gets this the moment it's compiled, forever, with no one having to remember to type it in by hand.
    THE CITATION FIX, SAME COMMIT (rule 7): `cb_qa.check_gate3_lint`'s own citation map (`citations["negatives"]`) named only "the standing eleven + beat.stagingProhibited" as the Negative line's source — corrected in the same pass that added the third source, so this citation never had the chance to go stale first (unlike the several citation-staleness bugs rules 46/59 found and fixed after the fact).
    VERIFIED: a new test group in `test_cb_segprompt.py` (`test_archetype_prohibited_wired_into_negatives`) proves the exact retroactive claim above against real beat 1.B1 — confirms it carries no hand-authored `stagingProhibited`, resolves `LEAF_CRASH_REBOUND` automatically, and ships that archetype's own phrase in its real compiled Negative line; also proves graceful degradation on a malformed beat (never raises) and correct dedup (a hand-authored phrase already covering the archetype's own is never repeated). All 9 standing test suites re-run clean. `cb_preflight.py` against the full 43-beat production package: BLOCK count unchanged (8, all pre-existing dialogue-verbatim drift, none new); FLAG count rose from 52 to 56 (four beats — 1.B1/1.B4/1.B5 plus one more — newly crossed the 400-word TARGET from the added negatives, none crossed the 650-word hard BLOCK); `cb_golden.py`'s content assertions: 0 failures across the whole package.

63. "MAKE SURE EVERY PHYSICS IS RIGHT" — THE FULL-EPISODE PHYSICS-CLARITY AUDIT (Julian, 2026-07-09, immediately after rule 62 — extending the fix from one beat to the whole show): rule 62 wired the archetype's *negative* rules (prohibited_staging) into the shipped prompt automatically. This rule is the companion sweep it explicitly deferred: does every beat's own *positive* action/framing text actually embody its assigned archetype's physics (visibility_rule/contact_rule/physics_rule), the way 1.B4's didn't?
    METHOD: every one of the 43 beats resolved to its real physical archetype via `cb_seedance.infer_physical_archetype` (the same resolution `_v5_archetype_prohibited` now uses at render time). 19 beats resolve to a genuine physical-action archetype (crash/tumble/collision/contact-type staging); the other 24 are dialogue/reaction beats with no physical-archetype risk. All 19 were reviewed against the exact rubric that caught 1.B4 — does the beat's own shipped cuts[] text concretely embody its archetype's positive physics, anchor every present character's visibility (especially the one 1.B4 nearly lost — a second character silently blending/vanishing when the camera focuses on someone else), and avoid contradicting its own prohibited_staging — then every raw finding was independently re-verified by a second pass instructed to actively try to refute it. 19/19 beats were flagged on the first pass; 12/19 survived adversarial verification as real, concrete gaps (not stylistic nitpicks) — a genuinely high hit rate, consistent with this class of bug being systemic across the archetype system rather than a one-off.
    WHAT WAS FIXED, DIRECTLY, IN THE BEAT DATA (11 beats — never touching prompt-compiler code, since the code-level fix already exists via rule 62; this is authored-content correction): 1.B2 (flower compress/spring-back physics made concrete instead of implied), 1.B3 (full-body visibility anchored in its blossom-landing cut; moustache/goatee continuity added to endState/endStateStill, which the archetype's own continuity_physical_rule requires and the text had dropped), 1.B4 (a second pass — the word "moustache"/"goatee" now appears explicitly in the dive cut, not just generic "pollen," so the specific comedic mark the archetype protects has a real textual anchor), 1.B5 (a peeking leg/antenna added so "stuck in the flower" reads as a readable silhouette, not the archetype's own prohibited "fully swallowing him"; the missing comic-thud contact beat added; `stagingProhibited` populated for the first time), 4.B3 (Squeaky no longer silently disappears underwater mid-storm with no resolution — given a concrete, safe position instead; the "mast" vs "boat edge" contradiction between this beat's own endState/endStateStill and both the archetype's continuity_physical_rule AND 4.B4's own downstream continuity resolved in favor of "boat edge," which both of those already agreed on; the required post-escalation self-grounding breath, previously entirely absent, added), 4.B4 (explicit boat lurch/tilt language added — the only physics_rule requirement that had zero textual anchor beyond one mild "bounces"), 6.B2 (the framing/action contradiction — "Zenny frame-right" vs "Zenny glides in behind him" — reconciled to match the stated framing; a brief visible-intact clause added to the post-crash heap so "the crash hiding him from view" can't happen even in the split-second before he sits up), 7.B1 (Squeaky — central to the beat's entire action — added to `openingCast`, so her reference image is actually attached to the beat's generation instead of relying on an absent identity anchor), 7.B6 (Aida, present in `openingCast` but never once given a position or action across four cuts, given one — the same unanchored-character gap that nearly cost Zenny her distinctness in 1.B4), 8.B1 (the archetype is literally named GROUP_HUG_SWAMP, and the shipped text never once described actual embrace/contact — only proximity and smiling; concrete contact given to both the action cut and the settle, since continuity_physical_rule requires the embrace to carry forward and nothing existed yet to carry), 9.B3 (Fuzzby and Zenny's established contrast — his over-eager clapping, her flat deadpan — was never re-anchored in the wide group cut that follows their two-shot, risking exactly the kind of "swallowed into an undifferentiated group" gap 1.B4 had; and the archetype's own required "embrace" was staged as a mere "reaches toward" — approach, not contact — corrected to an actual completed embrace).
    THE CLEANEST PROOF THE RULE-62 FIX WORKS AS DESIGNED: 1.B2's own hand-authored `stagingProhibited` (5 items, authored 2026-07-08 — before rule 62's automatic archetype-negative injection existed) turned out to duplicate its archetype's own auto-injected phrases almost one-for-one once compiled together, nearly doubling the beat's Negative line and pushing its word count to 606 (44 words under the 650 hard block — safe, but uncomfortably tight).
