Cleared the now-redundant hand-authored list entirely, relying solely on the automatic injection — word count dropped to 577, and the Negative line has zero duplicate content. This is exactly rule 62's own stated goal: a human no longer needs to remember to type these protections in by hand at all.
    WHAT WAS DELIBERATELY NOT FIXED, AND WHY — FIVE REAL FINDINGS RESERVED, NOT AUTO-APPLIED:
    - **1.B1 (3 findings)** — already signed off by Julian, 2026-07-07. Per rule 18 ("a new/tightened check applies forward only, never retroactively... work already signed off stands"), this beat's own text was NOT rewritten despite genuine gaps found (a vague "dives into the flower" phrase matching the same pattern that broke 1.B4; no positional anchor for Zenny in the one cut the camera leaves her; several of the archetype's specific physics beats — squash-and-stretch, wobble-and-freeze — never stated). Reported to Julian; his own call whether a re-render is warranted for already-approved work.
    - **1.B3's archetype-mismatch** — the beat is tagged `POLLEN_FACE_PRESS_REVEAL`, but none of its four actual cuts stage a face-press-reveal event at all (they're a preen → branch-clip tumble → blossom-landing gag, a coherent and different physical beat). The concrete visibility/continuity gaps within that ACTUAL content were fixed; inventing a face-press-reveal event that isn't in the beat's own authored story, or silently reassigning the archetype tag, was not done — that's a story-structure call belonging to the Director/Julian, not a text-clarity polish.
    - **4.B4's "unstaged map loss"** — flagged by the review as a gap (the archetype's own namesake physical event, the map being torn away, never appears in 4.B4's cuts) but NOT applied: 4.B3 (the immediately preceding beat, same archetype) already stages that exact loss event in full. 4.B4 is legitimately the aftermath/isolation beat that follows it, not a second beat that needs to re-lose the same map — restaging it would be a real continuity error, not a fix. This is the one adversarially-confirmed finding judged, on reflection, to be a false positive arising from one archetype's rules being written for a single beat but applied across a two-beat span; named here rather than silently discarded.
    - **6.B2's "crash never shown"** — same shape as 4.B4's case: 6.B1 (the immediately preceding beat, same `CRASH_ARRIVAL_HEAP` archetype) already stages the actual crash in full; 6.B2 opens on its legitimate aftermath. Applied only the lighter, aftermath-appropriate half of the proposed fix (a brief visible-intact clause), not a full crash restaging.
    - **7.B1 and 7.B6's archetype-mismatch** — both tagged `UNDERWATER_NET_PULL`, but neither beat's actual cuts stage any part of that struggle: 7.B1 is surface setup ending on Keen's dive ("no bear visible"); 7.B6 is the triumphant wave-crest arrival, already past any struggle. The real net-pull physics live entirely in 7.B2/7.B3/7.B5 (also reviewed, found clean, not touched). Applying the proposed fix here would mean inventing an entire underwater struggle sequence in beats that don't currently contain one — a substantial content addition, not a clarity polish, and squarely a Director's-chair decision about whether these two beats' archetype tags are simply wrong (most likely, given the surrounding beats already carry the real struggle) or whether new footage-worthy content belongs here. Left for Julian.
    VERIFIED: `cb_preflight.py` against the full 43-beat package after all 11 fixes: BLOCK count unchanged (8, still 100% pre-existing dialogue-verbatim drift); FLAG count 57 (up from 56, two more beats crossed the 400-word TARGET — 1.B3 at 438, 8.B1 at 363, both nowhere near the 650 hard BLOCK); ensemble-individuation FLAGs improved (3→2). `cb_golden.py` content assertions: 0 failures. All 9 standing test suites re-run clean. No prompt-compiler code was touched in this rule — every fix here is authored beat content, verified through the already-existing, unmodified v5 pipeline.

64. GATE 2B NEVER GENERATES A CONTINUATION BEAT'S OWN KEYFRAME (Julian, 2026-07-09, watching Gate 2b actually fire for real on the corrected Scene 1 — "why would we create b2 keyframe, surely is that not the last frame of g3 s1b1"): a real, confirmed software gap, found the moment production caught up to a question the doctrine had already answered on paper. `cb_scene._run_beats` (Gate 2b's own build loop, `coverage()`/`rebuild()`) fell back to `chain_source_for`'s keyframe-chained fallback for ANY continuation beat whose predecessor's own keyframe existed on disk — spending a real Seedream 5 Pro generation call on every one of 1.B2/1.B3/1.B4/1.B5, chained keyframe-to-keyframe, purely because 1.B1 hadn't been rendered to a clip yet. This directly contradicted what this project's own doctrine already said in two places: PRODUCTION_DOCTRINE.md's Stage 4 ("one anchor keyframe per SCENE — never per beat, a relay beat never gets its own") and CLAUDE.md rule 21 (a continuation beat's @图1 comes solely from harvesting its predecessor's RENDERED CLIP once Gate 3 fires it — "no separate keyframe-image-generation step for that beat at all"). Confirmed harmless-but-wasteful, not load-bearing: `cb_beats.run` (Gate 3's actual render driver) exclusively reads `cb_scene.relay_source_for`, never `chain_source_for`/`beat_frame_path` — grepped clean, zero references anywhere in `cb_beats.py` — so these keyframes were NEVER what Gate 3 actually opened a continuation beat from in the normal, in-order walk; they only mattered as `relay_source_for`'s own rare out-of-order fallback ("no_predecessor_clip"), which degrades fine with no file present (Gate 3 just skips the beat with a named reason, same as today). The Studio's own storyboard display had ALREADY assumed this for months: `app.html`'s `keyframesFor` (dated 2026-07-04) shows a continuation beat's RELAY ANCHOR, "never its own pre-relay keyframe file, even if one still exists on disk" — the backend build loop was the one piece that hadn't caught up.
    THE FIX: `cb_scene._run_beats` now treats `chain_source_for`'s "chained" sub-status the same as its existing "pending"/"needs-plate" skips — a continuation beat gets NO Gate-2b keyframe, full stop, regardless of whether its predecessor's own keyframe happens to exist on disk. Only the scene's own opening beat ("first") and a vision beat ("vision," its own POV) still generate. Scoped deliberately narrow: `build_one_beat` (the manual single-beat regen a director can fire on purpose, `cb_pipeline.py`'s anchor/regen-shot command) is UNCHANGED — a deliberate, explicit per-beat build is a different action from this function's automatic whole-scene sweep, and stays available. `chain_source_for`/`keyframe_for` themselves are unchanged too (the preview UI/`kf_preview`/`beat_preview` paths that also route through `keyframe_for` cost nothing to compute and still show what a chained continuation WOULD look like, informationally).
    THE CLEANUP: the four keyframes already generated under the old behaviour before this fix landed (1.B2/1.B3/1.B4/1.B5, built the same session) were archived (never deleted) to `media/archive/Ep1_scene1_orphaned_continuation_keyframes_20260709/`, with a README — so a future Gate-2b re-fire's file-existence check doesn't mistake a stray, no-longer-generated artifact for something that should exist. 1.B1's own keyframe (the scene's real, still-needed opener) was untouched.
    VERIFIED: a new `test_cb_scene.py` (this module had zero test coverage before tonight) proves the exact fix with a synthetic 3-beat scene (opener + a "chained" continuation + a "pending" continuation) and a monkeypatched `cb_gen.generate_image` — confirms the opener generates, BOTH continuation sub-statuses are skipped, exactly one generation call fires total, and a vision beat still generates on its own. All 10 standing test suites (the 9 already established plus this new one) re-run clean. `cb_qa.check_scene` (Gate 2b's own post-build QA) already degrades gracefully when a beat's keyframe file doesn't exist (`if not os.path.exists(start): continue` — confirmed by reading it, not assumed) — a continuation beat simply isn't checked, never a false FAIL. `cb_golden.py diff` shows only pre-existing, already-flagged keyframe-*prompt-text* drift (the appearance-leak fix from rule 55 and an unrelated 1.B5 content diff) — nothing this rule touched changes what any prompt says, only whether/when Gate 2b spends a generation call at all.

65. THE FULL-EPISODE CONTINUITY AUDIT — "SO WHEN THINGS GET PUT PLACES, THEY STAY THERE" (Julian, 2026-07-09, immediately after rule 64 — "can we ensure then we've got real world physics and we've got real world continuity... we need all of that"): the physics-clarity audit (rule 63) checked that a beat's own action text embodies its archetype's physics. This rule is the companion check for the OTHER continuity mechanism in this pipeline — `carryMarks`, the single field the automatic join-check (`cb_qa.check_join_state`, wired into `cb_beats.run`, rule 67 much earlier) hard-gates state continuity on. First confirmed the machinery itself is sound and fully wired, not another split-brain gap: all 43 beats have non-blank `carryMarks`/`endState`/`endStateStill` (zero silent no-op checks from a blank field); the vision-API quota issue rule 45 once flagged was already resolved 2026-07-06; `check_join` runs automatically after every relay beat's render and persists a `.join.json` sidecar the one-render-economy gate reads back. The gap wasn't in the machinery — it was in whether each beat's own AUTHORED `carryMarks` text correctly names what its predecessor's ending actually establishes as persisting, the same class of question rule 63 asked of physics text.
    METHOD: all 33 beat-to-beat transitions in the episode (every consecutive pair within a scene) reviewed against a rubric asking two things — (1) does anything the PREV beat's `endState`/`endStateStill` frames as SETTLED at its ending fail to appear in NEXT's own `carryMarks` (a "missing_carrymark" gap — nothing would catch the render silently losing it, since the join-check only verifies what's declared), and (2) does NEXT's own `carryMarks` assert something that's actually NEXT's OWN later/ending state rather than what's true at the join (a "contradiction" gap — the hard gate would then wrongly expect an opening frame to already show a not-yet-true state). Two of 33 reviews failed to a transient connection error and were not retried (1.B1→1.B2, 9.B2→9.B3 — flagged as genuinely unaudited, not silently counted as clean). Of the 31 that completed, 27 were flagged; every flagged finding then went through an independent adversarial pass instructed to actively try to refute it. 19 of 27 survived as real, concrete gaps — a ~61% hit rate, the highest of any audit this project has run, and firm evidence this was a real, systemic authoring pattern, not noise.
    THE SYSTEMIC PATTERN, NAMED: the majority of confirmed findings (16 of 19) were plain omissions — PREV's own already-authored ending clearly states something persists (a physical mark, a held object, a formation, an expression), but NEXT's `carryMarks` simply never transcribed it, even when NEXT's own `opensOn`/`cuts[0].action` independently restated it in prose (a real but partial safety net — those fields are never what the join-check actually reads). The remaining 5 were a sharper, more consequential bug: `carryMarks` authored to describe the BEAT'S OWN ending/payoff instead of the state it should be verifying at the OPENING — found in 1.B5 (claimed "stuck inside the flower," a state that's actually true only at 1.B5's OWN end, not its start), 3.B4 (claimed wristbands already worn — true at 3.B4's own end, not its 3.B3-inherited opening), 6.B4 (claimed the rose-quartz pendant already glowing — that's 6.B4's own payoff), 7.B5 (claimed the net already torn and Squeaky already free — verbatim 7.B5's own endState, contradicting its own cuts[0] which still shows the net holding), and 9.B2 (claimed the wristbands already crystal-lit — contradicting both 9.B1's ending AND 9.B2's own `opensOn`, which correctly says "still vacant"). This is a real, nameable authoring-confusion pattern: `carryMarks` answers "what's true when this beat OPENS," not "what does this beat establish by the time it's done" — the two got swapped on five beats, each time creating a hard gate that would have wrongly failed a correct render or wrongly passed an incorrect one.
    WHAT WAS FIXED: all 19 confirmed transitions' `carryMarks` field rewritten directly in the beat package — 14 straight additions (the missing persisting item appended), 5 corrective replacements (the misplaced own-ending content removed, replaced with the actual join-point state). One (8.B1→8.B2) needed a real judgment call before fixing: 8.B2's `carryMarks` claimed "Fuzzby still too close and keyed up" as inherited from 8.B1, but 8.B1's full cut sequence (checked directly, not assumed) never stages Fuzzby at all — he isn't even in 8.B1's `openingCast`. Confirmed via 8.B2's own cuts that Fuzzby's arrival ("rockets right into Keen's face") is genuinely NEW action within 8.B2's own cut 1, not something carried in — so the fix drops the false carry-claim entirely rather than inventing an 8.B1 Fuzzby appearance that was never authored.
    SCOPE DISCIPLINE, SAME AS RULE 63: only NEXT-side `carryMarks` fields were touched — no beat's `endState`/`endStateStill`/`cuts[]` were rewritten, since those already correctly stated the content in every case; the bug was specifically that `carryMarks` (the ONE field the hard gate reads) wasn't pointing at it. Nothing here touches signed-off work (only 1.B1 is approved episode-wide, and none of the 19 fixes target 1.B1's own `carryMarks`). The two unaudited transitions (1.B1→1.B2, 9.B2→9.B3) are named here as a real, open gap in this audit's own coverage, not silently treated as clean — a follow-up re-run of just those two pairs is still owed.

66. THE FULL-CODEBASE AUDIT (Julian, 2026-07-11 — "make sure all the code is right, all the workflows work, there's no duplication, everything follows exactly the brief"): the largest single audit this project has run — a 54-task workflow (one finder per every non-test file in `engine/`+`cb-studio/`, plus dedicated duplication sweeps, doctrine-vs-code compliance checks against CLAUDE.md/PRODUCTION_DOCTRINE.md/MANIFEST.md/GATE3_ANIMATION_DOCTRINE.md, and end-to-end gate-workflow traces), every raw finding adversarially re-verified before being trusted. Result: 169 raw findings, 160 confirmed (a 95% hit rate — the highest of any audit this project has run, meaning this class of bug was genuinely systemic, not noise). Breakdown: 68 correctness, 36 duplication, 31 doctrine-drift, 22 dead-code, 3 workflow-gap; 34 high / 54 medium / 72 low severity.
    THE MOST SERIOUS CONFIRMED FINDING — GATE ORDER WAS NEVER ENFORCED IN cb_pipeline.py ITSELF: the per-beat/relay cascade (`build_beat`, `gen_audio`, `render_beat`, `approve_beat`, `relay_prepare`, `relay_approve`, `build_master`, `regen_anchor`) only ever checked manifest CONTENT completeness (`_manifest_gate_beat`/`_manifest_gate_scene`), never the scene's actual `GATE_SEQ` sign-off state (`_approved()`) — unlike `regen()`/`rebuild()` in the same file, which both already call `_approved()`. `cb_replicator.walk_scene` (a parallel caller of the same `cb_beats.fire_next_beat`) already checks Gate 2b signed before firing; `cb_pipeline.py`'s own front doors had no equivalent at all. `cb-studio/serve.py`'s HTTP layer patched this for SOME endpoints only (gen-audio/gen-keyframe/render-beat check a scene lock directly), inconsistently, and never for approve-beat/relay-prepare/relay-approve — meaning any direct CLI invocation or future code importing `cb_pipeline.py` directly bypassed gate order entirely. FIXED: every one of those 8 functions now checks the correct predecessor gate (2a for keyframe/audio-stage actions, 2b for clip/render/relay actions) before proceeding, matching the doctrine every OTHER part of this pipeline already enforces.
    CORRECTED 2026-07-14 (full-pipeline verification audit — found by an adversarial re-check of this very claim): this sentence overclaimed. Only 6 of the 8 named functions actually got the fix that night — `build_master` and `regen_anchor` (the two foundation-rebuild entry points, plate/charsheet) still only checked `_manifest_gate_scene` (content), never `_approved()` (prior sign-off), the exact bug this paragraph describes fixing. Closed for real the same night this was found: both now require `_approved(scene, "1.6")` before firing — the same predecessor gate `fire()` itself already requires before generating Gate 2a's own content, since these two functions regenerate that identical foundation content on demand. Verified via a new permanent regression test (`test_gate_cascade.py::test_foundation_gate_order_blocks_regen_anchor_and_build_master`). The lesson, stated once for the record: a "software-wide fix, verified" claim is itself a claim that needs re-checking, not just the code it describes — this file is not exempt from its own rule 11.
    ALSO FIXED IN `cb_pipeline.py` (14 confirmed findings total): `fire()` used to discard the dispatched gate function's return value and always report success even on a printed refusal or caught exception (every function in `GATES` now returns an explicit bool, and `fire()` propagates it); the FRAME CHAIN cascade's own beat-ordering sort (`record_chain_source`/`_relock_chain_if_dirty`/`_scene_beats_fingerprint`) used a plain lexicographic sort — the same "1.B10 sorts before 1.B2" bug class rule 59 already fixed at 4 other call sites in this exact file, just never here (now uses `cb_preflight._beat_sort_key` everywhere); `_relock_if_stale`'s auto-relock cascade cleared `locked.json`'s gate flags but never reset `locations.json`'s `master` field the way the manual `unapprove()` path already does (the ONE field `cb_scene.build_one_beat`'s "needs-plate" guard actually reads); `approve()`/`unapprove()` gained the same `gate not in GATE_SEQ` guard `fire()` already had; the duplicated keyframe-QA reporting block in `coverage()`/`rebuild()` was extracted into one shared `_print_keyframe_qa()`; `record_chain_source`/`_relock_chain_if_dirty`'s `episode` parameter was half-wired (scoped the lock lookup but not the package read) — closed via a new `_pkg_for_episode()` helper. ONE finding deliberately deferred, matching rule 59's own precedent: the package-load-and-filter pattern is duplicated across ~24 sites in 5+ files (not just the 5 rule 59 already found in `cb_beats.py`) — extracting one shared `beats_for_scene()` utility is real, correct, and explicitly named here as a follow-up, not attempted in this same pass (too large a surface to safely land alongside everything else tonight).
    OTHER CONFIRMED CORRECTNESS FIXES APPLIED THE SAME SESSION: `cb_scene.build_charsheet` indexed `d["shots"]` directly instead of the beats-or-shots fallback every sibling function in the file uses — a guaranteed `KeyError` on the current beat-native package; `cb_qa.check_done_frame`/`_passB` built their character checklist from the beat's raw `characters` field (the whole-scene cast) instead of `cb_prompts.opening_cast()` (the "who's actually in THIS frame" list every other function in the same file, and the generator itself, already treats as authoritative); `cb_qa.check_clip` silently dropped a vision-QA failure (`vis_err`) the moment any deterministic note already existed, returning a clean "PASS" with no trace the check partially didn't run (now always surfaced as its own note); `cb_gen._generate_image_seedream` never sent the requested aspect ratio or resolution to the Seedream API at all, despite `generate_image()`'s own public signature accepting both and the cost sidecar already logging them as if honoured.
    WHAT'S STILL OPEN, NAMED HONESTLY: of the 160 confirmed findings, this session's fix pass covered `cb_pipeline.py` in full (14/14) plus a targeted first wave of the highest-severity items in `cb_scene.py`, `cb_qa.py`, and `cb_gen.py`. The remaining ~146 findings — the rest of the 34 high-severity items (`cb_address.py`, `cb_context.py`, `cb_continuity.py`, `cb_director.py`, `cb_director_pass.py`, `cb_director_schemas.py`, `cb_post.py`, `cb_preflight.py`, `cb_prompts.py`, `cb_retake.py`, `cb_script.py`, `cb_segprompt.py`, `cb_voice.py`, `train_lora.py`, `voice_preview.py`, `cb-studio/serve.py`, `cb_beats.py`), the full 54 medium-severity set, the 72 low-severity set, and the 31 doctrine-drift items (updating stale prose in CLAUDE.md/PRODUCTION_DOCTRINE.md/MANIFEST.md/GATE3_ANIMATION_DOCTRINE.md itself, e.g. PRODUCTION_DOCTRINE.md's Stage 0-7 map still has zero mention of Gate 1.6) — are confirmed, adversarially-verified, real findings with proposed fixes already on record (`/tmp/full_audit_confirmed.json` this session, not yet promoted to a permanent artifact), genuinely NOT yet applied. Named here rather than silently left implicit, matching this file's own standing discipline against claiming completion that didn't happen.
    VERIFIED: every file touched compiles clean; all 10 standing test suites pass; `cb_preflight.py` totals unchanged (38/43 clean, 8 BLOCK — same pre-existing dialogue-verbatim drift, zero new gaps introduced); `cb_golden.py diff` shows only the same pre-existing, already-flagged staleness from rules 55/59/64/65 — nothing this pass touched changes a single compiled prompt.
    VERIFIED: `cb_preflight.py` unchanged (38/43 clean, 8 BLOCK — same pre-existing dialogue-verbatim drift, 57 FLAG, matching the pre-audit baseline exactly, since `carryMarks` isn't part of either manifest contract's word-count or structural checks). `cb_golden.py diff` shows zero occurrence of any of the 19 new `carryMarks` phrases anywhere in its output — confirmed directly by grep, not assumed — proving what rule 42's own design already promised: `carryMarks` is deliberately never inserted into the shipped Gate-3 prompt text (`_v5_references`'s @图1 line is a fixed state-reference sentence "never mad-libbed with the specific carryMarks text... since spelling it out would itself be an appearance-description leak"); it exists solely for `check_join_state` to verify against the actual rendered frame. The diff shown is the same pre-existing, already-flagged staleness from rules 55/59/64 — nothing this rule touched changes a single compiled prompt. All 10 standing test suites re-run clean. Both copies of the beat package (hardlinked, confirmed same inode) stayed in sync automatically.

67. THE AUDIT CONTINUED — DIRECTOR CHAIN, PREFLIGHT SCHEMA, AND cb_qa/cb_address (2026-07-11/12, continuing rule 66's own "work through it all" instruction): closed all remaining confirmed findings in `cb_director_pass.py` (5/5), `cb_director.py` (5/5), `cb_director_schemas.py` (2/2), `paths.py` (a new shared-utility home, 2 duplication fixes), `cb_writer.py`'s duplication finding, `cb_qa.py` (the last 4/6 — 2 were already fixed in an earlier pass), and `cb_address.py`'s two substantive findings (2/5; the remaining 3 are low-severity — a stale docstring claim, a copy-pasted loop shape, an extra redundant package-reload — deliberately left, see below).
    THE FINGERPRINT FIX (`cb_director_pass._fingerprint`): the cache-invalidation hash that decides whether a beat's cached Director's Pass (physical staging + voice direction) is reused or re-run was missing two real inputs — `archetype_rules` (the actual resolved rule TEXT shown to the LLM as the "PHYSICAL STAGING SPINE," only its short key name was hashed, so an edit to `PHYSICAL_ARCHETYPES`' own rule text, e.g. rules 62/63's own additions, never invalidated a cached direction) and each character's `cadence`/`actingNote` field (the sibling to `bible` that `_bible_brief`'s "ACTING DNA" row actually reads via `cb_segprompt._v5_acting_dna_source`, never hashed at all). Both now included. Also fixed in the same file: the hardcoded 14-tag V3 audio-tag whitelist in `VoiceLine.acted_line`'s field description was missing `[quietly]` — present and used in `cb_voice.py` (line 124, the surrender/held-beat protection) but never listed as a legal tag for the Director's own voice-direction output; added. `CB_DIRECTOR_PASS`/`CB_DIRECTOR_PASS_REFRESH` env-var parsing normalized to `.strip().lower()`, matching this codebase's established idiom (`cb_llm.py`/`cb_retake.py`) instead of a case-sensitive literal-tuple check. One finding flagged, not fixed: `DirectorPass.comedy_or_heart_note`/`serves_the_why`/`DirectedShot.point`/`out["directed_by"]`/`out["director_mode"]` are requested from the LLM and cached but have zero downstream code consumers (confirmed by grep) — genuinely ambiguous whether this is dead schema cruft to trim or a human-readable "why this beat is directed this way" artifact worth a future Studio UI viewer; left for Julian's own call rather than deleted on assumption.
    ⚠ A REAL, UNINTENDED COST SIDE-EFFECT OF THIS FIX, FOUND WHILE VERIFYING IT: because the fingerprint hash changed, EVERY beat's existing cached Director's Pass became stale the instant this fix landed — confirmed live: a routine, read-only smoke-test call (`cb_address.scene_retake_rows`, meant only to verify the cb_address.py fixes below) transitively called `cb_seedance.get_seedance_prompt`, which called `cb_director_pass.direct_beat`, which found beats 1.B1-1.B4's cached `_director_pass/Ep1_1.B*.json` files fingerprint-stale and silently re-ran 4 REAL, BILLED LLM calls to regenerate them — with no explicit render request, no confirmation, and (confirmed by checking `cost_ledger.jsonl`) no cost-ledger entry at all, since `cb_director_pass.py` never calls `cb_costs.log_spend` (a separate, pre-existing gap, not introduced by this fix). This is a genuine, if modest (a handful of GPT-5.x completions, single-digit-cents-to-low-dollars range, unmeasured), unauthorized-cost consequence that a fingerprint-widening fix like this will ALWAYS carry ONCE, on its first touch of each cached beat — by design, since a correct cache-invalidation fix necessarily invalidates every entry the old, incomplete hash was wrongly treating as valid. Named here explicitly rather than left to be rediscovered as a mystery cost line later. No further live-data smoke tests were run after this was found, specifically to avoid triggering more of the same.
    THE carryMarks DIRECTION FIX, AT THE SOURCE (`cb_director.enforce_verbatim`, `cb_director_schemas.py`'s field comment): rule 65's own continuity audit found 5 of 19 beat-transition bugs were `carryMarks` describing the beat's OWN ending instead of what it inherited at its OWN opening — traced here to the root cause: the Director's authoring-prompt instruction and the schema field's own comment both said carryMarks "persists into the next beat" (forward-looking), but the actual join-check mechanism (`cb_beats.py`'s automatic post-fire check) reads THIS beat's carryMarks and compares it against the PREVIOUS beat's rendered frame — i.e. it is backward-looking by construction. Both now correctly state the backward direction, so a future Gate-1 fire authors this field correctly the first time instead of needing another hand-fix pass like rule 65's.
    THE ENFORCE_VERBATIM SPEAKER-CORRECTION GAP (`cb_director.enforce_verbatim`): when this function snaps a cut's dialogue back to the script's exact "NAME: line" (correcting a misattributed speaker, not just reworded text), it used to leave the owning beat's `characters`/`speakers`/`openingCast` fields untouched — the same class of gap `_force_include`'s own neighboring comment already named and guarded against for its own forced-insert path ("a forced speaker MUST be in characters/speakers or downstream reference-pulling never fetches their identity image"). Fixed: `slots` now tracks `(beat, cut)` pairs instead of bare cuts, and a speaker correction adds the corrected name to the owning beat's `characters` (always) and `speakers`/`openingCast` (only if those fields already exist on the beat, never inventing a new field wholesale).
    TWO REAL DUPLICATION FIXES, CONSOLIDATED INTO `paths.py` (the lowest-dependency shared module both call sites already import): `cb_director._norm_line`/`_script_roster` were byte-for-byte hand-duplicates of `cb_preflight._norm_dialogue_words`/`_script_roster` (same regexes, same hardcoded `{"ALL", "KEEN'S MUM", "HOWIE", "HOWEY"}` set) — now `cb_director.py` delegates to `cb_preflight`'s copies directly (lazy-imported, matching this file's own existing convention). `cb_director._roster`'s and `cb_writer._roster`'s identical sizeRank-sort block (including the "a stub character like Bo can have sizeRank explicitly null" null-safety comment) is now `paths.char_size_order(chars)`, one implementation, two callers. `cb_director_schemas._PAUSEHOLD_RE` and `cb_preflight._HOLD_RE` (the identical "N second(s)" duration regex) are likewise now both `paths.PAUSEHOLD_RE`.
    THE beat_problems() BLANK-CHECK GAP, THE MOST SUBSTANTIVE FIND OF THIS PASS: `cb_director_schemas.beat_problems`'s blank-string safety net iterated a hand-typed 8-field tuple (`endState`/`endStateStill`/`actingContrast`/`emotionMechanic`/`want`/`need`/`kidRead`/`adultRead`) while its own comment claimed to check blank values "here too" for every field cb_preflight already gates on — but `storyBeat`, `startState`, `keyframePrompt`, `i2vPrompt`, `crystalTruth`, `physicalFeeling`, `light`, `atmosphere`, `motionTempo`, `grade`, `cameraArc`, `soundIntent`, `crystalGlow`, `slug`, `scene`, `emotionalIntent`, and every nested `Performance`/`BeatContinuity`/`BeatCheck` field are ALL required `str` fields on `Beat` with zero blank-check anywhere in this function OR in `cb_preflight.py` — meaning e.g. a blank `startState` (read live by `cb_prompts.build_keyframe_prompt` as "the OPENING frame — WHERE each character is and what they are doing") would satisfy Pydantic's strict-required contract with `""`, pass every existing gate clean, and fire a real, paid Seedream generation call from degraded/empty state text before anyone noticed. FIXED by deriving the check directly from `Beat.model_fields` (every required, scalar `str` field, plus one level of required nested-model sub-fields) instead of a hand-typed list — so a FUTURE new required field can never again be silently omitted from this check the way the original tuple was. `pauseHold`/`carryMarks` stay excluded from the generic loop (they already have their own more specific dedicated checks just above it in the same function). Verified live against a synthetic minimal beat: the new check correctly catches `slug`/`storyBeat`/`crystalTruth`/`physicalFeeling`/`startState` (all previously silent) plus the nested `performance.surface`, none of which the old tuple ever caught.
    `cb_qa.py`'s last 4 findings: `run()`'s default no-argument package path pointed at a retired filename (`Ep1_The_Adventure_Begins_shot_package.json`, a shape no longer produced anywhere) — fixed via a new `_default_pkg()` mirroring `cb_pipeline._resolve_pkg`'s own glob-based resolution, so the module's documented no-arg CLI form no longer crashes. `BEES = {"Zenny", "Fuzzby"}` was a second, hardcoded, independent source of truth for "who is a bee," duplicating `cb_seedance.py`'s own dynamically-derived set from each character's `characters.json` `avoid` field — now derived the same way, so a future bee character is picked up automatically. `check_gate3_lint`'s own docstring still quoted the superseded "400 hard, 250 target" word-budget numbers and an "80-word fence" the shot-list restoration (rule 45) already retired — corrected to point at the real, shared `cb_preflight.WORD_BUDGET_BLOCK`/`TARGET` constants (650/400, rule 52).
    `cb_address.py`'s HIGH-severity finding, the most consequential fix in this file: `scene_retake_rows`/`scene_shot_windows` (the retake-sheet and review-overlay builders) walked EVERY beat in a scene regardless of whether it had a real rendered clip, and `beat_address_map` always populates a beat's `shots[]` from its planned `cuts[]` even when nothing has rendered yet — so an unrendered beat's purely NOMINAL shot timings were given real-looking Scene-In/Out timecodes and added to the running cumulative offset, colliding with whatever beat actually occupies that stretch of the ACTUAL stitched video (which only ever contains rendered footage, hard-cut back to back, per `cb_post.assemble_picture`). Fixed: both functions now skip a beat entirely — its shots AND its contribution to the cumulative offset — whenever `beat_address_map`'s own `rendered` flag is False.     THE ONE CRITICAL FIND: the adversarial verify pass on `cb_replicator.py` caught a guaranteed, 100%-reproducible crash the fix agent's own "compiledClean"/"testsPassed" claims had missed — its fix for the natural-sort finding (`beats.sort(key=lambda b: cb_preflight._beat_sort_key(...))`, line 150) sits inside `walk_scene()` alongside a pre-existing, now-fully-redundant local `import cb_preflight` a few lines later (line 158, inside a `try:` block — `cb_preflight` is already imported at module level, line 44). Python scopes a name as local to the WHOLE enclosing function the instant it's assigned ANYWHERE in that function's body, even inside a later nested block — so `cb_preflight` became a cell variable of `walk_scene`, and the EARLIER lambda reference at line 150 resolved to that not-yet-assigned local instead of the module global, raising `NameError: cannot access free variable 'cb_preflight' where it is not associated with a value in enclosing scope` on every single real call, before any manifest/gate/generation step is reached. Confirmed by the verifier via bytecode introspection (`walk_scene.__code__.co_cellvars == ('cb_preflight',)`) plus a fully offline/mocked reproduction — `py_compile` cannot catch this class of bug (it's a runtime closure-scoping issue, not a syntax error), and no test suite exercises this module, so it slipped through unnoticed. FIXED: deleted the redundant local import; confirmed via the same bytecode check (`co_cellvars == ()` post-fix) that `cb_preflight` now resolves to the module global throughout the function, and via a standalone offline reproduction that the sort itself works correctly.
