THE WORDLESSHELD MISDIAGNOSIS, CORRECTED (2026-07-12, live Gate-3 fires on the restored Scene 1): rule 61's "transient ElevenLabs contention" diagnosis for a beat's voice track silently coming back empty (no exception, no diagnostic) is WRONG for 1.B1 and 1.B2 specifically — traced to ground this time, not assumed. Both beats had `wordlessHeld` stored as the JSON STRING `"false"` (not the boolean `false`) in the live production package — a value that is TRUTHY in Python, so `cb_voice.build_dialogue_track`'s guard (`if shot.get("wordlessHeld"): return None` — rule 9's "the ONE wordless held beat per episode, silence carries it") fired on every single attempt for both beats, returning `None` cleanly before ever reaching the actual dialogue-synthesis code — no exception raised because none was thrown; no diagnostic printed because a deliberate design return isn't a failure. This is exactly why nothing about the symptom ever pointed at itself: `_build_voice_track_with_retry`'s own retry (rule 61) faithfully retried the SAME wrong guard twice and got the SAME clean `None` twice, indistinguishable from a genuine API blip from the caller's side.
    FOUND BY: reproducing the failure directly (not from logs) — `_resolve_turns` traced clean with valid turns when called standalone, but `build_dialogue_track` itself returned `None` with zero internal prints; monkey-patching `_resolve_turns`/`_voice_dir_lookup` to trace calls showed NEITHER was ever invoked, meaning the function returned before line 379 — i.e. at the `wordlessHeld` guard, the only earlier return point. `repr(shot.get("wordlessHeld"))` on the real beat data showed `'false'` (a `str`), confirmed by `type()` — a scan of the WHOLE 43-beat package found exactly 2 beats (1.B1, 1.B2 — the same two Scene-1 beats hand-edited most heavily across this session's history) carrying the string; the other 41 beats correctly hold real JSON booleans (40 `False`, 1 genuine `True`).
    FIXED: (1) the two beats' `wordlessHeld` values corrected to the real JSON boolean `false` in the live package. (2) `cb_voice.build_dialogue_track`'s guard hardened per this file's own "solve the problem, not the symptom" discipline (rule 62) — no longer a bare truthiness check; only `True` (the real bool) or the string `"true"` (case-insensitive) counts as wordless-held, so a stray `"false"` string (or any other non-empty junk value) can never again silently short-circuit a beat's whole voice pass. Verified live: re-running the exact production voice-build path for 1.B1 (`cb_beats._build_voice_track_with_retry`) now succeeds cleanly — "V3 Text-to-Dialogue — 2 turns acted in one in-context pass" — and writes the real `media/vo_Ep1_1.B1.mp3`.
    A SWEEP FOR THE SAME PATTERN ELSEWHERE (rule 11) FOUND A FALSE POSITIVE, NOT A SECOND BUG: a full-package scan for any string value equal to `"true"`/`"false"` (case-insensitive) also flagged 29 beats' `comedyMode` field holding the string `"TRUE"` — but `comedyMode` is a genuine two-value STRING ENUM (`"BIG"` | `"TRUE"` | null, rule 33/42's own comedy-mode doctrine — `"TRUE"` here means "small and real / heart," not a boolean), and every consumer in the codebase (`cb_director.py`, `cb_preflight.py`, `cb_qa.py`, `cb_prompts.py`) reads it via an explicit `str(...).upper() == "BIG"` string comparison, never bare truthiness — confirmed safe, not touched.
    Verified: `cb_voice.py` compiles clean; all 5 relevant standing suites (`test_cb_voice.py`, `test_gate_cascade.py`, `test_unapprove_locks.py`, `test_cb_beats.py`, `test_cb_preflight.py`) re-run clean, zero regressions; the real production beat package's hardlink confirmed still in sync across both paths.

71. WHY LIVE FIRES KEEP FINDING NEW THINGS, AND TWO PERMANENT CLOSES (2026-07-13, Julian's direct question — "why do we keep finding these issues... why aren't they being rectified"): the honest answer, not a defense — tonight was genuinely the FIRST time this pipeline had been fired live, end-to-end, against Scene 1's current real data. Nearly every prior audit in this file (dozens of them) is STATIC checking: reading code, unit tests against synthetic data, compiling prompts without firing them. Static checking proves internal consistency; it cannot prove a beat renders correctly, because an entire class of bug only exists at the intersection of real data and real execution (rule 70's wordlessHeld string-vs-bool — invisible sitting in JSON, only breaks when the exact truthiness-checking code path actually runs). That class of bug needed a live fire to surface, by construction — no amount of additional static review would have found it. Two of tonight's three found-and-fixed bugs (rule 70, and the one below) are exactly this class. The THIRD kind of issue found tonight (ElevenLabs mispronouncing "nuts") is not a code bug at all — it's generation-model variance, and the existing one-retake protocol (rule 28) is already the correct, already-built answer for it; no static check can predict a model's own audio output before it's actually generated.
    THE SECOND PERMANENT CLOSE, SAME NIGHT — THE STRIPPER'S DANGLING-WORD BUG (first found live firing 1.B2's real prompt, rule under this same session): `cb_segprompt._v5_strip_speed_adjectives` removes generic pace words (chaotic, fast, hyper, manic, frantic, speed, etc. — rule 42's own adjective-chaos-ban doctrine, made a real mechanical edit, not just a lint) from a beat's action/framing text. The beat text authored tonight said "at full chaotic speed," — BOTH "chaotic" and "speed" are on the strip list, and removing both left the broken fragment "at full," shipped into the real Seedance prompt. Fixed in the beat's own text immediately (dropped the generic phrase entirely — "rockets"/"barrels" already carry the speed, the MORE correct way to write it under this pipeline's own doctrine). Then closed PERMANENTLY: `cb_qa.check_gate3_lint` gained a new hard-BLOCK check — but the FIRST version of it was itself found to be broken before landing (a bug caught DURING verification, not after): it pattern-matched the ALREADY-STRIPPED output for a fixed shape (`preposition immediately before punctuation`), and that shape never matches "at full,", since the ORPHANED word ("full") sits between the preposition and the punctuation — tested against the real bug and it silently passed, proven with a direct repro before it ever shipped. Rewritten to check the ROOT CAUSE instead of guessing every possible broken RESULT: scans each cut's own RAW action/framing text (and the beat's endState) for two-or-more of the stripper's own listed words within one word of each other — "chaotic speed" (0 words apart) or "wildly fast" (adjacent) is ALWAYS a fragile construction regardless of what specific grammar the stripper leaves behind. Verified three ways: (1) reconstructing tonight's exact original broken text and confirming the check now correctly BLOCKS it (`cut 1 action: two adjacent generic pace words ('full chaotic speed, humming') will both be stripped...`); (2) running it against all 43 real beats in the live production package — zero false positives; (3) all 10 standing test suites re-run clean.
    A THIRD PERMANENT CLOSE, ONE LEVEL UP FROM RULE 70: `cb_preflight.check_beat_field_types`, new — every Beat field the schema (`cb_director_schemas.Beat.model_fields`) declares as a real `bool` (derived from the schema itself, never hand-typed, matching rule 47's own "a future field is automatically covered" discipline — currently `wordlessHeld`/`beautyMoment`) is now checked for actually BEING a Python `bool`, not merely a truthy/falsy value. Rule 70's fix closed the ONE call site (`cb_voice.build_dialogue_track`) that happened to do a bare truthiness check on `wordlessHeld`; this check closes the gap one level higher — a wrong-typed value now BLOCKS at preflight, before a beat is ever fired, instead of silently degrading at whichever specific call site does the truthy check. Verified: correctly BLOCKS a synthetic `wordlessHeld: 'false'` (string) beat with a named, explicit reason; passes clean on real bool data; the live production package (already correct after rule 70's data fix) shows zero new BLOCKs — 39/43 beats clean, same totals as before, confirming no regression.
    WHAT THIS DOES AND DOES NOT CLAIM: this closes the exact two bug classes found live tonight, permanently, so the identical failure can never recur silently. It does not claim no other live-fire-only bug exists — by this rule's own opening argument, that class of bug is only found by firing, not by writing more static checks in advance of firing. The honest posture, restated from rule 28: mechanical checks close what a machine CAN verify; they were never meant to, and cannot, substitute for actually running the thing.

72. THE CREATIVITY-VS-RULES AUDIT + THE DIRECTOR-FIDELITY TRACE (2026-07-13, direct answer to Julian's own framing — "make sure the script is being delivered correctly by the director into the storyboard... the director's part with the Seedance prompting, that is where I think he is falling down"): two independent background audits, queued earlier the same session, both landed together and converged on the same diagnosis rule 71 already named — the v5 prompt pipeline has real, currently-shipping bugs that no static test had caught, because every prior audit checked code shape, never the ACTUAL compiled text against ACTUAL rendered footage.
    AUDIT ONE — 49 mechanical rules/strippers/lints in the v5 pipeline, each fact-checked against real production data (not read-and-guessed): 34 of 49 confirmed real by an independent adversarial re-check (22 RISKY — actively corrupting shipped prompt text today; 12 TOO_RESTRICTIVE — false-positive blocks that would silently refuse legitimate creative content). AUDIT TWO — a direct trace of WHY 1.B1's real rendered clip shows Zenny's apparent size drifting to match/exceed Fuzzby's by the clip's end (the defect this session's own frame-by-frame inspection of the real footage first found): traced the size fact through every stage — authored correctly in `characters.json` (sizeRank/size), authored correctly in the beat's own `visibility_rule`, and correctly present in the Director's Pass's own camera direction — but NEVER reaching the compiled Seedance prompt at all, because the one field that could have carried it (`characters.json`'s `cadence`, quoted verbatim into the Acting DNA block) has its own `(bigger, male)`/`(smaller, female)` parenthetical mechanically stripped by `_META_PAREN_WORDS`, a regex built for genuine meta-production commentary that happened to also list "bigger"/"smaller"/"male"/"female." This is the literal, concrete shape of "the director's part with the Seedance prompting falling down": correct upstream authoring, a real compilation gap.
    FIVE FIXES APPLIED THE SAME NIGHT, ALL VERIFIED AGAINST REAL PRODUCTION DATA, NOT SYNTHETIC EXAMPLES:
    - **THE SIZE CLAUSE, BUILT WHERE IT NEVER EXISTED** (`cb_segprompt._v5_size_clause`, wired into `_v5_references`/Block 2): mirrors `cb_prompts.size_line`'s own already-established precedent for keyframes ("Fuzzby is the BIGGER bee, Zenny the SMALLER") — a relative-size instruction is staging/blocking, the same category as stating screen-left/screen-right position, never the appearance description rule 5 forbids (which governs HOW a character looks, not WHERE they sit in scale next to a co-star). This is that exact, already-precedented mechanism's video-prompt sibling, which simply never existed here before. Verified live: 1.B1's and 1.B2's real shipped prompts now both carry "SIZE: Fuzzby renders visibly larger than Zenny in every shared frame — hold this scale relationship throughout, never flattened or reversed."
    - **FIDELITYALLOCATION NOW ACTUALLY RESPECTED** (`_v5_active_cast`, via a new `_v5_fidelity_split` helper): the director's own authored `fidelityAllocation.primary`/`secondary`/`economized` (rule 50) previously had ZERO effect on which characters got full Acting DNA treatment in the shipped prompt — confirmed live on real beat 6.B4, where Fuzzby/Zenny were explicitly marked `economized` (deliberately de-emphasized for a serious ensemble Crystal Call beat) but still shipped full "manic, staccato, HYPER"/"deadpan, dry, understated" Acting DNA because blocking text happened to name them, while the beat's own designated `secondary` performer (Howey) got nothing but a background reference line — the exact reverse of the beat's own recorded creative intent, and precisely the "ensemble wallpaper" failure mode `fidelityAllocation` was built (rule 50) to prevent. Fixed: `primary`/`secondary` now force ACTIVE regardless of text mentions; `economized` now forces BACKGROUND regardless of text mentions. Verified live: 6.B4 now correctly ships Howey's own Acting DNA and correctly backgrounds Fuzzby/Zenny.
    - **THE POSSESSIVE-TAIL FALSE-POSITIVE, FIXED** (same function): "Keen's Mum"'s active/background detection matched the bare substring "mum" with no word boundary — a cut naming "chrysanthemum" (or, confirmed live in the real package, "maximum" in 10.B1's own action text) would wrongly assert Keen's Mum is present and acting in a scene she has nothing to do with. Now a real word-boundary regex match.
    - **THE SPEED-ADJECTIVE STRIPPER, WIDENED FROM RULE 71's NARROWER FIX**: rule 71's own fix only caught two adjacent strippable words colliding ("chaotic speed"). This audit found the bug is broader — a SINGLE strip breaks grammar whenever the matched word is the head of a comparative ("faster than X" → "than X," nothing left to compare) or the object of a fixed intensifying preposition phrase ("at full speed" → "at full," nothing left to modify) — confirmed live, currently shipping broken, in 6+ real beats (5.B3, 4.B3, 6.B2, 10.B2, 1.B5). `_SPEED_ADJ_RE` now excludes a match immediately preceded by "too "/"at full " or immediately followed by " than" — these constructions carry real comparative meaning worth keeping, matching the audit's own recommendation that the more creatively faithful fix is to leave them untouched, not to invent grammar-repair logic.
    - **THE "no a X" NEGATIVE-LINE GRAMMAR BUG, FIXED**: `_v5_negative_line`'s "no " prefix (Julian's own 2026-07-08 ruling, matching the standing eleven's article-free cadence) never stripped a leading indefinite article first, so a phrase authored/resolved as "a clean face" or "an easy pull" shipped as "no a clean face" / "no an easy pull" — confirmed live on 22 of 43 real beats. `_LEADING_ARTICLE_RE` now strips a leading "a "/"an " before the "no " prefix lands; verified zero of 43 real beats still carry the broken form.
    EVERY FIX VERIFIED THE SAME WAY: compiled against the REAL production package (not a hand-typed approximation), the specific real beats the audit named checked directly for the fix taking effect, a new permanent regression test added to `test_cb_segprompt.py` for each (never a one-off manual check, matching rule 3/this file's own standing "software-wide fixes" discipline), and all 10 standing test suites plus `cb_preflight.py`'s totals plus `cb_golden.py diff` re-run clean after every single change — `cb_preflight.py`'s BLOCK count unchanged at 8 (same pre-existing, unrelated dialogue-verbatim drift), FLAG count unchanged at 56 net (the two-word SIZE-clause addition to two-bee beats and the removed dead words roughly offset); `cb_golden.py diff` shows only the same pre-existing, already-flagged keyframe-builder staleness this session has repeatedly confirmed — nothing in this rule's own changes touches the keyframe/still-image prompt path at all, only the v5 video-prompt compiler.
    WHAT REMAINS OPEN, NAMED HONESTLY, NOT SILENTLY CLAIMED DONE: this audit confirmed 34 real findings; this rule closes 5 of them — the ones with the clearest, safest, most mechanical fixes and the most direct bearing on Julian's own question. 29 remain, a mix of further RISKY grammar/leak bugs in `_strip_spoken_words` (a quoted fragment functioning as a sentence's subject/object corrupts meaning, not just grammar, when stripped — confirmed live on real beat 10.B1), `_v5_cap_sentences` (can truncate mid-thought), `_MANNERISMS_APPEARANCE_DROP` (the same class of over-broad strip-list bug as the size-parenthetical one, applied to other mannerisms text), and several TOO_RESTRICTIVE false-positive BLOCKs in `cb_preflight.py`/`cb_qa.py` (a `pauseHold` regex that greps the wrong number out of a sentence; a speaker-order check that wrongly blocks legitimate "BOTH:" dialogue; `check_scene_dialogue_verbatim`'s own auto-fix contradicting Gate 1.6's explicit dialogue-revision exception; `check_characters_technical` too strict on a deliberate stub character) — the full raw findings are preserved in this session's own workflow transcript (`/private/tmp/claude-501/.../ce53b1b6.../tasks/wgs22wdqi.output`, run `wf_a3a97815-230`) for a follow-up pass, not lost, not silently dropped.

73. WHY RULE 72 WASN'T ENOUGH — A GENUINE GENERAL SAFETY NET ATTEMPTED, PROVEN UNSAFE, AND A HONEST METHODOLOGY SHIFT (2026-07-13, Julian's direct pushback — "why are we going back over these, we should have this right by now"): the honest answer, not a defense. Rule 72 closed the audit's own top-5 recommended fixes. It did NOT close the whole 34-finding list, and — separately, more importantly — no prior fix in this file's whole history has ever been VERIFIED as exhaustive, because none was actually tested against every real character/beat data point the mechanism touches; each was tested against the specific example the audit or the live fire happened to surface. That is the real, structural reason "one more" keeps turning up: sampling, not exhaustive coverage.
    THE FIRST ATTEMPT — A GENERAL "DANGLING WORD" DETECTOR — FAILED ITS OWN TEST, AND THAT FAILURE IS THE ACTUAL LESSON: tried building ONE general regex to scan any final compiled text for a "trailing function word before punctuation" (the shape every confirmed bug so far shares). Ran it against the REAL, un-modified, currently-correct authored prose in the production package before trusting it — and it produced 23 false positives on legitimate English: "taking them in," "close by," "step in," "watching in" are all correct phrasal-verb constructions that legitimately end a clause on a preposition/particle. A general scanner cannot tell "at full," (broken — nothing follows "full") from "step in." (correct — "in" completes "step in") without real grammar parsing, which this pipeline does not have and should not try to fake with a brittle heuristic. Building it anyway would have created a NEW category of false-positive BLOCK — the exact TOO_RESTRICTIVE failure mode rule 72's own audit already flagged 12 instances of. Abandoned, correctly, before it shipped — this is what "verify before trusting" is supposed to catch, and it did.
    THE ACTUAL FIX: STRIP-SITE-LOCAL VERIFICATION, PER TRANSFORM, AGAINST EVERY REAL DATA POINT THAT TRANSFORM TOUCHES — not a blanket final-text scanner, but running each specific text-mutating function against the FULL real corpus it operates on (not the one example an audit happened to cite), before AND after each fix. This found bugs the original creativity-vs-rules audit itself never caught:
    - **THE 10.B1 QUOTE-AS-SUBJECT BREAK, CLOSED AT THE DATA LEVEL**: the audit's own #1 finding (`_strip_spoken_words` corrupting "…triumphant certainty; "VERY" lands like a friendship contract…" into a subject-less "…lands like a friendship contract…") was the ONLY live occurrence of this pattern anywhere in the 43-beat package (confirmed by a full-package scan for any quote character in any text field after the fix — zero remain). Rewrote the beat's own delivery text to describe the same directing intent without a quoted word as its grammatical subject ("the emphasis lands like a friendship contract…").
    - **THE SEMICOLON-AS-SENTENCE-BOUNDARY BUG — FOUND INDEPENDENTLY, NOT IN THE ORIGINAL AUDIT'S OWN 34 FINDINGS**: testing `_v5_cap_sentences` against Sunny's own real, authored `characters.json` mannerisms text (not a synthetic example) surfaced a genuine, previously-undetected bug: the sentence-split regex (`(?<=[.;])\s+`) treated a bare semicolon as equivalent to a period — but a semicolon joins two clauses of ONE sentence in English, not two sentences. With a 1-sentence cap this truncated "Pure motion — bounding, skipping, twirling, leaping; she physically cannot hold still when she's lit up." down to the dangling fragment "Pure motion — bounding, skipping, twirling, leaping;" — discarding the sentence's own second half. The IDENTICAL buggy regex lived in `_v5_positive_movement_slice`, the function EVERY character's `cadence` compiles through on the PRIMARY (always-live) path, not just the rare mannerisms fallback — confirmed dormant today only because none of the 9 real semicolon-bearing cadence fields happens to also trigger the negation-duplicate filter, a "works by luck, not by design" state, not a safe one. Both functions now split only on real sentence-ending punctuation (`.`/`!`/`?`), never a bare semicolon — verified against all 11 real characters' cadence fields, zero regressions.
    - **THE DOUBLE-PERIOD BUG, CONFIRMED LIVE ON 33 OF 84 REAL DIALOGUE CUTS TODAY**: `_v5_cut_speaker_note` unconditionally appended a trailing "." after a beat's authored `delivery` text — but `delivery` is free-form directing prose that often already ends in its own terminal punctuation, so the shipped Seedance prompt has been sending a literal ".." on the end of a third of every real spoken cut in the package (e.g. real beat 8.B3 cut 2: "…childlike welcome rather than ceremony.."). Fixed by stripping trailing terminal punctuation before appending — mirrors the identical guard already present a few lines above in the sibling function `_v5_acting_dna`, so this bug should never have shipped in the first place; it was simply never applied consistently between the two near-identical functions. Same fix pass also caught and corrected a genuine subject-verb-agreement error: a chorus/group speaker resolved as literal "All" was getting the singular verb "performs" ("All performs their vocal beat…") instead of the grammatically correct plural "perform." Verified zero of the real package's 84 dialogue cuts still carry a double period.
    - **THE PLAIN-NAME WORD-BOUNDARY GAP — THE SAME BUG AS THE ALREADY-FIXED POSSESSIVE-TAIL CASE, FOUND BY DELIBERATELY PROBING FOR IT**: having just fixed the possessive-tail substring bug (rule 72), directly tested whether the PLAIN name-mention check next to it had the identical flaw — it did. `cast=['Bo', 'Aida']` with action text "Aida crosses the room, opening the box." wrongly marked Bo as ACTIVE purely because "box" contains the substring "bo." Not yet live-triggering (Bo is never cast in the real package today), but the same latent hazard the possessive-tail fix was built to close, missed the first time because the fix was scoped to the one case the audit happened to cite rather than swept to every code path sharing the same pattern (rule 11's own standing discipline, applied a beat late here — caught by directly re-checking, not by the original audit).
    NINE REAL, CONFIRMED BUGS CLOSED ACROSS THIS SESSION'S TWO PASSES (rules 72+73) — every one verified against real production data before AND after the fix, every one with a new permanent test in `test_cb_segprompt.py` (58 assertions total in that file now, up from 8 before tonight), every one re-checked against all 10 standing test suites, `cb_preflight.py`'s totals (unchanged: 39/43 clean, 8 BLOCK — same pre-existing dialogue-verbatim drift), and `cb_golden.py diff` (unchanged from rule 72's own baseline — nothing in this pass touches the keyframe/still-image path).
    WHAT THIS DOES AND DOES NOT CLAIM, STATED AS PLAINLY AS RULE 71 ALREADY STATED IT ONCE: this closes nine specific, confirmed, real bugs — six named in the original creativity-vs-rules audit, three found only by directly re-testing the fixed code against its full real data set rather than trusting the audit's own example list. It does not claim the pipeline is now bug-free, and it does not claim no general safety net is possible — only that the one general approach actually tried (a blanket dangling-word scanner) was tested and found to create more harm than it prevented. 25 findings from the original 34 remain genuinely open (the TOO_RESTRICTIVE false-block category in `cb_preflight.py`/`cb_qa.py`, `_MANNERISMS_APPEARANCE_DROP`'s latent future-authoring risk, `cb_seedance.py`'s silent-fallback-on-empty-prompt gap) — not silently dropped, named here and in rule 72's own closing paragraph, genuinely deferred to a follow-up pass rather than claimed done to make this rule read as a complete answer to Julian's question when it is not one.

    A TENTH BUG, FOUND WHILE PROVING TO JULIAN THAT 1.B1 WAS SAFE TO RE-FIRE (same night, immediately after this rule was first written): asked directly "if I re-fire B1, does it have the new wording in it?" — rather than just asserting yes, compiled 1.B1's real, current prompt and read it end to end before answering. Found a fourth variant of the SAME stripper bug class, live in the exact beat under discussion: `_v5_strip_speed_adjectives` stripping "rapid" out of the beat's own real, authored "wingbeats rapid and continuous" left the dangling fragment "wingbeats and continuous" — a coordinated-modifier-pair strip, not covered by either of the two exclusions rule 72 had already added (not a comparative, not an intensifying preposition). Swept the real package by comparing raw pre-strip text against compiled output for every occurrence of a speed-adjective word immediately followed by " and <one word>" before a clause boundary — found the identical construction ("wings beating rapidly and continuously") live in 3 more real beats (8.B2, 10.B1, 10.B2), all four using the same authored phrase pattern. `_SPEED_ADJ_RE` gained a third exclusion: a match immediately followed by " and " plus exactly one more word before the next clause boundary is left untouched (a coordinated pair, not filler); a match followed by "and" plus two-or-more words (a genuine new clause, e.g. "fast and lands hard.") still strips correctly, verified directly against both shapes. All four real beats confirmed fixed; a fifth permanent test added. This is the exact discipline rule 73 described in the abstract, demonstrated concretely under a direct question rather than a self-initiated audit — the read-the-real-output-before-answering habit is what caught it, not a new tool or a new pass.

74. AN EXTERNAL AI REVIEWER'S CRITIQUE, VERIFIED CLAIM BY CLAIM AGAINST REAL CODE (2026-07-13, same night — Julian: "I've put the whole of beat one into Fuzzby T5.6... I think we're miles away from what they've said, and that's worrying"): rather than agree or push back from memory, every one of the reviewer's ~15 distinct claims was checked directly against the real codebase and the real compiled 1.B1/1.B2 prompts before any response — matching this file's own rule 73 discipline (verify before trusting), applied here to an EXTERNAL claim, not just an internal fix. Verdict: not "miles away" — a genuinely mixed picture, not a clean win for either side.
    CONFIRMED REAL, FIXED THE SAME NIGHT (two small, surgical changes, not the reviewer's own proposed scale):
    - **THE "SINGLE-TAKE" UI MISLABEL**: `cb-studio/app.html` literally said "flattened into the single-take Seedance prompt above" — but the real compiled prompt has FOUR distinct timed camera setups (0-4s/4-8s/8-11s/11-15s, matching the reviewer's own cited lenses and ranges exactly) — confirmed the shot-timing law (rule 54) already makes this genuinely multi-shot, the LABEL was just wrong. Relabelled to "compiled into the multi-shot Seedance prompt above with its own per-shot timecodes/lenses." Verified live in the served Studio HTML (fetched `/` directly and confirmed the old string is gone, the new one is present) — this is a real observable UI change, not just a source edit.
    - **NO REFERENCE-PRIORITY TIE-BREAK EXISTED**: confirmed by direct grep across `cb_segprompt.py`/`cb_qa.py`/`cb_prompts.py` that no priority/conflict-resolution statement existed anywhere for when references disagree — and a relay beat's own @图1 clause genuinely does claim "lighting and local geography," which overlaps the plate's declared job. First attempt added a general 3-way priority sentence to EVERY beat (~13-22 words) — this immediately pushed real beat 1.B2 over the 650-word hard cap (655-664 words measured, confirmed by `test_cb_qa.py`/`test_cb_segprompt.py` failing on the very next full-suite run, not assumed clean). Narrowed to a ~9-word, RELAY-ONLY clause naming the one actually-diagnosed overlap ("If @图1 and @图4 disagree on lighting, @图4 wins.") — an opener's @图1 makes no such claim, so the clause is correctly omitted there (verified: 1.B1 does not carry it, 1.B2 does, both under budget — 1.B2 lands at 648/650, a real but accepted tight margin). This is the exact "diagnose the specific overlap, write the minimum text that fixes it" discipline rule 73 already established, demonstrated again under external pressure rather than abandoned for the reviewer's own more sweeping multi-paragraph "REFERENCE AUTHORITY" proposal.
    CONFIRMED REAL, NOT FIXED — ALREADY LOGGED, JULIAN'S CALL PENDING: the reviewer independently re-found the exact flower-dive/no-entering-flower contradiction rule 63 (2026-07-09) already caught and deliberately left unfixed in signed-off 1.B1, per rule 18's forward-only doctrine. Nothing new here — the reviewer arrived at the same diagnosis this project already had on record, not a fresh miss.
    CONFIRMED REAL, DELIBERATELY NOT FIXED — REVERSES A DOCUMENTED, TESTED PRIOR DECISION: `soundIntent` (real, specific per-beat sound design — ambience, wing buzz, the leaf FWIP, music ducks) is genuinely computed but genuinely never reaches the compiled Seedance prompt; it only feeds the Studio's own advisory "Sound brief... NOT sent to the render prompt" panel. The reviewer is factually correct about current behaviour. But this isn't an oversight — rule 42 documents the exact opposite decision, made for a stated reason ("heavy, over-specified camera/negatives JSON has not been producing the movement Julian wants... a leaner prompt trusting the beat's own story content is a genuinely different bet"). Whether to reverse that decision is Julian's call, not something to silently flip.
    GENUINE PHILOSOPHICAL DISAGREEMENT, NOT ADOPTED: the reviewer's full proposed rewrite (frame-exact cut points, camera-arc degree limits, dozens of extra hard negatives, exhaustive per-shot SFX cues — 3-5x the current word count) is, in aggregate, a re-proposal of the SAME density this project already built, shipped, watched underperform, and deliberately tore back down — twice (v3's JSON `shots[]` → v4's terser whole-beat camera sentence → v5's own early draft that blew the word cap and was rebuilt leaner, rule 42's own "A REAL DESIGN CUT, FLAGGED NOT HIDDEN"). Not adopted wholesale. If Julian wants to test any of it, the right move (per this file's own established practice, rule 52's Decision 1) is one narrow, targeted addition at a time against a specifically diagnosed failure, verified on real footage before it spreads — exactly how both fixes above were actually built tonight, not the reviewer's own sweeping rewrite.
    ALSO NOTED, NOT ACTED ON: several of the reviewer's named "missing contradiction checks" (single-take+multicut, wings-beating+mute-all-audio, sound-generated+no-sound-brief) are genuinely absent from `cb_qa.py`'s ~18 existing checks — real gaps, lower priority than the two fixed tonight, not yet built. The reviewer's proposed "render as four continuity-linked shots" fallback mode doesn't exist as a one-click feature but is partially achievable via the existing relay/harvest-chain machinery (rules 15/21/26/27) applied at shot granularity — a real, substantial feature gap if Julian wants it, not a quick fix.
    Verified: all 10 standing test suites pass, `cb_preflight.py` totals back to the exact pre-change baseline (39/43 clean, 8 BLOCK, 56 FLAG, 62 STRUCTURAL), `cb_golden.py diff` shows only the same pre-existing keyframe-builder staleness from rule 55 (nothing in `cb_prompts.py`'s keyframe path was touched), the real production package's MD5 confirmed byte-identical before and after, and the UI label fix confirmed live in the actual served Studio HTML, not just the source file.

75. "FUN AND FREE, BUT THE PHYSICS HAS TO BE RIGHT" — PIXAR-CALIBER STYLE LAW, KINETIC ACTING TAGS, AND THE POSITIVE PHYSICS ANCHOR (2026-07-13, Julian's own direct brief, immediately after rule 74's external-reviewer verification — "I want you to give the prompts a Pixar or DreamWorks feel, you choose"): rather than answer from memory, an independent multi-lens craft audit was commissioned first — five judges (worked-prompt-examples, directing-coherence, anti-slop/cinematography-language, model-capability-fit, and a fresh-eyes read with zero project context), each required to back every claim with a direct quote from the real, currently-shipping 1.B1/1.B2 prompts, then a synthesis pass keeping only what 3+ judges independently corroborated. Verdict: scores clustered 3-6.5/10 — "competent, not excellent," not "broken." The single most-corroborated weakness, named by all five judges independently: the prompt mixes registers — genuinely strong comedy prose (the FWIP crash chain, the pollen-moustache/wipe sequence, the flower spring-back physics — all independently praised, none of it rewritten) sits directly alongside two LOCKED, byte-identical-every-beat constants (the style line, the Acting DNA tag) that read as evaluative labels rather than direction. Chose Pixar over DreamWorks — the show's own craft bar is already named after it (rule 48's "would a Pixar shorts team be proud"), and DreamWorks' broader, more referential register isn't this show's voice.
    THREE PIECES BUILT, EACH VERIFIED AGAINST REAL COMPILED OUTPUT, NOT JUST READ ON THE PAGE:
    - **THE STYLE LAW, REWRITTEN** (`shows/crystal-bears/laws/style.txt`, the ONE global constant quoted verbatim into every beat, per rule 25/28): from adjective-salad ("bright controlled colour, cinematic lighting, clear staging, expressive physical comedy and strong reference-first character consistency") to concrete, render-actionable, physics-forward language ("real weight, squash-and-stretch, warm saturated light, bold legible staging, character locked to reference"), naming Pixar-caliber explicitly, at the SAME word cost as before (not longer — a hard constraint given the word budget, below). This is Julian's own authored line from decision 4 (rule 52) being revised again under his own direct present instruction, not silently overridden — the same authorship discipline rule 74 already held to when it flagged (not fixed) this exact tension for an EXTERNAL reviewer's suggestion.
    - **THE ACTING DNA TAGS, REWRITTEN FOR ALL 10 AUTHORED CHARACTERS** (`characters.json`'s `cadence` field — Bo's stays empty, per its own standing "do NOT invent these" stub-character note): from static adjective lists ("manic, staccato, HYPER — always fast, chaotic, bumping into things") to active, physical, kinetic verb-driven prose ("throws himself at everything wings-first, crash-lands into pride, never admits defeat") — same underlying character truth, same semantic content, just written as motion instead of labels. Deliberately did NOT make this per-shot/paraphrased (the audit's own suggested rewrite #2 would have re-opened rule 28's ACTINGDNA RETRACTION — Julian's own explicit prior kill of a richer, per-beat-authored acting-register field, for the stated reason that authored-not-verbatim content drifts from the canonical store): this stays ONE locked, evergreen, non-shot-specific line per character, exactly the same field, same verbatim-quote contract, just better-written prose for the same label. The dead `(bigger, male)`/`(smaller, female)`/`(male, young cub)` parentheticals (already stripped by `_META_PAREN_WORDS` before shipping, confirmed — `sizeRank`/`gender` are the real, separately-read canonical fields) were dropped as pure cleanup, not a functional change.
    - **THE POSITIVE PHYSICS ANCHOR, BUILT** (`cb_segprompt._v5_physics_anchor`, wired as the leading line of Block 4 alongside the pre-existing `_v5_archetype_prohibited`, rule 62): closes the gap the audit's capability judge named most sharply — "state the good outcome once... is strictly more reliable than listing sixteen bad outcomes and hoping none of them summon the thing they're trying to prevent." Rule 62 wired the archetype's NEGATIVE half (`prohibited_staging`) into the shipped prompt; this is the missing POSITIVE half — the same already-authored `PHYSICAL_ARCHETYPES[...]["physics_rule"]` (real, physically specific staging language authored for all 18 archetypes, e.g. "the flower compresses softly under his face, then springs back with elastic weight as a pollen puff bursts"), mechanically quoted (never paraphrased — THE FIDELITY LAW) and clause-capped via a new `_v5_cap_words_at_clause` helper (never truncates mid-clause, the same "whole unit or nothing" discipline `_v5_cap_sentences` already holds — rule 73's own hard lesson about mid-construction truncation, applied here from the start rather than discovered as a bug later). Deliberately did NOT touch `_v5_negative_line`'s existing archetype-negatives wiring (rule 62's own hard-won safety net, proven against a real documented bug — 1.B4's characters vanishing into a flower) — this is additive, not a replacement, so the mechanism that actually fixed that bug stays fully intact.
    THE WORD-BUDGET FIGHT, MEASURED AND RESOLVED, NOT GUESSED AT: 1.B2 (the tightest real beat in the show, already sitting at 648/650 before tonight per rule 52) was the binding constraint. Iterated against the REAL compiled output (`python3 cb_segprompt.py ... 1.B2`, not a hand-count) through several rounds: an early style-law draft (+5 words) plus richer cadence tags (+16 words net) plus an uncapped physics anchor pushed 1.B2 to 667/650 — over the hard cap. Trimmed in order: the style law tightened to exactly its pre-change word cost; the physics-anchor cap lowered (16→12→9 words, though this has a real, documented ceiling — see below); Fuzzby's and Zenny's cadence tags shortened further (Fuzzby 15→11 words, Zenny 16→8 words) than their own already-short originals. Final state: 1.B2 lands at 649/650 — compliant, but only one word of margin, flagged here explicitly (matching this file's own standing disclosure practice) rather than left as a silent trap for the next beat-content edit. 1.B1 (the opener, far less reference-overhead) lands at 484/650, comfortable.
    A REAL DESIGN LIMIT, NAMED NOT HIDDEN: `_v5_cap_words_at_clause` always keeps at least the archetype's own first whole clause even when that clause alone exceeds `_PHYSICS_ANCHOR_MAX_WORDS` — the same "never truncate mid-clause" discipline as `_v5_cap_sentences`, deliberately preferring a slightly-over-budget but grammatically whole line over a fragment (the exact class of bug rules 71-73 spent a whole night finding and fixing). LEAF_CRASH_REBOUND's own first clause (1.B1's archetype) is ~14 words, so lowering the nominal cap below that has zero further effect there — confirmed directly, not assumed, and accepted since 1.B1 has 166 words of real headroom regardless.
    VERIFIED: a new `test_physics_anchor()` added to `test_cb_segprompt.py` (63 assertions in that file now) — proves the anchor resolves for a real archetype, is correctly positioned as Block 4's leading line (not a new `"\n\n"` block, so `cb_qa.check_gate3_lint`'s block-index model stays unchanged), that `_v5_cap_words_at_clause` never fragments a clause and never crashes on empty input, and that a malformed beat degrades gracefully (matching `_v5_archetype_prohibited`'s own existing contract — its resolver has a generic fallback archetype, so "malformed" doesn't mean empty, it means whatever the fallback resolves to, exactly as already documented for the sibling function). `cb_qa.check_gate3_lint`'s own citation map extended with a `beat_story:physics` entry so this citation is correct from the moment it was written, never left to go stale first (the same discipline rule 62 already established for its own negatives citation). All 10 standing test suites re-run clean; `cb_preflight.py` against the real 43-beat package: 39/43 clean, 8 BLOCK unchanged (same pre-existing dialogue-verbatim drift, zero new gaps — the global style/cadence rewrite and the new physics anchor touch every beat's word count but none crossed the hard cap); `cb_golden.py diff` shows the expected, real content difference (the golden set was already broadly stale against v5 since rule 42, per rules 55/59/64/65/68 — this change doesn't newly break anything, it's simply real, deliberate, requested content change, same as every other rule in this file that touched shipped prompt text). The real production beat package's own content (`cuts[]`, `endState`, etc.) was NOT touched by this rule — every change here lives in the two global constants (`style.txt`, `characters.json`'s `cadence`) plus new, additive compiler code; no beat's own authored data was edited, and 1.B1's signed-off status (rule 51) was not disturbed at the content level (only its COMPILED prompt changes, which is expected and correct the moment a global constant changes — the existing fingerprint-cascade relock mechanism, rule 14, handles this exactly as designed for every other global-constant edit this file already documents).

76. THE FIRST REAL FIRE UNDER THE NEW PROMPTS — "NOT GREAT... LOTS OF NON-MOVEMENT, THE PACE IS POOR, THE CONTINUITY ISN'T GREAT" (2026-07-13, same night, immediately after rule 75 — Julian's own direct verdict on real re-rendered footage): per his own separate instruction the same session, Gate 3's media was archived clean (`media/archive/Ep1_scene1_pre_pixar_prompt_rewrite_20260713/`, README inside) and 1.B1/1.B2 were re-fired fresh under the rule-75 prompts. His verdict on watching the result was blunt and negative. Investigated with real evidence — the automated verdicts already on disk, plus actual extracted frames from the real .mp4s, not assumption — before responding.

    TWO REAL DEFECTS, ALREADY MACHINE-CAUGHT, VISUALLY CONFIRMED: (1) the join-check between 1.B1→1.B2 failed outright (`ok: false`, `STATE: BROKEN`), in its own words: "image 2 is a pulled-back wide shot of the field rather than continuing close on the characters" and "a large pink flower is now situated between the characters in image 2." Pulling 1.B1's actual last frame and 1.B2's actual first frame confirmed it directly — a tight two-shot cutting straight to a pulled-back wide with a brand-new flower prop that was never part of the established continuity. This is exactly what rule 34's COVERAGE criterion exists to catch, and it did; it is a real, Seedance-generation-level failure to hold the relay, not a text/data bug — nothing in the relay anchor wording was touched by rule 75. (2) 1.B2's own Clip QA separately flagged `CLIP_IDENTITY_DRIFT` ("a character drifts off-model during the take... re-render, the keyframe is the locked truth") — a second real, machine-confirmed defect on the same take. Per rule 28's one-render economy, both are diagnosed as TAKE-layer failures — the correct response is a retake, not a prompt rewrite, since the machine already names the fault as generation, not text.

    ONE REAL, SELF-INFLICTED DEFECT, FOUND AND FIXED THE SAME NIGHT: rule 75's own `_v5_physics_anchor`, squeezed to fit 1.B2's tight word budget (`_PHYSICS_ANCHOR_MAX_WORDS` tuned down to 9 during that night's own budget fight), kept ONLY the first clause of POLLEN_FACE_PRESS_REVEAL's `physics_rule` — "the flower compresses softly under his face" — and dropped the actually dynamic second half, "then springs back with elastic weight as a pollen puff bursts." The mechanism built specifically to fight non-movement was itself shipping the flatter, more static half of its own source sentence, on the exact beat that then read as under-moving. Confirmed by direct visual inspection of the real rendered frames (extracted via ffmpeg, read directly) — the actual footage does read as a sequence of held poses/portraits more than continuous propulsive motion, consistent with both his complaint and this specific text gap.
    FIXED, VERIFIED AGAINST REAL COMPILED OUTPUT, NOT ASSUMED: `_PHYSICS_ANCHOR_MAX_WORDS` widened 9→24 (comfortably holds a real two-clause physics description). To pay for it without blowing the word cap, `_v5_negative_line` now SUPPRESSES the archetype's own auto-injected `prohibited_staging` phrases whenever the beat also carries a physics anchor — the beat's own hand-authored `stagingProhibited` and the eleven standing negatives are untouched, only the archetype-derived duplicate-of-the-positive-statement is dropped. This is the completion of a trade-off rule 75 itself already named and deliberately deferred out of caution ("Deliberately did NOT touch `_v5_negative_line`'s existing archetype-negatives wiring... this is additive, not a replacement") — now justified by real evidence rather than applied pre-emptively. Rule 62's own hard-won safety net is NOT weakened in general: this only fires on the SAME beats that now state the identical protection positively instead, and only suppresses the archetype-derived portion, never the beat's own authored list or the standing eleven. Verified on real compiled output: 1.B1 now ships the FULL physics_rule ("over-commits the turn and SLAMS side-first into the leaf with a big springy WHAP, exaggerated squash-and-stretch, wings flailing") at 458 words (down from 484 despite the longer anchor — the freed negative-line budget outweighed it); 1.B2 now ships its own full two-clause physics description at 636 words (down from 649 — headroom went from 1 word to 13). `cb_qa.check_gate3_lint`'s negatives citation made conditional to match (states which source composition actually applied to this beat's own compile, never a generic worst case). One pre-existing test assertion updated to the new, correct contract (a beat WITH a physics anchor no longer repeats the archetype's negative phrases — its positive companion already covers the same ground); everything else in that test suite required no change. All 10 standing test suites re-run clean; `cb_preflight.py`: 39/43 clean, 8 BLOCK unchanged, FLAG count improved (63→60, real beats crossing back under the word-count target from the freed budget); `cb_golden.py diff` count unchanged from rule 75's own baseline.

    NOT YET DONE, NAMED HONESTLY: the fix closes the one CONFIRMED self-inflicted content gap. It does not and cannot fix the join-check failure or the identity drift — those are real, needed retakes under the corrected (now more dynamic) prompt, not yet fired as of this rule. 1.B1's own footage carried no automated flag at all, but the same visual read (more held-pose than continuous-motion) applies there too on direct inspection — whether that's enough to warrant a 1.B1 retake as well, given rule 18's "work already signed off stands" doesn't apply here (1.B1 was never actually re-signed after this session's re-fire), is Julian's own call, named here rather than decided unilaterally.

77. "THE PROMPT WRITING FOR SEEDANCE ISN'T RIGHT" — CHECKED AGAINST ACTUAL PLATFORM DOCTRINE, TWO REAL ARCHITECTURAL FINDINGS, ONE WORKED FIX (2026-07-13, same night, Julian's own words after rule 76): rather than answer from impression, this project's own `seedance-20` skill reference material (`multishot-grammar.md`, `allocation-model.md`, `seedance-motion` skill) was read directly and our actual architecture checked against it line by line — not just our text, our STRUCTURE.

    FINDING 1 — SHOT DENSITY IS RUNNING AT THE DOCTRINE'S OWN COMPRESSION FLOOR: `multishot-grammar.md`, verbatim: "Plan ≈4-6s per shot: two shots want ~10s, three want 12-15s. Ask for four shots in 5s and the model compresses or skips beats." Every one of this show's 43 beats is hard-locked to exactly 4 shots in 15s (`_v5_shot_time_ranges`, rule 54) — averaging under 4s/shot, below the doctrine's own comfortable range, in the exact zone its own failure table names: "A shot's action skipped/compressed → fewer shots · raise duration/auto."

    FINDING 2 — EVERY SHOT ASKS FOR EVERYTHING AT ONCE: `allocation-model.md`, verbatim: "A shot that needs a perfect face, a backflip, a crowded market, and a spoken line in one call will land none of them cleanly... a prompt that demands everything gets mediocrity everywhere." Every one of our shots demands full identity lock (4 reference images, "match exactly") AND full motion boldness (the physics anchor, the manic-Fuzzby doctrine) AND full scene density (the whole meadow, pollen, blossoms) AND, in half the shots, lip-synced dialogue — simultaneously, every time, never economized. `fidelityAllocation` (rule 50) only controls which CHARACTERS get Acting DNA text — it does not control which of identity/motion/density a given SHOT actually spends its budget on, the axis this doctrine actually describes. A real, confirmed gap, not yet closed.

    THE SPECIFIC "CLUNKY, SCRIPTED, FAKE" COMPLAINT — checked against `seedance-motion`'s own Motion Contract, which gives a direct Strong-phrase/Weak-phrase table (Strong: `"the heavy oak door swings shut and the candle flames bend toward it"`; Weak: `"the door closes dramatically"`) and states outright: "Consequences prove the action: weight shows in landing compression, momentum in overshoot and recovery... One physical cause with two or three visible consequences reads stronger than three separate actions." Our own real shot 1 text before tonight: *"Fuzzby rockets flower to flower collecting pollen, wingbeats rapid and continuous... while pollen loads bounce with each pass"* — six separately-named events/labels in one sentence, one of them ("wingbeats rapid and continuous") a generic attribute-label, not a physical consequence. Shot 2: *"dives low into the flower, scoops pollen, overdoes the exit, spins sideways into a leaf, hits FWIP, then bounces back into the air"* — seven stacked discrete actions in one sentence, exactly the checklist-of-poses shape the doctrine's own Weak-phrase column names, and a direct, credible textual explanation for "clunky and scripted" — a video model asked to hit seven named beats in four seconds has nowhere to spend on weight, arcs, or follow-through.

    WORKED FIX, ON 1.B1 ONLY, AS A REAL COMPARISON TEST (data-only — no beat outside 1.B1 touched, nothing in the compiler code changed for this rule beyond the bug found while writing it, below): `cuts[]` rewritten from 4 shots to 3 (0-5s/5-10s/10-15s, matching the doctrine's own "three want 12-15s" comfortable range) and every action clause rewritten to the doctrine's own cause-and-consequence pattern instead of a checklist of labeled events — e.g. the crash shot, was *"dives low into the flower, scoops pollen, overdoes the exit, spins sideways into a leaf, hits FWIP, then bounces back into the air; his wings keep beating rapidly through the ricochet"* (seven actions), now *"Fuzzby's own momentum carries him past the flower's centre and straight into the broad leaf beside it — the leaf bends hard under his weight, then springs him backward, pollen bursting off his body with the impact"* — ONE cause (his own momentum) with TWO visible physical consequences (the leaf bending under weight, pollen bursting on impact), the exact shape the Motion Contract names as strongest. All dialogue kept verbatim in its original cut (Law 6, untouched); `endState`/`endStateStill`/`carryMarks` left as-is (not part of the checklist problem). Compiles at 419 words (well under budget, was 484 before this rewrite).

    A REAL BUG FOUND WRITING THE FIX, FIXED THE SAME NIGHT — THE SAME CLASS RULES 71-73 ALREADY SPENT A WHOLE NIGHT ON, REPRODUCED LIVE: the new sentence "Fuzzby's own speed bends every stem he passes" compiled to "Fuzzby's own bends every stem he passes" — `_SPEED_ADJ_RE`'s own `speed(?:y|ily)?` pattern was stripping the bare NOUN "speed" via its own optional suffix, not just the adjective/adverb forms ("speedy"/"speedily") it was actually meant to ban (rule 33's adjective-chaos ban). A bare "speed" naming the actual physical cause of a visible consequence is exactly the concrete, causal language the seedance-motion doctrine asks for — the opposite of the vague pace-adjectives ("fast," "hyper," "wildly") that rule was built to catch. Fixed: the pattern now only matches `speedy`/`speedily` explicitly, never the bare noun. Swept the whole real 43-beat package for any other live occurrence — zero found; this was a fresh bug introduced writing tonight's own rewrite, not a pre-existing one. Two new permanent test cases added (`test_strip_speed_adjectives`, `test_cb_segprompt.py`) proving the noun survives and both real adjective/adverb forms still strip.

    VERIFIED: all 10 standing test suites re-run clean; `cb_preflight.py`: 39/43 clean, 8 BLOCK unchanged, zero new gaps; 1.B1's own compiled prompt confirmed grammatically intact end to end by direct re-read, not assumed. NOT YET FIRED as of this rule — this is a data-only rewrite of one beat's own cuts, staged as a direct, controlled comparison against tonight's already-flagged "clunky/scripted/fake" footage, held for Julian's own decision on whether to fire it. NOT DONE, NAMED HONESTLY: Finding 2 (per-shot fidelity allocation) is real and confirmed but NOT acted on this rule — closing it properly means either a new per-shot allocation field or a real rewrite of how `fidelityAllocation`/the shot list interact, a bigger design decision than a single-beat text rewrite, deliberately left for Julian's own direction rather than built unilaterally under the same time pressure as everything else tonight. The 3-shots-per-beat structure was applied to 1.B1 ONLY, as a test — not rolled out to the other 42 beats; whether to do so, and whether Finding 1's fix should be `_v5_shot_time_ranges` capping shots at 3 by default rather than always matching `len(cuts)`, is also Julian's own call, not decided here.

78. THE CAPCUT FORMULA — FROM "WE FIXED A PROMPT AGAIN" TO PERMANENT DOCTRINE (Julian, 2026-07-13, later the same night — first "we're fixing a prompt again... not the structure or the philosophies or principles of this," then, after the formula was found and proven on a real re-fire, "if this works we rewrite the seedance prompting workflow to ensure it all goes through it correctly and we have consistency"): the same night Julian shared his own CapCut-generated six-shot version of Scene 1 (built on techniques Laws 5/6 forbid — appearance description, dialogue-in-prompt/native voice) and called it "amazing," a real test-and-diagnose cycle on his own recipe's Shot 2 (the beat closest to 1.B1) surfaced a genuine, evidence-backed, previously-undocumented formula — found by two independent methods agreeing: a real structural diff of four actual fires that night (the CLAUDE.md rule 77 gymnast-landing 1.B1, CapCut's native-audio Shot 2, a broken V3-voice adaptation, and its fix) against each one's real human verdict, AND a full read of this project's own seedance-20 skill reference docs (never previously mined this deeply — `multishot-grammar.md`, `allocation-model.md`, `skills/seedance-motion/SKILL.md`'s Motion Contract, `prompt-compiler.md`).

    THE FOUR LEVERS, CONFIRMED BY BOTH SOURCES INDEPENDENTLY:
    1. **The vocal-performance cue must trail the action sentence, never split it.** Splicing "@Audio1" into the middle of a dive sentence ("flies toward the sunflower, performing his vocal beat from @Audio1, then dives headfirst") read to the model as two separate camera setups and broke spatial continuity — confirmed by pulling matching frames at the identical timestamp from the broken and fixed takes. The platform's own `audio-guide.md` independently states the identical principle: never let two sources compete for control of timing.
    2. **One physical cause with two-or-three chained, visible consequences beats a checklist of independently-clocked verbs.** The confirmed, never-actually-fixed reason a "continuity now works but the comedy's weaker" verdict landed: the crash beat's own text — "he bounces off, spins once in mid-air, stabilizes himself, puffs out his chest proudly, and says" — was byte-identical across all three CapCut variants tested that night, including the "fixed" one; nobody had touched the actual weak sentence, only the audio-splice bug next to it. `skills/seedance-motion/SKILL.md`'s own Motion Contract states this exact principle verbatim: "one physical cause with two or three visible consequences reads stronger than three separate actions."
    3. **Word budget should go to fresh per-shot action, not restated scaffolding.** A real n-gram overlap check found CapCut's "Must-Preserve Visual Details" and "Spatial Composition Requirements" blocks were ~50% verbatim restatement of facts the timed action prose already stated — the gymnast-landing 1.B1 (no such scaffold, references carry identity/position, prose carries only the delta) spent 61% of its words on fresh direction vs. ~34% for the CapCut variants. The platform's own `prompt-compiler.md` states the identical rule independently: "do not re-describe in prose what the attached source already shows... where the words disagree with the pixels, the prose becomes a drift instruction." Caveat, stated plainly: redundancy alone was NOT fatal — the native-audio CapCut version had the WORST scaffolding ratio of the three variants (54%) and still got the second-best verdict, because it had no audio-continuity conflict. Waste is inefficient; it is the specific broken mechanics (levers 1 and 2) that actually sink a take.
    4. **A dedicated camera clause and a standalone one-line character-truth statement, each stated once as law, never touching the action prose.** Present only in the real production compiler's own output (1.B1's `"24mm wide tracking shot... camera banks hard..."` per-shot clause, and its `"Fuzzby: throws himself at everything wings-first, crash-lands into pride, never admits defeat"` Acting DNA line) — absent from every CapCut variant, where camera is a bare shot-size word folded into the action sentence and personality is only implicit in verb choice.

    THE FORMULA PROVEN LIVE, THEN BUILT INTO PERMANENT SOFTWARE (per Julian's own explicit instruction, closing the loop rule 77 itself left open): all four levers applied at once to a rewritten CapCut Shot 2 — real audio, cause-and-consequence on both the dive AND the crash, no restated scaffolding, dedicated camera+personality clauses — produced the strongest take of the whole night on direct re-watch (real motion blur through the entrance, an unbroken continuous dive, a genuinely dynamic spin-out into the leaf, a settled and earned recovery). Rather than stop at that one proof, the same night: **THE CHECKLIST-VERB LINT** (`cb_qa.check_gate3_lint`, new check) — flags any cut whose action text splits into 4+ comma-separated fragments as an advisory "checklist risk," citing the Motion Contract; a computed proxy for lever 2, not a semantic judge of true causation (the same honest "report, never block a proxy" convention this codebase already uses for its other best-effort heuristics, e.g. the single-gag-arc check) — FLAG only, never a hard block. **LAW 15 — THE MOTION CONTRACT** and **LAW 16 — THE SHOT BUDGET**, added to `cb_director.py`'s own system prompt (the Director's operating doctrine, `_mind()`), with the exact worked WEAK/STRONG contrast from this session as the citable example, and the `cuts[]` schema instruction itself tightened from "2-4 internal cuts" to "2-3 preferred, 4 only when the gag genuinely cannot compress" — so lever 2 is now something Gate 1 authors correctly from the start, not something caught after the fact. **A PERMANENT REGRESSION GUARD** (`test_cb_segprompt.py`, `test_v5_beat_story_audio_cue_always_trails`) pins down that `_v5_cut_speaker_note` can never again be interleaved mid-action, sweeping the whole real 43-beat package to confirm zero cuts currently violate it — lever 1's fix was never actually a production-compiler bug (the code has always appended the speaker note as a strict trailing suffix), so this guards the invariant against a future refactor rather than fixing a live defect. **LEVERS 3 AND 4 REQUIRED NO NEW CODE** — confirmed live by reading `_v5_beat_story`/`_v5_acting_dna` directly: the real compiler has never had a redundant-scaffolding block (references carry identity/position by design, per THE FIDELITY LAW) and has always emitted a per-shot labelled camera clause (`"{start}–{end}s — {framing}:"`) and a standalone Acting DNA line (Block 3) — both of tonight's "missing" levers were only ever missing from the hand-written CapCut-adapted scratch prompts, never from production. Naming this explicitly matters: it means only two of the four levers needed real engineering work, and the other two are proof the existing v5 architecture was already right on those two fronts.

    WHAT THIS RULE DOES NOT CLAIM: lever 2's authoring-prompt fix (Laws 15/16) shapes every FUTURE Gate-1 fire; it does not retroactively rewrite the other 42 beats' already-authored action text or cuts counts, several of which the new checklist-verb lint now correctly flags (60 FLAGs package-wide after this rule, up from the pre-existing baseline — an honest, informational finding, not a new gap this rule caused). Whether to re-author existing beats against the new lint's findings is Julian's own call, matching this project's own standing practice for exactly this kind of scope decision (rule 77's own identical deferral).

    VERIFIED: all 10 standing test suites pass (2 new test cases added: `test_gate3_lint_checklist_verb_flag` in `test_cb_qa.py`, `test_v5_beat_story_audio_cue_always_trails` in `test_cb_segprompt.py`, both proven against both synthetic fixtures and a full sweep of the real 43-beat package); `cb_preflight.py`: 39/43 clean, 8 BLOCK unchanged (the same pre-existing dialogue-verbatim drift this whole session has repeatedly confirmed, zero new blockers introduced), FLAG count rose from the prior baseline as an honest reporting of real, pre-existing checklist-shaped action text across the package, not a regression; `cb_golden.py diff` shows only the same pre-existing keyframe-prompt staleness already flagged and explained in earlier rules — nothing this rule touched affects `cb_prompts.py`'s keyframe path at all, only `cb_qa.py`/`cb_director.py`. `cb_director.py`/`cb_qa.py` both confirmed to `py_compile` clean.


79. THE FIRST 1.B3 FAILURE — THREE REAL BUGS, TWO SOFTWARE FIXES, ONE DOCTRINE CORRECTION FROM JULIAN
    (2026-07-13, the same night as rule 78): the CapCut-formula fixes shipped that night were never applied to
    1.B3, which fired next in the one-beat-at-a-time Scene 1 walk. It came back genuinely bad — Julian's own
    words: "we cant keep going back to the twig its not the right contiunity there is foregin language and the
    mechaincs and psyhics are awful." Diagnosed with real evidence (20 extracted frames, the full compiled
    prompt, a workflow mining the seedance-20 skill's own platform docs) before any fix was attempted, per this
    file's own standing discipline.
    THREE CONFIRMED, DISTINCT DEFECTS: (1) the render opened and closed on nearly the identical branch
    composition — the beat's own story (dive to a new flower, clip a different branch, tumble, land in a
    blossom) never actually traveled; (2) the blossom-landing frame showed Fuzzby's whole body pasted flat onto
    the flower, arms symmetrically splayed — not the "body/wings/legs stay visible above the petals" the text
    asked for; (3) audible hallucinated background foreign-language chatter, despite the standing "no
    foreign-language speech" negative already being present.
    ROOT CAUSES, EACH TRACED TO A REAL MECHANISM, NOT GUESSED AT:
    - The @图1 relay clause's own preserve-list included "local geography" as an ongoing claim (not scoped to
      the pickup instant), fighting directly against any beat whose own action requires real travel — the
      reference frame was the one thing the model had strong grounding for, and the geography clause gave it
      permission to keep returning to it.
    - 1.B3's own cuts 2/3 were exactly the checklist-shaped action text the checklist-verb lint (rule 78) was
      built to catch — it DID flag them before firing; the judgment call to leave them as "close enough,
      already chained" was wrong, confirmed by the actual footage.
    - Seedance's own audio engine treats an uploaded @Audio1 reference as "a signal, not a playback
      instruction" (seedance-20 skill's audio-guide.md/model-mechanics.md) and can generate its own background
      audio on top of it — the standing "no foreign-language speech" negative only ever covered the primary
      voice, never invented background chatter, and this project's own "Seedance scores the WHOLE take"
      framing (rule 42) is itself an invitation for exactly that.
    JULIAN'S OWN CORRECTION TO THE FIRST PROPOSED FIX (2026-07-13, mid-diagnosis — "i think the the stay in
    space is the first frame then it can go anywhere as its the continuity holder not the direction"): the
    initial plan was a conditional, travel-aware softening of the geography clause. Julian's framing is
    sharper and universal — @图1's job is being the continuity HOLDER for the pickup moment ONLY, never a
    director for where the shot travels next. No detection logic needed; the fix applies to every relay beat,
    always.
    TWO SOFTWARE FIXES SHIPPED, BOTH GENERAL, NEITHER BEAT-SPECIFIC (matching rule 62's "solve the problem, not
    the symptom" discipline):
    - `cb_segprompt._v5_references`'s relay @图1 clause: "local geography" dropped from the preserve-list
      entirely (matching the first frame exactly already covers where things are AT THAT INSTANT — nothing
      needs to say so twice); a new sentence states the shot is explicitly free to travel after that first
      frame ("begin the new action — free to travel anywhere the story needs"). The anti-hold counter-
      instruction and the first-frame-match sentence are unchanged.
    - `_standing_negatives()` gains a TWELFTH item, "no invented background voices" — the eleven-to-twelve
      count swept everywhere it was cited (`cb_qa.py`'s citations, `cb_golden.py`, `cb_director_schemas.py`,
      `cb_director.py`'s own authoring-prompt text, `GATE3_ANIMATION_DOCTRINE.md`, `MANIFEST.md`) — and a new
      positive-preservation sentence sits next to @Audio1 itself, "No other voices generated," since the
      platform doctrine's own finding is that explicit preservation reads more reliably than negation alone.
      Both wordings were trimmed under real measurement pressure — the first drafts pushed real beat 1.B2 (the
      tightest beat in the show) 2 words over the 650-word hard cap; trimmed to fit with real headroom, not
      guessed at.
    1.B3's OWN DATA WAS ALSO REWRITTEN, NOT JUST THE CODE: cuts 2-4 rebuilt from a checklist into a real
    physical chain with NAMED, concrete destinations (a specific foxglove, a specific birch branch, a second
    named blossom) instead of vague "another flower"/"the branch in his path" — closing the exact ambiguity
    that let the model default back to the one landmark it knew. The first rewrite (via a research-and-draft
    workflow) still packed the crash/land/kick/spring/pop sequence into one 12-fragment cut-3 sentence, caught
    by the SAME checklist-verb lint on re-check — this time acted on for real, split into two focused cuts (the
    tumble/landing, then the recovery/pop-up) rather than dismissed a second time. Two cuts (3-fragment "THUP,
    THUP, THUP" onomatopoeia; a 4-fragment recovery beat) still flag on re-check and were kept as-authored after
    actually reading them, not reflexively — verified case by case that both are genuine connected physical
    beats the naive comma-count heuristic over-splits, not real disconnected checklists. One factual bug in the
    workflow's own draft was caught and fixed before it ever reached the beat data: its proposed endState had
    Zenny "watching from the birch branch" — the branch Fuzzby accidentally collides with mid-flight, which she
    never travels to; corrected to "her own branch nearby."
    THE STANDING CONCERN, ANSWERED WITH EVIDENCE, NOT REASSURANCE: Julian's own worry — "we have so many
    restricitions that it casuses contrdictions and throttles the creativity" — is real and not paranoia. Two
    confirmed, already-documented cases exist: the word budget once forced a physics description down to its
    flattest version (rule 76), and the geography clause fixed here directly fought a travel-based story. The
    answer applied both times was NOT another rule stacked on top — it was making an EXISTING rule correctly
    scoped (first-frame-only, not a director) or correctly triggered (act on the lint's flag instead of
    dismissing it). Layer 1 (mechanical scaffolding — word budget, canon facts, dialogue accuracy, standing
    negatives) exists to protect the technical skeleton; Layer 2 (the actual gag, the actual staging) stays the
    beat's own authored content and Julian's own reserved creative verdict (rule 28) — nothing built tonight
    touches that line.
    VERIFIED: all 5 relevant standing test suites (`test_cb_segprompt.py` — 3 new/updated cases,
    `test_cb_qa.py`, `test_cb_preflight.py`, `test_gate_cascade.py`, `test_unapprove_locks.py`) pass clean;
    `cb_preflight.py` against the real 43-beat package unchanged at 39/43 clean, 8 BLOCK (the same pre-existing
    dialogue-verbatim drift, zero new blockers) — FLAG count rose only from the extra words the fix
    legitimately costs across every beat, an honest reporting, not a regression; `cb_golden.py diff` shows only

    ONE — A PERMANENT VISUAL CHECK FOR "DID THIS SHOT ACTUALLY GO ANYWHERE": `cb_qa._passB` (the existing

    gains a new criterion, `CLIP_COMPOSITION_LOOP` — compares the clip's own first and last frame on three
    NAMED, concrete axes (character position, camera framing/distance, background elements), per rule 17's
    own "concrete visual criteria, never a vague question" discipline. Deliberately advisory-only (never in
    `CLIP_BLOCK_CODES`, never in `CLIP_CORROBORATE`) — a comparative judgment like this is more subjective
    than the identity/anatomy checks it sits beside, and the doctrine's own established pattern (rule 45's
    degraded-harvest ruling) is to surface a signal like this for human review, never auto-fail a take on it
    alone. Costs nothing extra — reuses the same frames and the same vision call every other check here
    already pays for. This is the exact automatic detector 1.B3's real failure needed and didn't have; had
    it existed that night, the "keeps going back to the twig" complaint would have been a printed NOTE on
    the QA sidecar, not something Julian had to catch by watching the footage.
    TWO — THE PIXAR-CRAFT JUDGE, WIRED IN AT LAST: `cb_craft.score_scene_craft` (built and proved rule 48,
    2026-07-07) had ZERO live callers anywhere in the pipeline — confirmed by grep before this rule, not

    established convention for every other optional LLM enrichment (Director's Pass, keyframe/clip QA): a

    itself depends on. Rule 28's reserved verdict is unchanged by this — this is a second opinion surfaced

    THREE — THE REAL GAP THIS SESSION'S OWN 1.B3 FAILURE ACTUALLY CAME THROUGH: `cb_replicator.walk_scene`
