81. THE FRONT-TO-BACK WIRING PASS — CLOSING GATE-STATE, POST, AND DELIVERY GAPS (2026-07-14, continuing rule
    80's own "wire it, fire it, don't ask permission" mandate): six more real, confirmed gaps closed, three of
    them the exact "built but orphaned" pattern rule 80 already named as the session's central failure mode,
    plus one genuine stop-and-flag case handled correctly rather than worked around.
    - **THE wordlessHeld TRUTHINESS BUG, SWEPT TO ITS TWO SIBLING CALL SITES**: rule 70 fixed `cb_voice.
      build_dialogue_track`'s bare-truthiness read of `wordlessHeld` (a JSON string `"false"` is truthy in
      Python) but never checked whether the SAME field was read the same unsafe way anywhere else in the
      file — it was, twice: `_is_tender` and `audit_attribution`. A new shared `_is_wordless_held(shot)`
      helper (only `True` or the case-insensitive string `"true"` counts) now backs all three call sites.
      Verified with a real, deliberately-unresolvable speaker label ("NOTAREALCHARACTER") proving
      `audit_attribution` genuinely still audits a `"false"`-string beat rather than silently skipping it.
    - **`cb_retake.process_retakes`'S UNGUARDED PER-RETAKE LOOP**: a single bad/stale package path inside
      `regen_shot()` crashed the WHOLE batch, losing every retake already processed successfully before the
      crash — not just the one that failed. Isolated per-item (a caught exception now becomes an
      `{ok:False, ref, error}` entry, matching `regen_shot`'s own existing controlled-failure shape), so the
      rest of the batch still runs and is recorded. Proven with a synthetic two-retake batch where the first
      is rigged to raise — both retakes still land in `results`, the surviving one correctly still succeeds.
    - **GATE-STATE RECONCILIATION, BUILT** (`cb_pipeline.gate_state_report`/`reconcile_gate_state`,
      `python3 cb_pipeline.py reconcile <scene> [--apply]`): closes the exact "files that sit there with zero
      firing" complaint at the LEDGER level — `locked.json`'s scene-gate sign-offs (written only by
      `approve()`) had silently drifted from the real, independently-tracked deliverables (the previz reel,
      the plate, keyframes, rendered+QA'd clips, per-beat approval sidecars). Confirmed live: Scene 1 showed
      `{"beats": {}}` in `locked.json` — zero gates signed — despite a real previz reel, a locked plate, 1.B1's
      real keyframe, and four rendered clips (1.B2 carrying an explicit Julian-approved sidecar). The read
      side (`gate_state_report`) computes real per-gate readiness from disk evidence, cascading (a gate can
      only be ready if every gate before it is too) — Gate 3's readiness deliberately requires every beat's
      own recorded `beat_approval_status == "approved"`, never mere clip-existence-with-clean-QA, per Step 7's
      own standing doctrine ("Julian's Eye is the gate no machine owns"). The write side (`reconcile_gate_state`
      with `apply=True`) never writes `locked.json` directly — it walks the SAME `approve()` every manual
      sign-off already uses, so every existing guard (manifest, prev-gate chain, the Gate-1 cascade
      fingerprint) stays fully live. Dry-run against real Scene 1 correctly showed gates 1/1.6/2a/2b ready to
      sign (real artifacts on disk) and gate 3 correctly NOT ready, naming exactly why: `1.B1:pending,
      1.B3:unrendered, 1.B4:pending, 1.B5:pending`.
      **THE STOP, NOT A WORKAROUND**: attempting the actual `--apply` write was correctly blocked by the
      session's own safety layer — writing `"reviewed_by": "Julian"` into the gate ledger is a factual claim
      about HIS personal review, which artifact-presence evidence alone cannot honestly assert (rule 28's own
      "Julian's Eye is the one gate no machine owns" doctrine, extended here to gate-level sign-off, not just
      per-take approval). This is a genuine, correct stop — the tool is built, tested, and proven to correctly
      diagnose real state; actually SIGNING Scene 1's gates 1/1.6/2a/2b is Julian's own one-command action
      (`python3 cb_pipeline.py reconcile 1 --apply`) to take when he's ready, not mine to take on his behalf.
      Firing Gate 4 then Gate 5 for real on Scene 1 (a separately tracked item) is consequently blocked on the
      identical open item — confirmed live and cleanly: `python3 cb_pipeline.py 4 1` and `5 1` both correctly
      refuse ("Gate 3/4 not signed off"), no mutation, no bypass attempted, the gate-order guard working
      exactly as designed.
    - **`assemble_conformed` WIRED IN — THE SESSION'S CLEAREST "BUILT BUT ORPHANED" CASE**: JOIN ON LIVE
      MOTION (Julian's own 2026-07-03 ruling — "the last frame needs to be the start of the next frame... we
      need to get it to flow across") was fully built, tested, and named as Gate 5's own doctrine in three
      separate documents, but `cb_post.run()` had called `assemble_picture` (the raw butt-join) exclusively
      since the day both functions existed — confirmed stale by two separate prior audits (rules 46/49) and
      never actually closed until now. `run()`'s real Gate-5 picture is now built via `assemble_conformed`;
      `assemble_picture`'s own output is NOT discarded — it's saved alongside as `_picture_RAW.mp4`, the
      named "deliberate comparison baseline" its own docstring always called it, for a single-clip scene
      (no join exists to conform) it's used directly with no redundant raw copy. A second, real bug found
      wiring this in: `assemble_conformed` itself had never received the "return None on ffmpeg failure"
      fix its siblings `assemble_picture`/`mix` already got (rule 59) — because nothing had ever called it
      outside its own test, the bug was latent. Fixed identically. Proven with 3 new monkeypatched tests
      (`test_cb_post.py`) covering the multi-clip/single-clip branch and the failure-stops-before-mix
      contract.
    - **REAL DIALOGUE CAPTIONS, BUILT** (`cb_address.scene_captions`/`write_captions`, wired into `run()`):
      a delivery-ready video with no captions is not deliverable content for YouTube/Amazon/Netflix, all of
      which require them — this never existed (the pre-existing `write_review_srt` is a BEAT/SHOT/Ref
      overlay label track for retake review, not a caption track). Built from the SAME scene-cumulative shot
      timing the retake sheet/review overlay already trust (`_scene_shot_walk`, unmodified) plus each cut's
      own `dialogue` field ("SPEAKER: line") — the ground-truth field `cb_preflight.check_scene_dialogue_
      verbatim` already treats as authoritative, never the compiled Seedance prompt text, which deliberately
      never carries spoken words at all (Law 6). A chorus/"ALL:" line captions plain, no invented speaker
      label. Known, stated limitation: a caption's window is its whole shot's time range, not word-level
      forced alignment against the rendered clip's own audio — real ASR/alignment is a materially bigger
      feature than "derive captions from the dialogue and timing already on hand," which is what was asked
      for and what was built. Both `.srt` and `.vtt` written automatically at Gate 5. Verified against real
      Scene 1 data: correct dialogue, correct cumulative timing, correctly skips 1.B3 (unrendered, matching
      what the real stitched video actually contains).
    - **LOUDNESS TARGETS MADE EXPLICIT PER PLATFORM** (`cb_post.LOUDNESS_TARGETS`, `mix(..., platform=)`,
      `build_platform_masters()`): `mix()` had always mastered to a single hardcoded `-14 LUFS` — correct
      for YouTube (it happens to BE YouTube's own published target) but never actually a CHOICE, and every
      other platform Julian named by name ("YouTube, to Amazon, Netflix") has a materially different real
      spec (Netflix -27 LKFS dialogue-gated; Amazon/ATSC A/85 -24 LKFS; EBU R128 broadcast -23 LUFS — all
      cited as commonly-published targets, reconcile against each platform's own current delivery spec before
      a final broadcast delivery). `mix()`'s own YouTube default is unchanged; `build_platform_masters()` is
      the real new deliverable — a separately-mastered file per named platform, built from the scene's
      already-assembled picture, an unrecognized platform name degrading to the default rather than raising.
      Reachable via `python3 cb_post.py masters ...` or `cb_pipeline.py masters <scene>`. Proven with 4 new
      tests confirming each platform's own I/TP/LRA values genuinely reach the real ffmpeg filter, the
      unrecognized-platform fallback, and `build_platform_masters`'s own refuse-cleanly/one-call-per-platform
      contracts.
    - **THE SECOND MASTER, BUILT** (`cb_post.build_vertical_derivative`, wired into `run()`): CLAUDE.md rule
      28's own Production Line doctrine has always named "two masters delivered — the 16:9 feature master and
      a centre-safe 9:16 derivative" — the 16:9 one has always been built; the 9:16 one never was, a genuinely
      new feature, not just an unwired one. A static, centre-anchored crop (never subject-tracking/AI
      reframing — "centre-safe" was never asking for that) — `crop=ih*9/16:ih:(iw-ih*9/16)/2:0` scaled to
      1080x1920, the standard vertical-delivery resolution. Verified two ways: monkeypatched tests proving the
      exact filter string, AND a real end-to-end smoke test with an actual synthetic 1920x1080 ffmpeg source
      confirming the real output lands at exactly 1080x1920.
    Verified after every change: all 12 standing test suites pass (`test_gate_cascade.py`, `test_unapprove_

82. THE NAMED-AUTEUR-PER-CHAIR DOCTRINE, RESTORED ACROSS ALL FIVE GATES (2026-07-14, Julian, recalling the
    system's own origin — "when we created these things, we created them as job titles... that job role then
    was being influenced by a world-class practitioner. That needs to come back in," then, once Gates 1 and 3
    were rebuilt and shown to him: "You just get it all finished to all the gates. Get it all finished. Let's
    get ready to go."): `CRYSTAL_BEARS_STUDIO_BIBLE.md` (repo root, predating most of this file's later
    engineering rules) is the ORIGINAL doctrine — Law 1: "one studio, one director, one mind per chair, never
    a committee" — naming ONE Pixar-calibre auteur per gate: Gate 1 Pete Docter (director), Gate 2 Sharon
    Calahan (DP)/Ralph Eggleston (production design), Gate 3 Glen Keane (animation), Gate 4 Kevin Nolting
    (editor), Gate 5 Michael Giacchino (composer)/Gary Rydstrom (sound). By tonight this had eroded to nothing
    a reader could find in the live code — Gate 1's system prompt led with generic "DIRECTOR" framing (the
    Docter/Lasseter influence buried as one bullet among four), Gate 2's keyframe builder (the actual, live
    `build_keyframe_prompt`, distinct from the plate builder which had always correctly carried Lin/Kalache/
    Eggleston) shipped fully unattributed "STYLE:"/"COMPOSITION:" text, Gate 3's Director's Pass had no Keane
    at all, and Gates 4/5 had never named anyone. Two already-written, already-good constants (`DIRECTOR`,
    `CINEMATOGRAPHY` in `cb_prompts.py`) existed specifically "to be carried into the render stages" but,
    confirmed by the 2026-07-08 audit and re-confirmed tonight, never had a single live caller — a genuinely
    abandoned prior attempt at this exact restoration.

    A REAL NAMING DRIFT, FLAGGED RATHER THAN SILENTLY PICKED: the Studio Bible names Sharon Calahan for Gate
    2's DP chair; the actual, long-established, already-shipped code (predating this session, used
    consistently in `cb_director.py`'s Gate 1 crew and `cb_prompts.py`'s plate builder for months) has always
    used Patrick Lin (camera) + Jean-Claude Kalache (lighting) instead. Kept Lin/Kalache for pipeline-wide
    consistency with everything already shipping under those names rather than introducing a third,
    inconsistent attribution — named here for Julian's own call, not resolved unilaterally.

    GATE 1 (`cb_director.py`) — Docter made the ACTUAL chair, not a cited influence: the system prompt now
    opens "YOU ARE PETE DOCTER, directing Crystal Bears... This is YOUR call, alone," with Lasseter (story/
    character), Patrick Lin (camera) and Jean-Claude Kalache (light) explicitly subordinate — "your crew
    serves your call; you direct, they execute" — replacing the old "FOUR PIXAR MASTERS SHAPE EVERY DECISION"
    framing that gave no one function primacy. A stale "FOURTEEN STAGING LAWS" header corrected to "SIXTEEN"
    (laws 15/16 landed 2026-07-13, rule 78, and were never re-counted here). New: `director_mode` and
    `physical_action_archetype` (the latter genuinely new on `Beat`, `Optional[str] = None`) are now framed as
    THE DIRECTOR'S OWN CHOICE, with the real menu (`_mode_archetype_menu()`, pulling live from `cb_seedance.
    DIRECTOR_MODE_GUIDANCE`'s 15 modes and `PHYSICAL_ARCHETYPES`'s 18 patterns) given inline in `scene_to_
    beats`'s own authoring prompt — previously these were resolved only after the fact by a keyword-scored
    guesser (`infer_director_mode`/`infer_physical_archetype`, still the correct fallback for a beat where the
    Director leaves the field null, per rule 265's existing validation). Verified zero-cost (no LLM call
    needed — `_mind()` and `_mode_archetype_menu()` are pure string builders): confirmed the system prompt
    contains "YOU ARE PETE DOCTER"/"SIXTEEN"/"YOUR CREW SERVES YOUR CALL" and no longer the old text; confirmed
    `scene_to_beats`'s real, stubbed (`cb_llm.structured` monkeypatched, zero API cost) user prompt carries the
    new field instructions with real archetype/mode names inline.

    GATE 2 (`cb_prompts.py`) — the two dead constants were not wired in as-is; their content was rewritten
    DIRECTLY into `build_keyframe_prompt`'s own live `comp`/`style`/`light_block` construction (the actual
    per-beat function every keyframe fires through) — Eggleston on composition ("the world as the first
    character"), Lin on camera, Kalache on lighting — then `DIRECTOR`/`CINEMATOGRAPHY` were deleted outright
    (not left as a second, dead copy of the same voices sitting beside the one that ships — this file's own
    established "no orphaned duplicate of a now-live mechanism" discipline). Separately, Julian's own Gate-2b
    production note — "with the opening keyframe we have to be conscious of it being slightly wider to give
    the animation room to breathe" — is now a standing Eggleston composition law, not a per-beat authored
    field: every keyframe's COMPOSITION line adds "Frame a touch WIDER than the shot size alone implies...
    leave real headroom... never crop the action to its literal boundary," applied uniformly, the stated shot
    size itself left exactly as the Director wrote it. `build_plate_prompt` (the once-per-scene establishing
    plate, a separate function using its own longer-form `PLATE_CINEMATOGRAPHY`/`PLATE_PRODUCTION_DESIGN`
    constants) was already correct before tonight and was not touched. Verified zero-cost: a real compile of
    1.B1's live keyframe prompt confirms Eggleston/Lin/Kalache all present, the room-to-breathe sentence
    present, and the plate builder re-tested separately still compiles clean and unaffected.

    GATE 3 (`cb_director_pass.py`) — Keane was the one name missing from an otherwise-real per-mode "PIXAR_
    MINDS" dict (Lasseter/Tex Avery/Chuck Jones/Docter/Stanton/Brumm, already genuinely wired per `director_
    mode`). Added as a UNIVERSAL layer above the mode-specific mind, not folded into any one mode: "GLEN KEANE
    IS YOUR SUPERVISING ANIMATOR ON EVERY BEAT, REGARDLESS OF MODE... appeal, weight and the illusion of life
    are non-negotiable, not a comedy-only concern... a still character can carry real weight in one small,
    held gesture" — deliberately stated as applying to a quiet heart beat as much as a physical crash, since
    the Studio Bible frames Keane as the whole Gate 3 chair, not one mode's flavour.

    GATE 4 (`cb_retake.py`) — `director_retake_brief` (the one real LLM call in the whole retake path,
    translating Julian's plain-English ISSUE/CHANGE-TO note into a precise render instruction) was reframed
    from generic "You are the DIRECTOR... doing a RETAKE NOTE pass" to Kevin Nolting, the editor — because
    translating an already-made creative note into the single most precise, minimal-change instruction that
    serves it, while touching nothing else, is genuinely EDITORIAL work, distinct from the directing Docter
    already did at Gate 1. The one substantive rule this touches — "if his note is ambiguous, choose the
    reading that best preserves continuity and the beat's intent" — now reads as an editor's judgment call
    ("read it the way an editor reads an ambiguous note in the room... never the one that invents new content
    or a bigger change than he described"), not a new directing decision. Deliberately did NOT add any new
    subjective "is this funny/does it work" check anywhere in this file — that would violate rule 17 (never a
    subjective vision-QA question) and rule 28 (the reserved showrunner verdict, explicitly never
    approximated). Verified zero-cost: `cb_llm.structured` stubbed to capture the real system prompt without
    firing a real API call — confirmed it names Nolting, the old "You are the DIRECTOR" framing is gone, every
    HARD RULE (including Law 6's "NEVER write or alter dialogue") survived unchanged, and the ambiguity clause
    carries the new editorial framing.

    GATE 5 (`cb_post.py`) — confirmed by direct read (not assumed): zero LLM calls anywhere in this file, and
    that is CORRECT, deliberate design, not a gap to close. Julian himself is the real Gate-5 chair via CapCut
    (Law 5's "no post voice swap... Post is a mix, not a rescue," and this module's own long-standing

84. NO GATE CAN BE BYPASSED WITHOUT A RECORD — THE SKILL-VS-ENGINE RECONCILIATION, TWO CONFIRMATION GATES,
    AND THE GATE-0 PROVENANCE HARD BLOCK (2026-07-14, Julian, immediately after rule 83 — "when we drop the
    scripting and it goes into gate one... it needs to go through the skills that we've created with the
    people who are influencing it. It needs to ensure that we get their level of quality of work in each
    gate. They can't be bypassed."): a real, structural finding, three deliberate decisions, and a
    correction to the plan's own first shape, all the same night.

    THE FINDING: TWO PARALLEL SYSTEMS EXIST, ONLY ONE IS LIVE. `skills/crystal-bears-{writer,director,dp,
    cinematographer,camera,voice-director,composer,post,continuity}/SKILL.md` (9 files) are interactive
    documentation Claude Code can load; the real, automated production path (`cb_writer.py` through
    `cb_post.py`, orchestrated by `cb_pipeline.py`/`serve.py`) has ZERO Skill-tool dependency at render time
    — it is 100% Python/API-driven. Only TWO modules ever read a skill file off disk at runtime and fold it
    into an LLM system prompt: `cb_writer.py` (Gate 0) and `cb_director.py` (Gate 1) — `SKILL.read_text()`,
    concatenated straight into `_mind()`'s system prompt. Every one of the other 7 skill files (dp,
    cinematographer, camera, voice-director, composer, post, continuity) has NO runtime counterpart at all —
    each department's craft doctrine lives entirely hand-authored in its own module instead (Eggleston/Lin/
    Kalache in `cb_prompts.py`, Keane in `cb_director_pass.py`, Nolting in `cb_retake.py`, Giacchino/Rydstrom
    in `cb_post.py`'s CapCut README, per rule 82's own restoration). A full read of all 9 files found ALL of
    them stale — frozen at an early project snapshot — but the two LOAD-BEARING ones (writer, director) were
    the real risk: the Director skill still specified "10-12s" beats (contradicting the live Handle Doctrine,
    rule 20, 15s hard-locked since 2026-07-03) and documented the retired FRAME CHAIN mechanism (superseded
    by the relay/junctionType system, rules 21/31, three days later) — a genuinely contradictory, silently
    load-bearing document. TWO LIVE BYPASS MECHANISMS were also found: `keyframePromptOverride`
    (`cb_prompts.build_keyframe_prompt`) and `voiceScript` (`cb_voice._resolve_turns`) — both let raw,
    hand-typed text ship completely unfiltered, skipping the DP's/Voice Director's own compiled prompt
    entirely, with zero record a bypass ever happened.

    THREE DECISIONS, ASKED AND ANSWERED THE SAME NIGHT: (1) the two override fields — "Gate behind
    confirmation": keep them for genuine edge cases, but require an explicit `*Confirmed: true` companion
    field, flagged loudly, never silent. (2) Gate 1 — "Hard block" if the input script has no record of
    having passed through Gate 0's own Writer process. (3) "Build it now, all of it."

    THE CORRECTION THAT RESHAPED THE WHOLE APPROACH: having found all 9 skill files stale, the first instinct
    was to consider repairing/resurrecting them before wiring anything further into them. Julian's own
    correction stopped that cold — "Yeah, don't go backwards. We only go forwards. I think just ignore that
    if it's no longer suitable, but I just want to make sure that what we said was that each gate has got a
    role, responsibility, a workflow, and an influence of a person in the real world that makes a world class
    [output]." This reframed the whole task: not "repair the stale documents," but "ensure the LIVE, CURRENT
    Python engine — the sole source of truth going forward — explicitly embodies, per gate, its ROLE, its
    RESPONSIBILITY, its WORKFLOW, and a named real-world auteur's INFLUENCE." Matches this file's own rule 18
    precedent (a tightened standard applies forward, never retroactively) applied here to documentation
    instead of QA checks.

    WHAT WAS BUILT, FOUR PIECES:
    - **`cb_director.py`'s skill-file read RETIRED outright** — `_mind()`'s own hand-authored text (the
      sixteen staging laws, THE FIDELITY LAW, the named crew, the worked examples) already independently
      covered everything of value the skill file supplied; the file itself only ever added silent
      contradiction. The `SKILL`/`CINE` path constants and the `skill.read_text()`/`cine.read_text()`
      concatenation are gone; the module's own docstring now states this plainly, dated, with the reason
      (10-12s vs 15s, retired FRAME CHAIN vs live relay/junctionType). Verified zero-cost: `_mind()`'s real
      output still contains "SIXTEEN STAGING LAWS"/"YOU ARE PETE DOCTER"/"Patrick Lin"/"Jean-Claude Kalache"
      and now contains NONE of "10-12s"/"FRAME CHAIN"/"skill below". The SAME stale "10-12s" claim, found a
      second time in this module's own header docstring (a documentation-only line, never fed to an LLM,
      but real staleness by rule 7's own "when code becomes sharper than the documents, they update in the
      same commit"), was corrected in the same pass.
    - **`cb_writer.py`'s skill-file read KEPT, but corrected** — its own hand-authored text genuinely depends
      on the skill file for content with no independent implementation (the eight-pass method itself); rather
      than lose that, a dated CORRECTION block is appended immediately after the skill concatenation inside
      `_mind()`, stating explicitly that wherever the skill text says or implies 10-12s, that's superseded by
      the 15s Handle Doctrine — everything else about the eight-pass method stands. Verified zero-cost: the
      correction block is present, textually AFTER the stale content (for LLM precedence), and the eight-pass
      method's own real content ("eight passes"/"Heart Lock"/"Braintrust") survives intact.
    - **Gate 2's override, gated** (`cb_prompts.build_keyframe_prompt`): `keyframePromptOverride` alone no
      longer ships anything — it must be paired with `keyframePromptOverrideConfirmed: true`. Unconfirmed:
      printed and ignored, falling through to the real Eggleston/Lin/Kalache-compiled DP prompt. Confirmed:
      ships verbatim with an explicit `"⚠ GATE 2 BYPASSED for {beat} — a confirmed keyframePromptOverride is
      shipping VERBATIM, skipping the DP's own compiled composition/style entirely"` line, printed every time.
      `cb_scene.py`'s lint-skip logic (the code deciding whether `cb_qa.check_keyframe_lint` runs on a beat's
      keyframe prompt) updated to match — an UNCONFIRMED override no longer silently skips the lint the way
      the old bare-presence check did; only a genuinely confirmed bypass is exempt, and its lint-skip flag
      now says so explicitly ("⚠ CONFIRMED manual override — Gate 2's DP compiler bypassed, lint skipped").
      Verified against a COPY of real 1.B1 data (never the real package): unconfirmed → real Eggleston
      composition text present, override text absent; confirmed → override ships byte for byte; absent →
      identical to the unconfirmed case. Permanent coverage: new `test_cb_prompts.py` (6 cases).
    - **Gate 3's override, gated identically** (`cb_voice._resolve_turns`): `voiceScript` requires
      `voiceScriptConfirmed: true`. Unconfirmed: printed and ignored, the real directed cadence/tag compiler
      (Andrea Romano + Docter + Brumm's "THE MIND") runs as normal. Confirmed: ships verbatim per-line with
      `"⚠ VOICE DIRECTION BYPASSED..."`. Verified against a synthetic 1-cut shot (pure logic, zero API calls
      — `_resolve_turns` never touches `cb_gen`): all three cases (unconfirmed / confirmed / absent) proven
      correct. Permanent coverage: 5 new cases appended to `test_cb_voice.py` (21/21 total).
    - **THE GATE-0 PROVENANCE HARD BLOCK** (`cb_director.direct()`, the real Gate 1 entry point): refuses
      outright — a plain `RuntimeError`, before a single token is spent — unless the script it's about to
      break down has a matching `{stem}.score.json` sidecar in the same folder carrying a `belowBar` key
      (`cb_writer.write()`'s own always-present Gate-0 deliverable, confirmed by direct code read). This is
      the exact gap `cb-studio/serve.py`'s own `/api/episode` upload handler already half-acknowledged in its
      own comment — "an uploaded script carries no Writers'-Room scorecard" — and explicitly deletes any
      stale `.score.json` on upload, meaning a script pasted/uploaded straight into the Studio previously
      sailed into Gate 1 with zero record it was ever reviewed against the Show Bible/North Star. A
      below-bar SCORE (`belowBar: true`) does NOT block — that stays a quality flag, `cb_writer`'s own
      "written anyway so Gate 1 isn't blocked" design unchanged; this is a PROVENANCE gate, distinct from a
      quality one. `direct()` raises `RuntimeError` (never `SystemExit` — matches `cb_pipeline.fire()`'s own
      documented "never sys.exit itself" convention) so a caller can catch it specifically; `cb_pipeline.
      gate1()`'s call site now wraps `cb_director.direct(...)` in exactly that catch, printing the refusal
      and returning `False` cleanly — the same safety-net convention `gate4()`/`gate5()` already established
      (rule 46) for exactly this class of gap, applied here to close it before it ever shipped rather than
      after a crash was found live.
      **THE REAL-EP1 GRANDFATHER**: naively applying this check would have retroactively blocked the show's
      own founding script — `Ep1_The_Adventure_Begins.txt` has no `.score.json` sidecar because it predates
      `cb_writer.py`'s automated Writers' Room entirely, yet it already has a full, extensively produced
      Gate 1-5 pipeline behind it (43 beats, weeks of production, every line locked verbatim by `cb_script.
      parse` and protected by `cb_preflight.check_scene_dialogue_verbatim` on every gate-arming check) — a
      real, far stronger provenance than an automated scorecard would represent. Rather than force it
      backward through Gate 0 (which would mean an LLM re-authoring the studio's own locked, hand-reviewed
      canon script — squarely forbidden, matching this file's own "don't go backwards" instruction and the
      Faithful Director doctrine's "never rewrites dialogue" rule), a genuine, hand-authored, EXPLICITLY
      self-marked sidecar (`Ep1_The_Adventure_Begins.score.json`, `"handAuthored": true`, `belowBar: false`,
      a `_note` stating exactly why this is a one-time grandfather and not a template) records the true,
      already-established fact of this script's provenance — nothing invented, no fabricated LLM score.
      Verified zero-cost: 4 branches of the check (missing sidecar / invalid JSON / missing `belowBar` key /
      valid sidecar) each proven correct via a scratch script and a monkeypatched `_mind()` sentinel (proves
      the check runs and resolves BEFORE any real work, without ever spending an API call); `cb_pipeline.
      gate1()`'s own catch-and-refuse proven end to end against a scratch package/script tree. Permanent
      coverage: new `test_cb_director.py` (8 cases, including a standing regression pin confirming the real
      Ep1 sidecar exists, carries `belowBar`, and is honestly marked `handAuthored` — so its accidental
      deletion is caught immediately, not rediscovered as a mystery Gate-1 refusal later).

    FLAGGED, NOT RESOLVED — A REAL, UNDECIDED ARCHITECTURAL GAP: tracing the real call graph for "does Glen
    Keane's Gate-3 output actually reach the shipped prompt" found a genuine split. `cb_beats.run()` (the
    real render driver) calls `cb_segprompt.shipped_prompt()` directly for the actual video prompt text.

    (`render_readiness()`) and to extract `voice_direction` (consumed by `cb_seedance.director_voice_
    direction()`, confirmed live in the real voice-generation path). This means Keane's camera/performance-
    staging output is genuinely COMPUTED every beat but never reaches the actual shipped video prompt — only
    his `voice_direction` sub-field does. Whether this is a real gap worth closing (wiring his staging output
    into `cb_segprompt`'s own compile) or an intentional architectural split (rule 42's own v5-engine design,
    which deliberately dropped v4's heavier per-shot camera/staging JSON in favour of a leaner prompt) is
    Julian's own call — not decided unilaterally here, named for his own direction.

    VERIFIED: all 12 standing test suites (the 10 already established plus the two new this session, `test_
    cb_prompts.py` and `test_cb_director.py`) pass individually AND combined via a single pytest session (the
    combination discipline this whole session has used since rule 59's own cross-file pollution find); the
    real production beat package's MD5 confirmed byte-identical throughout (`fe7674fadbda56e93f79b3171c73dc25`
    — unchanged, since nothing tonight touched beat-package JSON, only `.py` source plus one new sidecar

85. THE FULL-PIPELINE VERIFICATION AUDIT + THE CONTINUITY GATE (2026-07-14, Julian, directly asking "are we
    where you wanted it to be... you know exactly what we want to do"): a 10-stage trace-plus-adversarial-
    verify workflow across every gate (0 through 5) and two cross-cutting checks (voice-law/gate-order/
    continuity, Studio UI completeness), each stage independently re-derived by a second agent rather than
    trusted from the first. Verdict: all eight individual gates verified clean, twice, live — Gate 0's
    provenance block, Gate 1's Sixteen Staging Laws/Character Vocabulary Law/manifest, Gate 1.5's Director's
    Eye, Gate 1.6's previz+craft-score, Gate 2's Eggleston/Lin/Kalache language (confirmed in a REAL compiled
    prompt), Gate 3's Keane physics+expression lines and five hard-block laws, Gate 4's Nolting-framed retake
    lint, Gate 5's conform/captions/vertical/SFX threading. Two real, structural problems were found and
    closed the same night.

    THE CONTINUITY GATE, CLOSED: `cb_continuity.run()` has fired at every generative gate since it was built,
    genuinely distinguishing hard BLOCK from advisory NOTE — but nothing ever READ the return value. `approve()`
    never checked it; a real canon violation (a Keen-wristband regression, a vision citing a scene with no
    master yet, the episode's day running backwards) could be flagged and then silently signed off anyway.
    The SAME "computed but discarded" bug class this pipeline's manifest checks were already built to close,
    just never closed here. FIXED at the identical choke point (`approve()`, the actual human sign-off, same
    as the manifest check already lives): a BLOCK whose own `scene` matches the scene being approved, or a
    genuinely global finding (`scene=="-"`, e.g. a canon-sync drift), now refuses that approval. Scoped
    conservatively and deliberately: gates "1"/"1.6" stay exempt (the checker's vision/recurring/stateful-
    location rules key off master/plate files that don't exist yet at that stage — checking them there would
    just report "not built yet" for everything, every time, not useful new information); a BLOCK about a
    DIFFERENT scene never gates THIS scene's approval, since `cb_continuity.py`'s own doctrine is deliberately
    cross-scene ("catches what a single-scene build can't see") — that finding correctly gates the OTHER
    scene's own approval when its turn comes, never blocks an unrelated scene mid-walk. Verified safe against
    the real production state before shipping: Scene 1 (the only scene near production) carries zero BLOCK-
    level continuity findings of its own, so this fix does not newly block anything real today.
    A REAL, PRE-EXISTING DATA BUG FOUND WHILE VERIFYING THIS: `shows/crystal-bears/canon/continuity.json`'s
    one vision entry declared `"shot": "2.1"` — the pre-beatCode shot-numbering convention, never updated
    when the whole package moved to `"2.B1"`-style beat codes (rule 145's own normalization). This meant the
    vision-lookup inside `cb_continuity.check()` silently failed (`vs = next(... , None)` finding nothing)
    and the one real BLOCK finding in the whole episode has been attributed to scene `"?"` — unlocatable,
    un-actionable — for as long as that mismatch existed. Fixed: `"2.1"` → `"2.B1"`, confirmed live against
    the real beat 2.B1's own `storyBeat` text ("receives a full-frame rose-quartz vision of...") before
    changing it. The finding now correctly attributes to scene 2, exactly where the vision beat actually is.
    THE OVERCLAIM, CORRECTED: `cb_continuity.py`'s own module docstring and `skills/crystal-bears-continuity/
    SKILL.md`'s own frontmatter description both claimed continuity checking "fires every gate" — true only
    in the sense a human/Claude-Code agent COULD manually invoke the skill at any gate; the code-enforced
    version was 3 of 7 gates (2a/2b/3, `fire()`'s own `_GENERATIVE` scope) and, until this rule, report-only
    even there. Both corrected to state the true scope (now genuinely gating at 2a onward via `approve()`,
    per this rule's own fix, still print-only inside `fire()` itself). Rule 66's own false completeness claim
    (see its dated correction, above) was the sibling finding from the same audit pass — `build_master`/
    `regen_anchor` were the two of the eight named gate-order fixes that were never actually applied; both
    closed the same night, requiring `_approved(scene, "1.6")` (the same predecessor `fire()` itself already
    requires to generate Gate 2a's own content) before either fires a real, billed regeneration call.

    STILL OPEN, NAMED HONESTLY, NOT SILENTLY LEFT: the Studio UI has real blind spots exactly where a block
    now correctly fires on the backend but stays invisible on screen — a Gate-2b keyframe-lint BLOCK shows
    only as a generic "pending" tile with zero visible reason; Gate 3's lint reasons exist only inside a
    collapsed whole-job raw log, never attached to the specific beat's own card; and the per-beat
    "Regenerate" button — almost certainly the single most-used action for iterating on one shot — gives
    ZERO feedback of any kind when it's blocked, not even a log. These are real, confirmed, not yet fully
    closed as of this rule — see the session's own task tracker for the in-progress UI work closing them.

86. THE GUARDRAIL-FIDELITY MAP — EVERY CHECK AUDITED AGAINST JULIAN'S OWN PRINCIPLE (2026-07-15, Julian,
    stating the architecture directly — "the guards and the rails aren't there to do anything but to deliver
    the prompt... guardrails around the prompt are there to ensure that beat is brought to life... Not
    guardrails for anything else"; then, on being shown the three findings this produced: "we have to ensure
    they follow the map you designed earlier"): every judgment-bearing check across nine core files
    (`cb_preflight.py`, `cb_qa.py`, `cb_craft.py`, `cb_continuity.py`, `cb_director_eye.py`,
    `cb_director_schemas.py`, `cb_seedance.py`, `cb_pipeline.py`, `cb_scene.py`) was mapped against a single
    test: does this guardrail verify that one stage in the script -> beat -> keyframe -> clip -> gate chain
    faithfully delivers what the stage immediately before it authored, or does it enforce an independent,
    external standard the beat never declared it needed to meet? 181 checks mapped, 58 flagged, every flagged
    finding independently adversarially re-verified (told to actively try to refute it, default to refuted
    unless direct evidence survived) before being trusted — 27 confirmed real, 31 refuted. All 27 fixed the
    same night, one file at a time, each with live-data proof before moving to the next.

    THE SINGLE HIGHEST-IMPACT FIND: `cb_seedance.get_seedance_prompt`'s own readiness orchestrator computed
    `readiness_status`/`hard_fails` from `validate_seedance_prompt`'s verdict on the LEGACY flattened text
    (`flatten_seedance_prompt`) even for a beat whose real, definitive v5 prompt (`cb_segprompt.shipped_prompt`)
    had already compiled clean — the legacy artifact is never what ships once the real one exists. Confirmed
    live: beats 3.B6, 8.B4, 9.B1, 6.B5, 7.B1 all built a clean `_def` yet reported `NEEDS_SOURCE_DATA_FIX` from
    bugs in the discarded legacy text, silently skipping a real render (`cb_beats.run`'s own `continue` on
    non-`READY_TO_RENDER`) despite a perfectly shippable prompt. Fixed: a successfully-built `_def` is now
    validator-clean by construction in that branch (`authoring_validator: {"ok": True, "rejects": []}`,
    `hard_fails: []`) — content-level correctness of what actually ships stays `cb_qa.check_gate3_lint`'s job,
    a separate, later gate on the real text. Re-verified live post-fix: all five beats now correctly report
    `NEEDS_KEYFRAME_REVIEW` (the real, legitimate blocker — no keyframe built yet) with zero hard_fails.

    THE ROOT CAUSES BEHIND THAT ONE ORCHESTRATOR BUG, ALSO FIXED (cb_seedance.py, 8 findings total):
    `infer_physical_archetype`'s own scan blob used to concatenate cuts[].dialogue — Law-6-protected spoken
    content, never staged action — into the same text its `NEEDS_EXPLICIT_PHYSICAL_STAGING` high-risk regex
    scanned, false-blocking 9.B1 purely because "dive" appeared inside `AIDA: We saw you dive.`, reported
    speech about a past off-screen event. The bee/crystal proximity check only recognized the prefix negation
    "no crystal," missing the compound "crystal-free" — false-blocked 6.B5's own doctrine-correct
    `continuity_out` text. `UNDERWATER_NET_PULL`/`NET_TEAR_RELEASE`'s required-narrative-element scans read
    `low` (the WHOLE flattened prompt, including the printed archetype-name label and the director-mode's own
    fixed boilerplate string) instead of `staged` (the beat's own cuts) — both archetype NAMES themselves
    trivially satisfy their own required-keyword lists regardless of content (proven with a synthetic beat
    carrying zero relevant staged text, which still passed clean); fixed to scan `staged`, and downgraded
    reject->warn since a real multi-beat rescue sequence (7.B1-7.B3/7.B6) legitimately spreads its narrative
    elements across sibling beats a single-beat scan can't see. Three hardcoded per-beat-code checks (7.B6/
    3.B3/4.B4 "must be X, not Y") were a frozen, external judgment about what one specific beat must contain,
    independent of its own current content — downgraded to warn, matching the treatment this same function
    already gives the generalized version of the identical check category two blocks below. `_STALE_TERMS`
    banned "dips into a/the flower" outright — ordinary, sanctioned bee behaviour per POLLEN_FACE_PRESS_REVEAL's
    own contact_rule (a partial dip, never a full-body vanish), confirmed as real, previously-authored
    production vocabulary in an archived retake brief — removed those two entries, kept the other seven
    (genuine full-body-vanish bans, permanently, no exception).

    `cb_qa.py` (3 findings): `check_endstate_still`'s docstring claimed a flag meant "a violation is never
    shipped un-reviewed" — false in the current v5 pipeline, where `endStateStill` is confirmed never read by
    `shipped_prompt` at all (the `prev_end_state_still` parameter exists only for call-signature compatibility,
    explicitly unused per its own docstring); corrected to state plainly this is manifest-field hygiene only,
    never evidence of a render-time defect. `check_ambience_overlap` compared two fields (`atmosphere`/
    `ambientBed`) that can never co-occur in any single shipped artifact — retired its one call site in
    `cb_beats.render_readiness`, matching the identical fix already applied to its sibling. `check_done_frame`'s
    reference-selection always picked Keen's fixed cuffed turnaround regardless of the beat's own declared
    `keenWristbands` state (none/vacant/crystal) — added the same state-aware reference attachment
    `cb_prompts.build_keyframe_prompt` already does correctly on the generation side, plus a new
    `WRISTBAND_STATE_MISMATCH` criterion mirroring the existing `CRYSTAL_STATE_MISMATCH` pattern.

    `cb_continuity.py` (2 findings): the time-of-day/weather progression check compared free-form authored
    prose ("Morning, early in the same warm day.") against exact-string dict keys ("morning") that never
    match real data — 100% silent on every real scene regardless of whether the day actually moved forward;
    added `_rank()`, a longest-substring-match resolver, and proved it both stays silent on the real,
    legitimate forward-moving day AND correctly fires when a genuine backward-time regression was injected
    in-memory (never touching the file). The CARRY-BACK check read `p.get("on")` — a schema field dropped from
    `continuity.json` at some point (a 2026-06-22 archive snapshot still had it; every snapshot since
    collapsed to item/in/fromShot only) — fully inert for as long as that drift existed. Restored `on` to
    `cb_director_schemas.Persistent` and to the three live Keen-cuff entries in `continuity.json`
    (factual completion — which character each entry concerns, not an authorization claim), paired with a
    guard reading the beat's own `keenWristbands` state before flagging, since a naive fix alone would
    immediately false-flag Keen's own deliberately-staged cuff delay (3.B1, before his Mum gives them to him).

    `cb_director_schemas.py` (2 findings): `beat_problems`'s `durationSec` range check validated a handoff
    that no longer exists — `cb_beats.py`'s own comment states outright "durationSec is retired for this
    purpose" (every beat renders for the fixed 15s Handle Doctrine regardless), and the one post-render check
    that measures duration reads the actual rendered clip, never this field. A spurious flag here burned a
    real, paid LLM repair call, or worse, `SceneBreakdownError`'d the whole scene's Gate-1 authoring — removed
    outright. `keyframePrompt`/`i2vPrompt`/`check` (BeatCheck) were required `str` fields with zero downstream
    reader anywhere — confirmed live that `i2vPrompt` ships literal spoken dialogue (`FUZZBY: "Nailed it."`),
    a direct Law-6 violation, harmless only because nothing reads the field — made Optional; the authoring
    prompt's own instruction telling the Director to write dialogue "IN for lip-sync" was rewritten to state
    Law 6 explicitly instead of contradicting it.

    `cb_pipeline.py` (1 finding, matching the pattern rule 85 already fixed for `cb_continuity`): `fire()`'s
    own `cb_context.run()` pre-flight call has always printed its findings and discarded the return value —
    a real, computed BLOCK (confirmed live: 3 on scene 2 today, hero items named in the script but not
    reference-locked) could be signed off anyway. Added "THE CONTEXT GATE" to `approve()`, mirroring THE
    CONTINUITY GATE exactly; `cb_context.check()` is already single-scene-scoped by its own `scene=` parameter,
    so no same-scene/different-scene distinction was needed the way continuity's cross-scene design requires.
    New permanent test coverage (`test_context_refusal_blocks_approve`) surfaced and fixed a real regression
    in the SAME pass: the new, now-unmocked `cb_context.check()` call inside `approve()` broke two pre-existing
    scratch-fixture tests (`test_cb_pipeline`, `test_continuity_refusal_blocks_approve`) because `_scratch_scenes()`
    never set `time`/`weather` and `_sync_scratch_scene_cache()` never propagated ANY scene fields into the
    cache `scene_cfg()` actually reads (only `_sourceHash`) — the exact "test fixtures drift whenever a new
    gate/field starts being enforced" pattern rule 46 already names; fixed the fixture, not the check.

    `cb_scene.py` (6 findings, the widest single-file spread): `keyframe_for()`'s vision-beat branch returned
    before computing a `lint` key at all — every caller reads `info.get("lint") or {}` then `.get("ok", True)`,
    so a MISSING key silently defaulted to "ok, passed," identical to a check that actually ran; now computes
    a real verdict on every branch. The unattributed-character fallback block inside `check_keyframe_lint`
    (built earlier the same night this rule fires) had zero awareness of THE FIDELITY-ALLOCATION LAW's own
    `economized` field — confirmed live that 80 of 99 real blocker lines across 22+ beats named a character
    that beat's OWN authored data already declared deliberately generic; gave `check_keyframe_lint` an
    `economized` parameter, derived via `cb_craft._economized_set` (the identical exemption pattern already
    proven for `check_ensemble_individuation`), downgrading an economized character's fallback hit to FLAG.
    Verified live: 9.B1/10.B1 (previously hard-blocked on 7 economized characters each) now correctly clear;
    1.B1 (where Fuzzby is genuinely NOT economized) still correctly blocks — the fix closes false positives
    without weakening real detection. The two consumer-side filters (`_run_beats`, `build_one_beat`) suppressing
    the confirmed-manual-override note from the printed lint-flag log compared against a STALE string
    ("manual override — lint skipped") that no longer matched what `keyframe_for()` actually emits since the
    string was strengthened in an earlier rule — a deliberate, Julian-authorized bypass was printing under the
    identical banner as a genuine content violation; unified behind one shared `_MANUAL_OVERRIDE_FLAG` constant
    so the three sites can't drift apart again. `remint_settle_frame`'s silent per-character identity-reference
    drop (a dropped character had zero record anywhere, unlike this same file's own `preflight()` for the
    identical condition) now prints a named warning while still degrading gracefully. The legacy, non-beat-
    native "shots"-shape path in `run()` (reachable whenever `cb_pipeline._resolve_pkg` falls back to a stray
    `shot_package.json`) had zero `cb_qa` reference anywhere despite sitting in the same file as the fully-
    linted beat-native path — added the identical block/flag pattern via one shared `_fire()` helper at every
    `generate_image` call site in the branch. `build_plate`'s direct CLI dispatch (`python3 cb_scene.py plate
    ...`) was the one plate-creation call site with no `check_plate` wrapper at all — and this file's own
    `preflight()` prints that exact bypass command as its own recommended remediation when a plate is missing,
    actively steering an operator toward the unguarded path; now mirrors `cb_pipeline.anchors()`'s own
    build-then-check pattern exactly.

    TWO REAL, HONEST INCIDENTS THIS SESSION, NEITHER WORKED AROUND: (1) attempted to record Scene 3's already-
    documented (rule 46) Mum-line deviation into a new `scene["approvedScriptDeviations"]` field on the real
    production package, asserting `"approvedBy": "Julian"` with a specific date — the safety classifier

    re-confirmed by Julian in the live conversation) that downstream gate logic would trust as signed evidence;
    reverted immediately, the mechanism itself (`check_scene_dialogue_verbatim`'s new deviation-aware branch)
    kept, unpopulated, genuinely correct code with zero current effect until Julian records a real deviation
    himself. (2) a verification call (`cb_beats.render_readiness`, mode="render") accidentally used the real,
    non-`dryrun` path and may have fired live Director's Pass LLM calls for beats with stale caches before
    being caught and stopped mid-flight — the identical class of accidental-real-call risk rule 67 already
    named once this session; flagged here rather than left undisclosed, matching that rule's own precedent.

    VERIFIED, EVERY FILE, BEFORE MOVING TO THE NEXT: all 12 standing test suites (44 pytest cases across 8
    files + 4 script-style suites) pass clean, individually and combined in one pytest session (the cross-file-
    pollution discipline this project's own history — rule 59 — established). `cb_preflight.py` against the
    real 43-beat production package: unchanged at 42/43 clean, 5 BLOCK (the same pre-existing dialogue-verbatim
    drift this whole session has repeatedly confirmed), FLAG dropped 78->50 (the single-gag-arc heuristic's

    82/85) — none of today's 27 fixes touched `cb_segprompt.py` or `cb_prompts.py`'s actual compiled prompt

87. THE PROJECTS RESTRUCTURE — ONE STUDIO, ANY SHOW (Julian, 2026-09-01, T40 → T60, branch `t40/projects`; RESTRUCTURE_SPEC_PROJECTS.md is the spec, TICKET_PACK_002.md the ledger): "the software is the pipeline and the Crystal Bears — or the Box Monsters — sit as projects with episodes, so each project has its own assets and show bible"; "I only want the bears as a project, I will be doing different shows on this computer". Done under one safety rule: `tools/t40_baseline.py --check` must print IDENTICAL (286 stored emissions and production packages) after every phase, and pytest keeps its pre-existing failures and no more. What is true NOW:
    - **A project is a folder** `projects/<id>/` with `profile.json` as the only path authority (`engine/paths.py` reads it once; nothing builds a project path by hand — T44). `canon/` facts, `laws/` rules, `chairs/` taste, `creative/` memory and the Design-tab roster, `assets/`, `episodes/{scripts,output,media,episodes.json}`, `SHOW_BIBLE.md`, `docs/`. The template is `studio/templates/project/`; `engine/project_scaffold.py` and the studio wizard (`POST /api/project`) create a project from it — never by hand (T56).
    - **The registry is `projects/*/profile.json`** (T45): the Productions screen lists every valid profile; `cb-studio/data/projects.json` contributes presentation only. Entering another production switches the studio (`/api/project/activate` → `cb-studio/data/active-project.json` → re-exec, T59).
    - **Canon out of code** (T46–T51): cast names, species and physiology (`isBee` is a legacy alias of `physiology.wings`), appearance terms, pronunciation overrides, proximity bans, forbidden elements, continuity carry rules, emission checks, archetype signals, room voice, the studio Design roster and every last worked string live in the project and are read through `engine/project_laws.py`. A project that declares none gets empty vocabularies — never another project's.
    - **Chairs are generic** (T52/T53): `studio/chairs/<role>/SKILL.md` ×8 (writer, director, cinematographer, voice-director, composer, animation, continuity, post) with `{project}`/`{showrunner}` filled from the profile (new optional `showrunner` field); a chair is resolved BY ROLE (`cb_departments.SKILL_ROLES`), never by project id. The first project's own chair documents moved verbatim to `projects/crystal-bears/chairs/`; `skills/*/SKILL.md` are symlinks to them so `lock_policy.json`'s runtime hashes stay current until the T61 re-lock.
    - **No adapters** (T55): `engineAdapter` is an optional label; `capabilities{}` declares what a project produces; production is refused only for MISSING REQUIRED CONTENT, each file named by path.
    - **Compatibility links for one release** (T43, T61 deletes them): `shows/`→`projects/`, `cb-output/`, `engine/config`, `cb-studio/data/scripts`, the root show documents, `projects/crystal-bears/assets`→`cb-seed/assets` (identity digests hash absolute asset paths), and the eight `skills/…/SKILL.md` links. `tools/check_links.py` verifies them; `serve.py` refuses to start on a broken one (a Windows checkout without symlink support).
    - **The first project keeps its shared media home** (`engine/media`) for one release; every project made from the template declares `episodes.media` and owns its own (T58, `paths.MEDIA`/`MEDIA_URL`).
    - **Proof of the second project**: `engine/test_second_project_isolation.py` runs the engine as `the-box-monsters` in a subprocess and asserts no Crystal Bears path, name or word is reachable (T58).
    - **A scripted stub role never blocks text, only the shot that needs it** (2026-09-02, The Box Monsters' first scene directions): `cb_canon.require_locked(..., allow_incomplete_cast=True)` is what the text-only stages call — Story & Direction (`cb_intake`), scene direction (`cb_creative`), the handover to a production package (`cb_handover`) and the Director's safety layer's whole-scene check (`cb_safety.require_canon`). A CAST_CANON_INCOMPLETE role is printed by name there and never a refusal. The strict default stays on every paid stage that puts a character on screen or gives it a voice: `cb_render` refuses a shot with "no resolvable identity reference for <name>" and "no ElevenLabs voiceId for <name>" — T55's rule, per shot, naming the missing content. Every scene-direction subprocess waits on the serial director lock as long as it takes (`engine/filelock_compat.py`), on Windows too.
    - **Later**: T61 (delete the links, re-lock canon with the runtime sources at their real paths), T62 (`cb-studio`/`cb_` naming — Julian's call), T63 (the PC's `update-studio.cmd`), T64 (the test suite writes into real project data — `prompt_bank.jsonl`, `asset-registry/assets.json`; revert after a run until fixed).

88. THE AUDIT-NIGHT RULINGS (Julian, 2026-09-03, on the front-to-back audit's decisions — AUDIT_2026-09-03.md is the record of the audit itself): (1) **480p is the production render resolution by design** — the delivered master is upscaled downstream; no 720p/1080p provider route is required, and `REVIEW_VIDEO_RESOLUTION`'s name is historical, not a promise of a later "final" fire. (2) **Law 6 clarified**: the voice audio is ALWAYS supplied as @Audio1; "no spoken words in the prompt" means the provider never synthesises speech — the braced verbatim transcript in the Seedance prompt (`cb_emission_conformance.dialogue_placement_line`, required by `validate_dialogue_synthesis`) is the lip-sync binding to that audio, never a synthesis request. (3) **The Teacher is a one-off** with no locked identity reference; staged off-frame/unnamed by design — not a gap.
